# CODE NOTES — Core

Generated by `tools/FvsComments --apply` (NOCOMMENTS_02, 2026-11-15): the text of every comment removed from `.cs` files, word for word, under its file and the member it sat on. Grep it for a file, symbol or tag; never read it whole. A note is history and rationale, not a rule: where it differs from the owning spec (`INDEX.md` §1), the spec wins.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Abstractions/Fault.cs

### FaultTier — FAULTTIER_01 (line 4)

FAULTTIER_01 — HOW LOUD A FAILURE IS ALLOWED TO BE.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
WHY THIS EXISTS. An audit of `src/` counted **917 catch blocks in ~90,000 lines** — one
per 98 lines — of which 368 were bare `catch (Exception)` and 391 sites carried a comment
saying "best-effort" or "ignore". The near-universal shape was:

```csharp

    try { ... } catch (Exception ex) { RuntimeLog.Fail("AREA", ex); }

```

That is not error handling. It is error *absorption*: a log line the user will never open,
followed by the program continuing as though nothing happened. A failed thumbnail, a failed
ffprobe and a failed settings write all present identically to the person using the app —
as **nothing happening**. The user is left to guess whether they mis-clicked.

Silence is the one response that must not be available. Every catch block now answers one
question — *what does the person in front of this window need to know?* — and the three
possible answers are the three members of this enum.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### FaultTier.Recoverable (line 28)

The operation failed and the program genuinely recovered on its own — a retry succeeded, a
cache miss fell through to the source, an optional probe returned nothing and a documented
default took over. **Nothing reaches the screen.** Goes to the DEBUG log only.

⚠️ The bar is "the user's outcome is unchanged", not "we kept running". If the feature
they asked for did not happen, it is `Degraded`, however gracefully the code
coped.

### FaultTier.Degraded (line 39)

Something the user can perceive stopped working, but the session is intact and the rest of
the app still functions. Surfaces as a `FloatingNotice` that says **what stopped**
and **what still works**, and is written to the log at failure level.

This is the tier that almost every current silent catch belongs in. "Thumbnails could
not be generated for this clip — editing and export still work" is a complete notice; it
tells the user the blank strip is known, is not their fault, and is not going to cost them
the export.

### FaultTier.Fatal (line 51)

The operation the user explicitly asked for cannot proceed, or continuing risks their work.
Surfaces as a modal dialog naming the failure, the log path and a copy button, and is
written to the log at failure level.

Export refused to start, the project could not be saved, the update installer failed
its signature check. A dialog is justified precisely because the user is about to act on a
belief that is now false.

### Fault — FAULTTIER_01 (line 63)

FAULTTIER_01 — one failure, classified, with the words the user will actually read.

**Tier:** How loud this is allowed to be. See `FaultTier`.
**Area:**
The short uppercase subsystem tag already used by `RuntimeLog` ("EXPORT", "IPC", "THUMBS").
Kept identical so a fault and its surrounding log lines can be grepped together.

**UserMessage:**
Plain language, written for the person in front of the window, obeying the colloquial tooltip
standard of `04_UI_UX_AVALONIA_SPEC.md` §4 (UI-SAFEGUARDS). For `FaultTier.Degraded`
it must name what stopped AND what still works. Never an exception message verbatim — an
`ObjectDisposedException` string is not an explanation. Ignored for
`FaultTier.Recoverable`, which never reaches a screen.

**TechnicalDetail:**
What goes in the log beside the stack trace: the file, the exit code, the filtergraph stage.
May contain full paths — the log pipeline's own privacy gate (`05_SYSTEM_LIFECYCLE_STORAGE.md`
§2, SYS-LOGGING) decides whether they survive to a production INFO line.

**Exception:** The original exception, when there was one. Null for a policy refusal.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Abstractions/Faults.cs

### Faults — FAULTTIER_02, COMPOSITION_02, FAULTTIER_01 (line 7)

FAULTTIER_02 — THE AMBIENT SINK. WHERE A CATCH BLOCK REPORTS WHEN IT CANNOT BE HANDED ONE.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
**WHY THIS EXISTS, AND WHY IT IS NOT A BACKDOOR AROUND COMPOSITION_02.**

`FAULTTIER_01` gave the codebase the right vocabulary and the right destination, and then
the migration did not happen: an audit of `src/` finds ~900 catch blocks and **three**
that reach `IFaultSink`. That is not negligence. It is arithmetic. Reporting a
fault requires an `IFaultSink` instance; the code that catches exceptions is
overwhelmingly static helpers and window code-behind that Avalonia constructs, neither of which
has a constructor anyone can pass one to. So the conversion needed a plumbing change per call
site, and a 900-site plumbing change does not get done — which is exactly the outcome the
FAULTTIER_01 comment predicted when it said the right thing must be shorter to type than the
wrong thing.

⚠️ **THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES THIS LEGITIMATE.** `COMPOSITION_02` retires the service
locator for COLLABORATORS — things a class does its work with, which a test must substitute to
exercise that work. A fault sink is not a collaborator, it is a DIAGNOSTIC CHANNEL, in the same
category as `RuntimeLog` and `CoreLogger`, both of which are already static by
deliberate decision and are not on anyone's list to inject. A failure must be reportable from
anywhere, including from a static utility, a finaliser and a thread with no object graph in
scope. Making that channel reachable is what allows the silence to end.

**NEW CODE STILL TAKES `IFaultSink` IN ITS CONSTRUCTOR.** This is for the places
that cannot, and the architecture test counts it so the number can be watched. A view-model
reaching for this instead of declaring its dependency is a review failure, not a shortcut.

**THREADING.** `Sink` is read without a lock on purpose — a reference field's
reads and writes are atomic, it is written exactly once during startup, and a fault reporter
that can block is a fault reporter that deadlocks the thread it was trying to help.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### Faults.Sink (line 54)

The installed sink. Before `Install` runs this is a `NullFaultSink`,
which logs and surfaces nothing — correct for the pre-UI startup window, where there is no
window to show a notice on.

### Faults.IsInstalled (line 61)

True once the real sink is in place. Startup code can tell the difference.

### Faults.Install (line 64)

Installs the production sink. Called once, from the composition root, immediately after it
builds one.

⚠️ Installing twice is a defect — it means two graphs exist and half the application
is reporting to the wrong one — but it is NOT worth throwing over. A throw here would take
down startup to complain about diagnostics. The second call is ignored and recorded.

### Faults.ResetForTests (line 87)

Test teardown. Never called in production — the sink lives as long as the process.

### Faults.Recoverable (line 95)

The user's outcome is unchanged. DEBUG breadcrumb only, nothing on screen.

⚠️ The bar is "the outcome is unchanged", not "we kept running".

### Faults.Degraded (line 102)

Something the user can perceive stopped; the session is intact.
`userMessage` must name what stopped AND what still works.

### Faults.Fatal (line 109)

The thing the user asked for cannot happen. They get a dialog.

### Faults.Report (line 113)

⚠️ THIS METHOD MAY NOT THROW, EVER.

A reporter that can fault is a reporter every call site wraps in `try { } catch { }` —
which is the exact shape being retired, reintroduced by the thing retiring it. The
production sink has its own outermost guard for the same reason; this is the second one,
because this path is reachable before any sink is installed and from threads that are
already unwinding.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Abstractions/IClock.cs

### IClock — INJSEAM_02 (line 4)

INJSEAM_02 — the wall clock, behind an interface.

Small, but it is the difference between a test and a stopwatch. Three pieces of shipped logic
are pure functions of "what time is it": the update probe's 24-hour throttle
(`05_SYSTEM_LIFECYCLE_STORAGE.md` §6, SYS-AUTOUPDATE), log retention's 14-day trim
(§2, SYS-LOGGING), and `ProjectDocument.CreatedUtc`/`ModifiedUtc`
(`06_PROJECT_DOCUMENT_MODEL.md`). With `DateTimeOffset.UtcNow` called inline, testing
"does the throttle actually suppress a second probe 23 hours later" means sleeping, or not
testing it. There is currently no test for it.

### SystemClock (line 22)

The real clock. The only implementation that ships.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Abstractions/IFaultSink.cs

### IFaultSink — FAULTTIER_01 (line 6)

FAULTTIER_01 — the one place a caught exception is allowed to go.

Implemented in the App layer by `UserFacingFaultSink`, which owns the actual routing to
`RuntimeLog`, `FloatingNotice` and `NativeDialog`. Core and the view-models
depend on THIS, never on those, which is what lets a view-model be tested without an Avalonia
dispatcher and lets a test assert that a given failure was surfaced rather than swallowed.

**THREADING.** Implementations MUST be callable from any thread. A worker thread reporting a
degraded fault must not have to know whether it is on the UI thread — the sink marshals. This is
deliberate: the old `catch { RuntimeLog.Fail(...) }` shape was thread-agnostic, and any
replacement that is not would simply be skipped by the call sites it was meant to convert.

### IFaultSink.Report (line 25)

Classify, log, and — for Degraded and Fatal — surface this failure to the user.

### FaultSinkExtensions — FAULTTIER_01 (line 29)

FAULTTIER_01 — the call-site sugar that makes the right thing shorter to type than the wrong
thing.

A conversion that reads `_faults.Report(new Fault(FaultTier.Degraded, "THUMBS", "...", ex.Message, ex))`
loses to `catch { }` every time, and the audit above is what losing looks like at scale.
These extensions exist so the migration is a mechanical, obviously-correct edit.

### FaultSinkExtensions.Recoverable (line 41)

The user's outcome is unchanged; this is a DEBUG breadcrumb and nothing more.
**No user message is taken, on purpose** — if you find yourself wanting to write one,
the fault is `Degraded`.

### FaultSinkExtensions.Degraded (line 49)

Something visible stopped working; the session survives.
`userMessage` must name what stopped AND what still works.

### FaultSinkExtensions.Fatal (line 56)

The thing the user asked for cannot happen. They get a dialog.

### FaultSinkExtensions.Guard (line 60)

Runs `action` and converts any exception into a classified fault.
The literal replacement for `try { action(); } catch { }`.

**Returns:** `true` when the action completed without throwing.

### FaultSinkExtensions.GuardAsync (line 79)

Async `Guard(IFaultSink, string, string, Action, FaultTier)`.

⚠️ `OperationCanceledException` is RE-THROWN, never reported. A cancel is
the user getting what they asked for; reporting it as a fault is how a Cancel button ends up
showing an error pill. Callers that must not propagate it catch it themselves — the suite
already does this correctly in `UpdateService` and `ProcessWorker`.

### FaultSinkExtensions.GuardValue (line 105)

Value-returning `Guard(IFaultSink, string, string, Action, FaultTier)`; yields
`fallback` when the function throws. Replaces the
`try { return Compute(); } catch { return default; }` shape, which is the single most
common swallow in the codebase and the one that most reliably produces a blank readout.

### NullFaultSink (line 125)

A sink that only logs, for unit tests and for the pre-UI startup window in `Program.cs`
before an Avalonia window exists to host a notice. **Not a production fallback**: shipping
code that resolves to this is back to swallowing, which is the defect this whole file closes.

### NullFaultSink.Captured (line 136)

Everything reported to this sink, in order. For test assertions.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Abstractions/IProjectStore.cs

### IProjectStore — INJSEAM_01, PROJ_08 (line 6)

INJSEAM_01 / PROJ_08 — reading and writing `.fvsproj`, behind an interface.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
**WHY AN INTERFACE OVER A PERFECTLY GOOD STATIC CLASS.** `ProjectStore` is a static
class that touches the real filesystem. Every view-model that needs to save therefore drags the
disk into its own tests, and "what does the UI do when the save fails" — the case that actually
matters, because it is the one the user must be told about — becomes untestable without
contriving a read-only directory.

The implementation (`FileProjectStore`) is a thin forward to the existing static methods.
The atomic-write protocol, the `.bak` cascade and the amputation rule all stay exactly
where they are, in `ProjectStore`/`ProjectSerializer`, governed by spec 06. This
interface adds a seam; it does not add a second implementation of the protocol, and it must
never be allowed to grow one (`05_SYSTEM_LIFECYCLE_STORAGE.md` §4c: "Never reimplement this
sequence at a call site").
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### IProjectStore.Save (line 28)

Atomically write `document` to `path`, rotating the backup.

### IProjectStore.Load (line 31)

Read a project. Returns null on failure with `error` populated;
`loadedFromBackup` reports that the live file was damaged and the
`.bak` was used, which the caller MUST surface — silently loading an older version of
the user's work is its own defect.

### IProjectStore.NormalizeExtension (line 39)

Force the `.fvsproj` extension onto a user-chosen path.

### FileProjectStore (line 43)

The shipping implementation: a forward to `ProjectStore`. Deliberately contains no
logic of its own — see the warning on `IProjectStore`.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Diagnostics/DiagnosticReport.cs

### DiagnosticReport — FAULTTIER_01 (line 11)

SYS-DIAGREPORT — THE BUNDLE A USER CAN ACTUALLY SEND YOU.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
**WHY THIS EXISTS.** The fault tiers (`FAULTTIER_01`) route every classified failure to
a log under `%ProgramData%`. That is the right destination for the failure and the wrong
destination for the DIAGNOSIS: no user has ever navigated to ProgramData, found a rotating log
directory, picked the right file and attached it to a bug report. So every encoder failure on
every GPU that is not the developer's own has been, in practice, invisible.

⚠️ THIS MATTERS MORE HERE THAN IN MOST APPLICATIONS. The core risk in this product is hardware
variance: `HardwareScanner` picks between NVENC, AMF, QSV and d3d11va at runtime, and that
matrix is validated on exactly one machine — the one it was written on. "Export fails on some
AMD cards" is unactionable. "Export fails on gfx1030 with amf, falling back to libx264, exit
code 1, filtergraph stage 3" is a fix.

**WHAT THIS CLASS IS AND IS NOT.** It BUILDS a report and writes it to a file the user can
attach. It does not upload anything, and there is deliberately no network code here:
auto-upload is a consent problem, a privacy problem and a hosting problem, and none of those
need solving before the diagnosis problem is. A file the user can find, read in full, and
choose to send is the honest version of telemetry.

⚠️ **THE USER CAN READ EVERY BYTE OF IT, AND THAT CONSTRAINS WHAT GOES IN.** It is plain
text, not an opaque blob. Paths are reduced to their file names, because a full path carries a
Windows user name (`C:\Users\<user>\...`) and nothing in a diagnosis needs it. The
suite's own log privacy gate (05 §2 SYS-LOGGING) already makes this decision for log lines;
this applies the same rule to everything else in the bundle.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### DiagnosticReport.Add (line 52)

Adds a titled block. Order is preserved; the first sections are the ones read first.

### DiagnosticReport.AddMachineProfile (line 60)

SYS-DIAGREPORT — the machine profile. This is the half of the report that makes a bug
reproducible rather than merely believable.

### DiagnosticReport.AddLogTail (line 89)

SYS-DIAGREPORT — the tail of a log file.

The TAIL, not the whole file: a log can be tens of megabytes and the useful part of it is
always the end. A report nobody will open because it is 40MB is the same as no report.

### DiagnosticReport.Redact (line 133)

SYS-DIAGREPORT — removes the Windows user name from a line.

⚠️ Targeted, not clever. It rewrites `C:\Users\someone\` to `C:\Users\<user>\`
and leaves everything else alone. A general-purpose scrubber that tried to guess at
"sensitive" content would mangle filtergraphs and exit codes, which are the entire point of
the report — and would still miss things. One known pattern, removed reliably.

### DiagnosticReport.ToString (line 171)

Renders the whole report as the text the user sees and sends.

### DiagnosticReport.ToJson (line 194)

SYS-DIAGREPORT — machine-readable twin of the text above, for a future upload endpoint or an
issue-tracker integration. Kept in step by construction: both render the same sections.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Diagnostics/SecretRedactor.cs

### SecretRedactor (line 6)

Central secret redaction helper shared by Core diagnostics, RuntimeLog, and AI providers.
Scrubs API keys (explicit and pattern-matched Google/Anthropic/OpenAI tokens), URL key query
parameters, and sensitive authorization headers without mutating unrelated filtergraphs or paths.

### SecretRedactor.RedactSecrets (line 21)

Redacts API keys, query secrets, and auth headers from arbitrary log/error text.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Editing/CropConfigJson.cs

### CropConfigJson — CROPJSON_01, EDITSTATE_01 (line 9)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
CROPJSON_01 — the typed accessors over the crop-tool config document.

Extracted from `CropToolWindow`, where they were private statics among 172 methods. They
touch no window state, and between them they had FORTY call sites in that file — which is
exactly why they were invisible there: the reading and writing rules for a cross-process
config file were buried inside a UI class. EDITSTATE_01 moved them to Core beside their only
consumer, `CropProfileCodec`.

⚠️ THIS DOCUMENT IS A CROSS-PROCESS, CROSS-VERSION CONTRACT. `CropConfigStore` is read
back by the Main App after the Crop Tool hands off. A config written by a new build must still
load in an older one and vice versa, so the tolerant parsing below (fallback on absent,
malformed or wrongly-typed nodes) is the contract, not defensive padding. Bodies moved
verbatim; do not "tighten" any of them.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### CropConfigJson.ReadSectionNode — KEYCASE_01 (line 40)

KEYCASE_01 — reads a section entry by element key, case-insensitively.

RoleByKey is an OrdinalIgnoreCase dictionary, so everywhere else in this window "Loot" and
"loot" are one role. System.Text.Json.Nodes.JsonObject, however, indexes ORDINALLY: a config
written by an older build, hand-edited, or produced on a case-preserving path could hold
"Loot" while the role table offers "loot", and every `section[role.Key]` read then returned
null. The element looked absent on load and was re-created as a second entry on save, so the
exporter drew one of them and the user edited the other.

### CropConfigJson.WriteSectionNode — KEYCASE_01 (line 68)

KEYCASE_01 — writes a section entry under `key` and removes every
case-variant of it, so a save can never leave two spellings of the same element behind.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Editing/CropEditSession.cs

### CropEditSession — EDITSTATE_01, UNDO_27 (line 14)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
EDITSTATE_01 — THE CROP TOOL'S DURABLE EDIT STATE, OUT OF THE WINDOW.

Owns what 08 §3a says MOVES: the placed crop layers (source rectangle, portrait layout, layer
order), the logical selection (by role KEY, never by control reference), the tombstones of
deliberately deleted elements, the active profile and the profile-name rules, the element
catalogue (built-ins + this profile's custom elements), the capture resolution the geometry is
measured against, the dirty flag, and the history (the shared
`UndoStack{T}` of `CropLayoutSnapshot`).

⚠️ NOT HERE, ON PURPOSE: canvases, the element visuals, drag adorners, resize handles, hit
testing, pointer capture, the frozen-frame zoom and display transforms — the window's. And the
Magic Wand's transient state (the scan, the AI request and its `Task`, cancellation, the
candidate rectangles and their visuals, progress): none of it is an edit. A candidate enters
this type only when it is COMMITTED as a layer (`AddLayer`), which is one history
step recorded by the window (UNDO_27).

⚠️ No crop maths of its own: every content-space question goes to `CoordinateMath`.

⚠️ UI-thread only, like the history it owns (07 §3).

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### CropEditSession.MaxProfileNameLength (line 42)

Upper bound on a profile name: well short of MAX_PATH once directory and extension are added.

### CropEditSession.MinItemSize (line 45)

The smallest a placed element may be resized to, in portrait pixels.

### CropEditSession.CropEditSession — NOMASK_01 (line 52)

**isReservedProfileName:** NOMASK_01 — names a new profile may never take (the built-in "no mask").

### CropEditSession.OriginalResolution — RESGUESS_01 (line 60)

RESGUESS_01 — these are a PLACEHOLDER, not a fact, until `CaptureResolutionKnown`.
A profile can be opened before any video is loaded; clamping a 1440p or 2160p profile's
rectangles against this 1920x1080 guess truncates them, and a save would write the
truncation back. Anything that can corrupt stored geometry checks the flag first.

### CropEditSession.CaptureResolutionKnown — RESGUESS_01 (line 70)

RESGUESS_01 — true once a real video or snapshot has reported its dimensions.

### CropEditSession.Layers (line 74)

Every placed element, in the order they were placed.

### CropEditSession.SelectedRoleKey (line 77)

The selected element's KEY — identity survives an undo that rebuilds every layer object.

### CropEditSession.PaintOrder — ZCOLLIDE_01, ZTIEBREAK_01 (line 85)

ZCOLLIDE_01 / ZTIEBREAK_01 — the shared paint order: ascending Z, then RoleKey
(OrdinalIgnoreCase). The composer, the layer list (reversed) and MobileFilterBuilder agree.

### CropEditSession.ListOrder (line 92)

The layer list's order: top of the stack first, the exact reverse of `PaintOrder`.

### CropEditSession.DeletedRoleKeys — DELETESET_01, KEYCASE_01 (line 96)

ISSUE_2 / DELETESET_01 — role keys the user explicitly deleted while editing this profile. A
save MERGES into the document on disk, so this set is the only signal that a removal was
deliberate rather than merely absent. Cleared on a profile switch: a tombstone belongs to the
profile that created it. KEYCASE_01 — OrdinalIgnoreCase, like every other role lookup.

### CropEditSession.Dirty — CROPUNSAVED_01 (line 104)

Unsaved work exists (CROPUNSAVED_01).

### CropEditSession.ActiveProfile — GATE_01 (line 108)

GATE_01 — the profile this session is editing, or null before one has been chosen. Null is
the START state and it is load-bearing: nothing may be loaded, edited or saved without one,
because a save ends by writing over the active profile's FILE.

### CropEditSession.GateUnlocked — ISSUE_01, ISSUE_02 (line 115)

ISSUE_01 / ISSUE_02 — the profile gate is open (a profile was deliberately chosen).

### CropEditSession.ExistingProfileNames — ISSUE_01 (line 118)

ISSUE_01 — profile names already on disk; case-insensitive because the file system is.

### CropEditSession.ValidateNewProfileName — ISSUE_01 (line 128)

ISSUE_01 — the single source of truth for "is this a usable new profile name": null when it
is, else the sentence the user reads. An EMPTY box is not an error (SAVE AS NEW simply stays
off); painting a red border round an untouched field is noise.

### CropEditSession.CanSave — GATE_01, EMPTYSAVE_01 (line 154)

GATE_01 / EMPTYSAVE_01 — SAVE is live only with a profile chosen and unsaved work: a placed
element, OR a pending tombstone (deleting the last element and saving is a real edit).

### CropEditSession.BuiltInRoles (line 161)

The five built-in elements. Custom ones live in `CustomRoles`.

### CropEditSession.CustomRoles — ROLEPOPUP_01 (line 174)

ROLEPOPUP_01 — elements beyond the built-ins: the ones "+ New element" creates, plus any key
found in a profile this build has no built-in for. Anything that enumerates elements must go
through `AllRoles`, or a custom element is saved and then never loaded back.

### CropEditSession.RegisterCustomRole — ROLEPOPUP_01, NODUPES_02 (line 198)

ROLEPOPUP_01 — mints (or returns) the element behind a typed name. NODUPES_02 — looked up
twice: by derived key (an exact repeat, every custom name), then by DISPLAY NAME (a built-in
whose label does not derive back to its own key — "Loot Area" is `loot`, not
`loot_area`). Without the second door the user gets a twin wearing the same label,
saved under a key the exporter ignores.

### CropEditSession.AdoptRolesFromConfig — ROLEPOPUP_01, DELETESET_01 (line 226)

ROLEPOPUP_01 / A3 — teaches this session every element key a profile section contains.
Entries with no usable rectangle are skipped: a key zeroed by a delete is a tombstone, not
an element (DELETESET_01).

### CropEditSession.PrettifyRoleKey — ROLEPOPUP_01 (line 244)

ROLEPOPUP_01 — `own_ammo` → `Own Ammo`, for display in the chooser.

### CropEditSession.QuadrantGuess — ROLEPOPUP_01 (line 251)

ROLEPOPUP_01 — which element a box is MOST LIKELY to be, from its quadrant of the frame.
Purely an ordering hint; it never assigns anything on its own.

### CropEditSession.ToContent (line 281)

A source rectangle in portrait content space, with the element's drift rule.

### CropEditSession.QuantizeLayerSize (line 285)

The placed size for a desired width: the source crop's content-space shape, scaled by an
exact fraction and quantised the way the exporter quantises it.

### CropEditSession.SourceAspectOf — RESIZEFEEL_01 (line 300)

RESIZEFEEL_01 — height ÷ width of the SOURCE crop in content space: the only ratio that means
anything; every placed size is a rounded copy of it.

### CropEditSession.TryVerifyGeometry — RESGUESS_01 (line 310)

RESGUESS_01 — once a real frame exists, an element read from the profile without one can be
checked against it: clamped to the frame and marked verified. This is the ONLY way an
unverified element becomes verified, and so the only way a save may rewrite its crop.
Returns true when it became verified; an element that does not fit is left untouched.

### CropEditSession.NormalizeLayout (line 338)

Re-quantises a layer's size from its source crop and keeps it on the canvas.

### CropEditSession.AddLayer — NODUPES_01, NODUPES_02, DELETESET_01 (line 348)

Commits a selection as `role` (a drawn box or an accepted Magic Wand
candidate — they are the same commit). `content` is the source rectangle in
content space, where the element lands when its role has no preferred position.

NODUPES_01 — one element, one entry: an element already placed is REPLACED, matched by key
(OrdinalIgnoreCase — keys adopted from a profile may carry any case) AND by display name
(NODUPES_02 — repairs profiles that hold both `loot` and `loot_area`). A twin under
a DIFFERENT key is being retired, so it is tombstoned (DELETESET_01) or the save's merge
would keep it. `replaced` lists the layers removed, for the window to
release their visuals.

Selects the new layer and marks the edit dirty. The window records the history step.

### CropEditSession.AdoptSavedLayer (line 412)

IDEA_1 — a layer reopened from the saved profile. Not an edit: nothing is dirtied.

### CropEditSession.DeleteSelected (line 420)

Deletes the selected element and tombstones its key (ISSUE_2). Returns it, or null.

ZCOLLIDE_01 — a dense, strictly increasing z sequence from 1 in the current paint order. Two
elements on one z are three different answers (Avalonia, the list, MobileFilterBuilder).

### CropEditSession.MoveSelectedLayer — ZCOLLIDE_01 (line 441)

ZCOLLIDE_01 — moves the selected element one place up (+) or down (−) the stack by SWAPPING
with its neighbour, so one press is always exactly one place and never a duplicate z.
Returns the two layers whose z changed, or null when it was already at that end.

### CropEditSession.ResetWorking — DELETESET_01, EMPTYSAVE_01 (line 464)

Clears the working layers (a profile switch, or RESET). DELETESET_01 — tombstones die with
the working state. EMPTYSAVE_01 — RESET (`tombstonePlacedElements`)
tombstones what it removed and stays dirty, so SAVE can empty the profile on disk; a profile
switch does neither. Starts a fresh history. Returns the removed layers.

### CropEditSession.History — UNDO_27 (line 484)

UNDO_27 — the Crop Tool's history: committed layout only, never candidates.

### CropEditSession.IsRestoring (line 487)

True while a restore is being applied and re-rendered: nothing may record history.

### CropEditSession.ResetHistory (line 492)

A new starting point (profile opened, RESET): the history before it describes nothing on screen.

### CropEditSession.Record (line 495)

Records the current layout as the step `label` (U4/U3/U2 are the stack's).

### CropEditSession.Step — MERGEUNDO_01 (line 504)

Undo (true) or redo (false). Applies the target layout, lets `rebuild` (the
window re-creating its visuals, which normalises each layout) run under the re-entrancy guard,
then re-reads the result as an equivalent form — never a new step (MERGEUNDO_01), so the redo
branch survives. Returns the label stepped over, or null when there was nothing to step to.

### CropEditSession.ApplySnapshot (line 529)

Replaces every layer with the snapshot's. A restored key is no longer deleted. The selection
is kept BY IDENTITY when that element still exists (the layer objects are all new).

document and the exporter), the name the user reads, and where it lands by default. A key is
never re-derived from a display name (NODUPES_02).

**DefaultX:** Preferred portrait X; -1 = no preference (place where the geometry says).
**DefaultY:** Preferred portrait Y; -1 = no preference.

EDITSTATE_01 — one placed HUD element as the EDIT sees it: the crop on the source frame, the
layout on the portrait canvas, its layer and where it came from. Identity is
`RoleKey` (OrdinalIgnoreCase, KEYCASE_01).

The window owns the element's VISUALS (canvas, image, outline, handles, label) and points them
at one of these; it never holds the geometry itself. Undo history stores
`CropItemState`, the immutable projection made by `ToState` — never this
mutable object.

IDEA_1 — settable so a layer reopened before any video was loaded can get its picture later.

GHOSTKILL_01 — loaded from the profile rather than drawn this session. Not geometry, so it

### CropLayer.FromState (line 68)

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Editing/CropProfileCodec.cs

### CropProfileCodec — EDITSTATE_01 (line 13)

EDITSTATE_01 — the Crop Tool's layers as the crop-config document (a cross-process,
cross-version contract read back by the Main App), and back. Pure: a document and a session in,
layers or written sections out. The window keeps the I/O (`CropConfigStore`), the
thumbnails and the profile-file sync.

### CropProfileCodec.ReadSavedLayers — ROLEPOPUP_01, RESGUESS_01 (line 21)

IDEA_1 — the saved elements of a (sanitised) config as editable layers, for every element
the session knows about that is neither already placed nor tombstoned. Registers every key
the profile contains first (ROLEPOPUP_01 / A3), so a custom element is not invisible on reopen.

THE DRIFT TRAP: the saved `crops_1080p` rect is content space, and converting it back
and forth both round OUTWARD, growing the box every cycle. So the source rect is persisted
separately (`crops_source`) and read back verbatim; the inverse transform is only the
one-time migration for a pre-v4 file. RESGUESS_01 — clamped only against a REAL capture size;
otherwise the layer is marked unverified.

### CropProfileCodec.WriteLayers — RESGUESS_01, DELETESET_01 (line 107)

Writes every placed layer and every tombstone into `config` (MERGED: the
document may hold elements this session never touched). Re-quantises each verified layer's
size first. RESGUESS_01 — an unverified layer writes only its position and z order, never a
guess over its stored crop. DELETESET_01 — a tombstoned key still in the document is zeroed.
Returns how many layers were written position-only.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Editing/GranularEditSession.History.cs

### GranularRestoreStep (line 10)

One undo or redo as the editor has to present it: where it came from, where it went, what it was.

### GranularHistoryParking — UNDO_25, UNDO_24, COMPOSITION_02 (line 13)

UNDO_25 — THE ONE SLOT a closed Granular editor's history waits in for its clip to reopen.

⚠️ PROCESS-LIFETIME ONLY, DELIBERATELY: it survives closing the WINDOW, not closing the APP —
cross-restart history is the sidecar's job (UNDO_24). ⚠️ ONE SLOT, NOT A DICTIONARY: holding
history for every clip ever opened is an unbounded leak in a process that also holds decoded
video frames.

An instance, not a static field, so the window passes it in explicitly (08 COMPOSITION_02) and
a test can use its own without leaking into another. The window uses `Shared`.

### GranularHistoryParking.Shared (line 30)

The process-wide slot the editor window uses.

### GranularEditSession.History — UNDO_26 (line 39)

UNDO_26 — this editor's history.

### GranularEditSession.HistoryBaseline — UNDO_26 (line 42)

UNDO_26 — the state this session OPENED on (the accepted project revision the history is
built from), and what a parked history must match to be replayed.

### GranularEditSession.IsRestoring (line 48)

True while a restore is being applied and re-rendered: nothing may record history.

### GranularEditSession.BeginRestore (line 51)

Guards a restore AND the window's re-render that follows it (07 §3 re-entrancy): controls
repopulated from the restored state raise change events that would otherwise checkpoint.

### GranularEditSession.Capture (line 66)

U1 — plain data only: immutable arrays of records, doubles and the selection.

### GranularEditSession.Checkpointed — RECOVERY_03 (line 73)

Raised after every recorded checkpoint — by a window call or by any command here — with
whether it burned a redo branch (U3). The window refreshes its undo buttons and arms the
RECOVERY_03 debounce from it, exactly where the old `PushUndo` did.

### GranularEditSession.Checkpoint — UNDO_01, UNDO_02 (line 80)

UNDO_01 — records the state BEFORE a change. Call at the TOP of any action that alters
segments, cuts, memes, base speed or the freeze. No-op while restoring.

**coalesceKey:** UNDO_02 — non-null for continuous controls; null for discrete clicks.

### GranularEditSession.EndGesture — UNDO_02 (line 93)

UNDO_02 — pointer released: commit the gesture so the next drag is its own step.

### GranularEditSession.Undo (line 99)

Steps back one entry and applies it. Null when there is nothing to undo.

### GranularEditSession.Redo (line 102)

Steps forward one entry and applies it. Null when there is nothing to redo.

### GranularEditSession.ApplySnapshot — UNDO_01 (line 114)

UNDO_01 — puts project data back from a snapshot. Data only; the window re-renders.

### GranularEditSession.ClearHistory — UNDO_01, UNDO_26 (line 139)

UNDO_01 / UNDO_26 — THE APPLY BOUNDARY. Drops the history after APPLY (07 §7.1). Returns the
sizes dropped, or null when there was nothing to drop.

### GranularEditSession.DescribeRestore — UNDO_02 (line 151)

UNDO_02 (rule 7) — plain-English description of what a restore actually does, worked out by
DIFFING the two states rather than from a hand-written string per call site.

### GranularEditSession.AdoptParkedHistory — UNDO_25, UNDO_26 (line 195)

UNDO_25 / UNDO_26 — takes back the history this clip had when its editor was last closed.
Call ONCE, after seeding, so the baseline is the state the session opened on. Returns true
when a parked history was replayed. A parked history for the SAME clip but another revision
is stale and is discarded for good; one for another clip is left for that clip.

### GranularEditSession.ParkHistory — UNDO_25 (line 225)

UNDO_25 — parks the history on the way out, in place of throwing it away, then clears the
live one. An empty history clears the slot rather than parking an empty one, so reopening a
clip with no history does not resurrect a stale slot belonging to another.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Editing/GranularEditSession.Memes.cs

### GranularEditSession — MEME_06, MEME_09, MEMEMODE_01 (line 10)

MEME_06 / MEME_09 / MEMEMODE_01 — placing, moving, changing and removing memes as LOGICAL
commands.

**Two modes, two frames of reference — never collapsed.** A FULL SCREEN meme is an
insertion: it occupies its whole length on the OUTPUT ruler and obeys D7/D8 (snapped off speed
blocks and freezes, never two at one point, kept `MemeMinSeparationOutSec` apart). A
CORNER OVERLAY occupies ZERO output seconds, sits exactly where the playhead was (only pushed out
of deleted footage), may overlap anything, and never moves anything after it.

⚠️ Every source↔output question goes to an `OutputTimeline`. A meme is a POINT
in the stored model and a BLOCK on screen; there is no linear shortcut between them.

### GranularEditSession.MemeMinSeparationOutSec — MEME_06 (line 25)

MEME_06 — two memes closer than this (OUTPUT seconds) would be merged onto one seam by
ProcessWorker (a zero-length piece makes the filter graph fail to configure). Blocked here
instead, so the merge can never happen silently.

### GranularEditSession.MemeSeparationIsSafe — MEME_06, MEMESEP_02 (line 32)

MEME_06 / MEMESEP_02 — refuses a FULL SCREEN placement within `MemeMinSeparationOutSec`
OUTPUT seconds of another FULL SCREEN meme. NOT COSMETIC: see the constant.

Corner memes never take part (they are never spliced, so they can never be merged onto
one seam), and the distance is measured on `ruler` (the output ruler without
memes) when one is given, because the export's rule is in OUTPUT seconds: at 2x, two memes
0.06 s of source apart are 0.03 s of output apart and would be merged. Without a ruler the
source distance is used (it is never smaller than the output distance at 1x or slower).

### GranularEditSession.ResolveNewMemeAnchor — MEMEMODE_01 (line 60)

MEMEMODE_01 — where a NEW meme would land for a playhead at `rawSourceRelSec`,
asked of the LIVE ruler `live`. Corner: anywhere the gameplay survives.
Full screen: D8 snap, D7 "one per point", and the separation rule.

### GranularEditSession.AddMeme — MEME_06 (line 81)

MEME_06 — adds a meme at an anchor already resolved by `ResolveNewMemeAnchor`, and selects it.

### GranularEditSession.AddMeme — MEMETRIM_01, MEMEPOS_01 (line 86)

MEMETRIM_01 / MEMEPOS_01 — adds a fully described meme (its Id is replaced by a fresh one) and selects it.

### GranularEditSession.UpdateMeme — MEMEMODE_01 (line 96)

MEMEMODE_01 — another file, full screen ↔ corner, corner, size, sound: ONE undo step.
Going full screen re-applies D7/D8 against every OTHER full-screen meme. A change that
changes nothing records nothing (U4). Returns the before/after pair, or a refusal.

### GranularEditSession.UpdateMeme — MEMEMODE_01, MEMETRIM_01, MEMEPOS_01, MEMESNAP_02 (line 113)

MEMEMODE_01 / MEMETRIM_01 / MEMEPOS_01 — replaces every property of a placed meme except its
Id and anchor with those of `wanted`, as ONE undo step. Going full screen
re-applies D7/D8; `movedBySec` reports how far that moved it (MEMESNAP_02),
so the window can say so instead of moving it silently.

### GranularEditSession.RemoveSelectedMeme — MEME_06 (line 155)

MEME_06 — removes the selected meme. Returns it, or null when none was selected.

### GranularEditSession.TimelineExcludingMeme — MEME_06 (line 169)

MEME_06 — the ruler with everything EXCEPT one meme: segments, the freeze, the cuts and every
OTHER meme. Fixed for the whole of a drag of that meme, and the ruler a mode change re-applies
D7/D8 against. ⚠️ Not the freeze-free base ruler: a meme is still positioned against a freeze.

### GranularEditSession.MoveMemeTo — MEME_06, MEME_09 (line 188)

MEME_06 / MEME_09 — moves a meme to a new SOURCE point within the window's drag gesture, snapped and
validated exactly as placement is. Snapped against `baseTimeline` (the ruler
WITHOUT memes); ⚠️ NEAREST EDGE first, because SnapInsertionPoint alone throws a dragged meme
to the far end of any block it enters. `blockedBy` names the block refusing
it, for the status line. Returns true when the model changed.

### GranularEditSession.MoveMemeTo — MEMEDRAG_02 (line 198)

MEMEDRAG_02 — as above, and `refusal` says why a step was refused (a blocked
drag used to just stick with no word). A CORNER meme moves freely: it is only pushed out of
deleted footage, never snapped off blocks and never checked for spacing (it adds no length).

### GranularEditSession.DropMemesInsideCuts — MEMECUT_02 (line 231)

MEMECUT_02 — a meme anchored inside deleted footage goes with it, in the Speed Editor exactly as
on the main screen (TimelineViewModel.DropMemesInsideCuts). Sliding it to the edge of the hole
would move it somewhere the user never put it. Called inside the DELETE PARTS step, so one
Ctrl+Z brings the scene AND its memes back. Returns the file names removed.

### GranularEditSession.ResnapMemesOffBlocks — MEMESNAP_03 (line 258)

MEMESNAP_03 — D8 is re-checked after the speed blocks change: a FULL SCREEN meme a block now
covers is moved to that block's nearest edge (a block dragged over a meme pushes it along).
No checkpoint of its own: it runs right after the edit that caused it, so it belongs to that
edit's undo step. Returns the file names moved.

### GranularEditSession.SnapMemeToNearestLegalEdge — MEME_09 (line 279)

MEME_09 — pulls a dragged meme OUT of any speed block by the SHORTEST route, so the band
rests against the obstacle from whichever side it approached. Only chooses a better candidate;
the result still goes through SnapInsertionPoint, which owns the legality rule.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Editing/GranularEditSession.Segments.cs

### EditOutcome (line 9)

The outcome of a logical edit command: success, or the plain-English reason it was refused.

### GranularEditSession — EDITSTATE_01, UNDO_26 (line 16)

EDITSTATE_01 — speed blocks, MARK START / MARK END, zoom configuration, the freeze and DELETE PARTS
as LOGICAL commands. Each one records history the way the editor always has (07 UNDO_26: the
state BEFORE the change) and changes only data; the window translates gestures into these calls
and re-renders afterwards.

### GranularEditSession.MinSegmentAfterCutMs (line 24)

CUT_03 — a speed block left shorter than this by a cut is dropped rather than kept.

### GranularEditSession.MinSurvivingMs (line 27)

CUT_02 — minimum surviving footage, in ms. See MainWindow's identical guard.

### GranularEditSession.DefaultZoomBlockMs (line 30)

IDEA_3 — the length of a zoom container auto-created at the playhead.

### GranularEditSession.MarkStart (line 36)

MARK START at a TRIM-RELATIVE ms. Refused inside an existing block (returns its index);
otherwise clears the block selection and arms the start.

### GranularEditSession.MarkEnd (line 48)

MARK END at a TRIM-RELATIVE ms. Without a START, the start is inferred: the clip start when
no block ends before here, else one second after the previous block's end (or that end
itself when a second does not fit). Refused inside a block when no START is armed, and
before the START when one is.

### GranularEditSession.AddPendingSegment — MINLEN_01 (line 92)

Turns the MARK START / MARK END pair into a block at the BASE speed. The overlap ban and the
minimum length (MINLEN_01) are export-correctness rules, not UI politeness: every creation
path goes through them. Clears the marks on success and resets the pending speed.

### GranularEditSession.CreateZoomBlockFromPendingMarks — ZOOM_05, ZOOMLIVE_07 (line 124)

ZOOM_05 — the block a zoom lives on, from the MARK START / MARK END span. The same three rules
as `AddPendingSegment`; clears the marks. `newIndex` is the new
block, for the window to select (selection changes close an open zoom box — ZOOMLIVE_07 —
which only the window can do, so it is not pre-empted here).

### GranularEditSession.CreateZoomContainerAt (line 152)

IDEA_3 — a zoom needs a block to live on. Nothing under the playhead: create one at the BASE
speed (so the zoom is the only visible effect) from the playhead, up to
`DefaultZoomBlockMs`, kept clear of its neighbours. `newIndex` is
the new block, for the window to select.

### GranularEditSession.NeighbourBounds (line 192)

How far block `index` (spanning `startMs`–`endMs`)
may travel before it touches a neighbour: the nearest earlier block's end and the nearest later
block's start (each kept `SegGapMs` clear), else the clip's own edges. The
neighbour indices are -1 when the wall is the clip edge. A drag clamps against these.

### GranularEditSession.ApplyPendingSpeedToSelection — UNDO_02 (line 219)

The pending speed applied to the selected block. `gestureKey` is the
window's: the wheel is a continuous gesture, a preset button a discrete click (UNDO_02).

### GranularEditSession.DeleteSelectedSegment (line 231)

Deletes the selected block, speed and zoom together. Returns it, or null when nothing was selected.

### GranularEditSession.ClearAll — FREEZE_CLEAR_01 (line 241)

FREEZE_CLEAR_01 — CLEAR ALL removes every block, the marks AND the frozen frame (the freeze
changes the finished length; "clear all" that left it behind would not put the clip back to
normal). Returns whether a freeze was cleared.

### GranularEditSession.RemoveZoomFromSelection — ZOOM_04 (line 258)

ZOOM_04 — strips the zoom from the selected block and keeps its speed change.

### GranularEditSession.ApplyZoomToSelection (line 274)

Commits a zoom rectangle, already in SOURCE pixels, to the selected block.

### GranularEditSession.ClampZoomInsideItsBlock — ZOOMLIVE_05 (line 286)

ZOOMLIVE_05 — KEEP A ZOOM INSIDE THE BLOCK THAT OWNS IT.

`ZoomStartMs` / `ZoomEndMs` are deliberately independent of the block's own edges —
that is what the two magnifiers exist to control. The consequence nobody asks for: shrink or
move the block afterwards and the zoom span can end up partly or wholly OUTSIDE it. The
exporter would then be handed a zoom phase covering source time this block never renders,
and the preview and the export would disagree about when the zoom happens.

Called on every block move/resize release. It only ever narrows, never widens, so a user who
deliberately zoomed a sub-range keeps their sub-range unless the block shrank past it.

### GranularEditSession.ClampZoomEdgeAgainstSlowNeighbours (line 322)

OPTION C — stops the user parking two SLOW zooms close enough that the export has to take
a glide away from one of them.

WHY: a Slow zoom borrows 0.5s of footage before it to glide in and 0.5s after it to glide
out. Two Slow zooms therefore need a full 1.0s between them
(`FreeVideoStudio.Core.Media.GranularSpeedBuilder.ZoomRampRequiredGapBetweenSlowZooms`)
or neither of them can have a ramp on the facing side — Option A makes both snap in that
case, which is correct but is not what someone who ticked "Slow" was going for.
This clamp means they never reach that state by dragging in the first place.

PRECEDENT: the editor already enforces exactly this kind of rule for speed blocks — a hard
`GranularEditSession.SegGapMs` (1000ms) "social distance". This is the same idea for zooms, at the
same distance, so it should feel familiar rather than new.

SCOPE, deliberately narrow:
  * only applies when the zoom being dragged is SLOW **and** the neighbour is SLOW. An
    Instant zoom borrows nothing, so there is nothing to protect and clamping against it
    would just remove freedom for no benefit.
  * only clamps against the nearest zoom on the side being dragged.
  * returns the value unchanged when it is already legal, so normal dragging is untouched.
Flipping a zoom to Slow AFTER placing it can still produce a tight pair — that path is
handled by Option A in the export maths (both simply snap), never by a broken half-zoom hop.

### GranularEditSession.SlowZoomHasRoom — ZOOMLIVE_05 (line 367)

ZOOMLIVE_05 — is there room for THIS zoom to become a gliding one?

Mirrors `ClampZoomEdgeAgainstSlowNeighbours` exactly, but asks the yes/no
question instead of moving an edge. Both must agree; if the gap constant changes, it changes
for both because they read the same one.

### GranularEditSession.GetSlowZoomGlideAvailability — SLOWZOOMGLIDE_01 (line 398)

SLOWZOOMGLIDE_01 — Evaluates per-side glide availability for segment `segIndex`.

### GranularEditSession.ClearFreeze — FREEZE_CLEAR_01 (line 413)

FREEZE_CLEAR_01 — the logical half of UNFREEZE: no frame, no preset.

### GranularEditSession.SetFreezeAt (line 420)

FREEZE IMAGE — freezes the frame at an ABSOLUTE source ms, clamped into the editable window,
held for `durationS`. One undo step ("set freeze").

### GranularEditSession.RemoveFreeze (line 433)

UNFREEZE IMAGE — one undo step ("remove freeze").

### GranularEditSession.SetFreezeDuration (line 440)

A freeze preset picked while a freeze exists: the hold's new length, inside the window's gesture.

### GranularEditSession.ClampFreezeIntoClip (line 449)

FREEZE_DRAG — keeps the hold inside the clip (of `clipDurationSec`) and inside its legal length.

### GranularEditSession.MoveFreezeToSourceRelSec (line 457)

FREEZE_DRAG — moves the frozen frame to a TRIM-RELATIVE source second, as part of the window's gesture.

### GranularEditSession.MoveFreezeByFrames — FOCUS_01 (line 464)

FOCUS_01 — nudges the frozen frame by 60fps frames, kept inside the clip.

### GranularEditSession.NudgeFreezeDurationByFrames — FOCUS_01 (line 474)

FOCUS_01 — trims or extends the hold a 60fps frame at a time; the start stays put.

### GranularEditSession.CutsForTimeline (line 484)

CUT_02 — the cut list in the units OutputTimeline wants: clip-relative SECONDS.

### GranularEditSession.SurvivingMsAfterCuts (line 495)

CUT_02 — footage that would survive if `cuts` were applied to a clip of
`durMs`, measured through the export's own `OutputTimeline.NormalizeCuts`.

### GranularEditSession.CanDeleteRange (line 507)

CUT_02 — whether deleting [start, end] (already clamped to the clip) is allowed: at least
`MinSurvivingMs` must survive. Returns the surviving ms either way.

### GranularEditSession.DeleteRange (line 518)

CUT_02 / CUT_03 — DELETE PARTS as one undoable step: the cut, its normalisation, and the
reconciliation of blocks and freeze that overlapped the hole. The marks are spent and the
selection cleared, so the same stretch cannot be deleted twice.

### GranularEditSession.LastCutDroppedMemes — MEMECUT_02 (line 534)

MEMECUT_02 — the memes the last DELETE PARTS took with it, for the window's notice.

### GranularEditSession.NormalizeCuts (line 537)

Runs the export's own normalisation so the editor can never show a cut the export would not make.

### GranularEditSession.ApplyCutToSegmentsAndFreeze (line 545)

CUT_03 — removes deleted footage from the speed blocks and the freeze. Everything is SOURCE
time, so footage after a cut does not move (OutputTimeline maps it). Four cases:
  fully inside the cut          -> deleted outright
  starts before, ends inside    -> truncated to the cut's start
  starts inside, ends after     -> moved forward to the cut's end
  spans the whole cut           -> LEFT ALONE: OutputTimeline already splits it around the
                                   hole; shortening it here would double-count the removal.
A survivor trimmed below `MinSegmentAfterCutMs` is dropped. Every change is
logged, so the log alone explains why a block the user made is gone.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Editing/GranularEditSession.cs

### GranularEditSession — EDITSTATE_01, MVVM_01 (line 13)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
EDITSTATE_01 — THE GRANULAR SPEED EDITOR'S DURABLE EDIT STATE, OUT OF THE WINDOW.

`GranularSpeedEditorWindow` held every value the user is editing in private fields beside
its canvases, timers and the mpv host, so none of it could be constructed — let alone asserted —
without a live visual tree (08 §3a MVVM_01). This type owns exactly the part 08 §3a says MOVES:
the speed segments, the cuts, the meme placements, base speed, the freeze, the zoom
configuration carried on each segment, the logical selection, the pending MARK START/END span,
the dirty bookkeeping, the history (on the shared `UndoStack{T}` through
`GranularEditHistory`) and the values the Main App and crash recovery read back.

⚠️ WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT HERE. Canvas geometry, pixel drag deltas, pointer capture, hit
testing, marching ants, the zoom box in UI coordinates, timers, the filmstrip bitmap, the mpv
host and the timeline CACHES all stay in the window. Core does not reference Avalonia, so this
type cannot hold a control even by accident (07 §7).

⚠️ NO SECOND TIMELINE. Every source↔output question is answered by `OutputTimeline`;
the commands that need one take it as an argument from the window's cache. Nothing here maps
time on its own.

⚠️ UI-thread only, like the history it owns (07 §3).

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### GranularEditSession.SegMinWidthMs — LANES_02, MINLEN_01 (line 45)

LANES_02 / MINLEN_01 — one minimum governs every path that can produce a block.

### GranularEditSession.SegGapMs (line 48)

The "social distance" between blocks created around existing ones.

### GranularEditSession.MinFreezeDurationS (line 51)

A hold shorter than this is not a freeze, it is a stutter.

### GranularEditSession.MaxFreezeDurationS (line 54)

Ceiling on a dragged hold. The presets stop at 3s; drag is the advanced path, so it
gets more room — but not unbounded, or one careless sweep adds a minute to the export.

### GranularEditSession.GranularEditSession — MERGEEDIT_02, UNDO_25 (line 60)

**videoPath:** The clip (or, MERGEEDIT_02, the merge's `edl://` URL).
**trimStartMs:** ABSOLUTE source ms where this editor's clock starts.
**trimEndMs:** ABSOLUTE source ms where it ends; 0 = not yet known.
**parking:** UNDO_25 — where a closed editor's history waits for its clip to reopen.

### GranularEditSession.TrimStartMs (line 76)

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Editing/GranularJsonRead.cs

### GranularJsonRead — GRANJSON_01, EDITSTATE_01 (line 6)

GRANJSON_01 — tolerant readers for the granular editor's recovery payload, lifted out of
`GranularSpeedEditorWindow` (8,122 lines, 181 fields, one class). EDITSTATE_01 moved them
to Core beside their only consumer, `GranularRecoveryCodec`.

⚠️ TOLERANCE IS THE CONTRACT. Every one of these returns a caller-supplied fallback rather than
throwing, because they parse a RECOVERY FILE written by a possibly older build after a possibly
unclean shutdown. A stricter reader turns "one unexpected field" into "your crashed session
cannot be restored", which is the opposite of what recovery is for. Bodies moved verbatim; do
not make any of them throw.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Editing/GranularRecoveryCodec.cs

### GranularRecoveryCodec — RECOVERY_03, EDITSTATE_01 (line 12)

RECOVERY_03 — the Granular editor's live session as the `"granular_session"` node of the
crash-recovery file, and back.

EDITSTATE_01 — the persistence-facing VALUES moved here from the window with the state they
describe. The window keeps what is genuinely its own: the 300ms debounce timer, the
`EditorRecoveryWriter` and the file read. This type is pure: a session in, a node out.

Segments and cuts are TRIM-RELATIVE ms and memes are CLIP-RELATIVE source seconds — the exact
frames of reference the session holds them in, so rehydration needs no translation. A node is
honoured only when its video path AND trim window match; anything else is a stale snapshot from
another clip (or an older trim) and is ignored. A malformed entry is dropped, never fatal.

### GranularRecoveryCodec.Build (line 34)

The live editor state as one JSON node. UI thread only (reads the lists).

### GranularRecoveryCodec.TryApply — RECOVERY_03, MERGEEDIT_02 (line 100)

RECOVERY_03 — replaces the session's edit with `node` when it holds an
unfinished session for THIS video and trim window. Returns whether it did. A merge never
restores here (MERGEEDIT_02 — the Merger autosaves the merge itself).

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Infrastructure/AotJson.cs

### AotJson — AOTSAFETY_02, AOTSAFETY_01 (line 7)

AOTSAFETY_02 — APPENDING A NODE TO A `JsonArray` WITHOUT TRIPPING THE TRIM ANALYSER.

`JsonArray.Add<T>(T)` carries `RequiresUnreferencedCode` and
`RequiresDynamicCode`, because `T` could be an arbitrary POCO that System.Text.Json
would have to reflect over. Handing it a `JsonNode` never does that — the node is
already a JSON tree — but C# overload resolution picks the generic anyway, because
`JsonArray` implements `ICollection<JsonNode?>.Add` EXPLICITLY, so the safe
non-generic overload is not visible on the type. The call site therefore inherits a warning for
a risk it does not actually take.

Casting to the interface binds to the unannotated method. The distinction matters:
`AddNode` makes the safety PROVABLE to the analyser, where a
`#pragma warning disable` would merely assert it and would go on hiding the next call —
possibly one that really does pass a POCO. That is precisely the failure mode AOTSAFETY_01
(`docs/06_PROJECT_DOCUMENT_MODEL.md#PROJ-AOT`) exists to end, so it must not be reproduced
one call site at a time.

### AotJson.AddNode (line 31)

Appends `node` without reflection. See the type remarks.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Infrastructure/ApplicationPaths.cs

### ApplicationPaths.CleanShutdownIntentFile — RECOVERY_02, ISSUE_09 (line 55)

RECOVERY_02 — "the user meant to close the app" marker.

Written SYNCHRONOUSLY as the very first act of MainWindow.OnClosing, before any await, and
deleted by the normal cleanup. Its whole purpose is the case where the app is closing
legitimately but never reaches `RecoveryManager.CleanupLock` — most commonly a
Windows shutdown / restart / sign-out, where the OS terminates the process partway through
the asynchronous close. Without it the leftover session lock makes the next launch announce
a crash that never happened.

Deliberately lives beside the other recovery sentinels rather than in UiStateStore
(ISSUE_09): it is part of the crash-detection family, must be readable before any UI state
exists, and must be writable with one synchronous call on a shutting-down process.

ISSUE_09 — the SINGLE home for small per-user UI state files (onboarding counters,
dismissed hints, and anything similar).

WHY THIS EXISTS: these files used to be scattered in
`%APPDATA%\FreeVideoStudio\Settings` — a THIRD state root, separate from
ProgramData (settings.json, session_state.json, recovery) and %TMP% (logs, staging).
That fragmentation is exactly what made the "preserve my settings" upgrade option leaky:
the uninstaller/upgrader had to know about every root, and it did not.

New small state files belong HERE. STORAGEPATHS_01 routes normal development UI state
to temporary preferences; profiles and crash recovery retain ProgramDataRoot.

ISSUE_09 — the legacy %APPDATA% location, kept ONLY so existing installs can be migrated
once (see UiStateStore.Migrate). Never write here.

USERSCOPE_01 — the pre-migration machine-wide root. Read once for migration; never written.

USERSCOPE_01 — the per-user root.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Infrastructure/AtomicJsonFile.cs

### AtomicJsonFile.WriteText — ATOMICTEXT_01, SETTX_02 (line 69)

ATOMICTEXT_01 — the SAME power-outage-safe protocol as `WriteObject`, for callers
that already hold a fully formed JSON document as text and must not have it reshaped.

WHY THIS EXISTS: `SettingsManager`'s transaction (SETTX_02) serialises `AppSettings` through a
source-generated `JsonSerializerContext` (required for NativeAOT). Round-tripping that
output through `JsonObject` just to reach `WriteObject` would re-emit
the document from a different writer and risk silent formatting/ordering drift in a file the
migration tests compare against. This overload keeps the bytes the caller produced and gives
them the identical three-step guarantee that
`docs/05_SYSTEM_LIFECYCLE_STORAGE.md#SYS-RECOVERY` mandates:
  1. unique GUID temp file in the TARGET directory, opened `FileOptions.WriteThrough`
  2. `stream.Flush(flushToDisk: true)` — the bytes are on the platter, not in the OS cache
  3. `File.Move(temp, path, overwrite: true)` — an atomic same-volume NTFS rename

Writing a fixed-name temp file, or renaming before the flush, is what produces a correctly
named but ZERO-FILLED document after a power cut. Do not "simplify" this back to
File.WriteAllText.

### AtomicJsonFile.WriteBytes — ATOMICBYTES_01, CACHEBOUND_01 (line 95)

ATOMICBYTES_01 — the SAME power-outage-safe protocol for an opaque byte payload (cache files:
the Merger's lane PNG/peaks, CACHEBOUND_01). One implementation, not a copy at the call site
(`docs/05_SYSTEM_LIFECYCLE_STORAGE.md#SYS-ATOMICWRITE`: "Never reimplement this sequence").
Takes no lock: callers that share the file across processes still hold their own mutex.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Infrastructure/BinaryDelta.cs

### BinaryDelta — BINDELTA_01, BINDELTA_02 (line 9)

BINDELTA_01 — a binary patch: "the new file = the old file + these differences".

The main program file changes in every release, but most of its bytes only MOVE (new code
shifts everything after it). A plain copy-or-insert diff loses those; this one follows bsdiff:
it finds long matches between old and new, then extends each match ALLOWING byte differences,
stored as (new − old). Shifted code turns into long runs of zero differences, which Brotli
compresses to almost nothing. Matches are found with a hash index over the old file instead of
bsdiff's suffix array (memory: one int per 4 old bytes).

⚠️ A patch is never trusted on its own. The header names the exact old and new files by
length and SHA-256; `Apply(Stream, Stream, Stream, CancellationToken)` refuses any
other old file and re-hashes what it built. A wrong, damaged or malicious patch therefore produces
an error, never a wrong file.

BINDELTA_02 — applying never holds a whole program file in memory: old file and patch are
streamed through 64 KiB buffers (`StreamBufferSize`) into the output stream.

Format: "FVSDELTA1", old length (8), old SHA-256 (32), new length (8), new SHA-256 (32),
then a Brotli stream of blocks: varint diffLength, varint extraLength, zig-zag varint seek,
diffLength difference bytes, extraLength literal bytes.

### BinaryDelta.Header (line 39)

Header of a patch: which file it applies to and which file it makes.

### BinaryDelta.Create (line 43)

Writes a patch that turns `oldFile` into `newFile`.

### BinaryDelta.Search (line 140)

The longest exact match for neu[scan..] among the indexed candidate and the last offset.

### BinaryDelta.Apply (line 194)

Rebuilds the new file in memory. Kept for small inputs and existing callers; it runs the SAME
streaming decoder as `Apply(Stream, Stream, Stream, CancellationToken)`. Throws
`InvalidDataException` when the old file is not the one the patch was made for,
the patch is damaged, or the result is not the promised file.

### BinaryDelta.StreamBufferSize — BINDELTA_02 (line 209)

BINDELTA_02 — size of every managed buffer the streaming apply uses. Peak memory is
3 × this + the Brotli decoder's window (≤ 16 MB, `BrotliFile.Window`), whatever
the size of the program file. The old whole-file path held old EXE + new EXE in RAM.

### BinaryDelta.Apply — BINDELTA_02 (line 216)

BINDELTA_02 — rebuilds the new file from a seekable `oldFile` and
`patch` into `output`, through bounded 64 KiB buffers.
The old file is hashed and length-checked BEFORE anything is written; the result is hashed
while it is written and checked against the header at the end. On any exception the
output holds an unverified partial file and must be discarded (see `ApplyFile`).

### BinaryDelta.ApplyFile — BINDELTA_02 (line 305)

BINDELTA_02 — streams `oldPath` + `patchPath` into a NEW file
`outputPath` (replaced if present) and flushes it to disk. When anything fails
(damaged patch, wrong old file, wrong result, cancellation) the output file is deleted, so a
file at `outputPath` after a normal return is complete and header-verified.
Callers still verify it against the signed manifest and publisher before promoting it.

### BinaryDelta.AddModulo256 (line 346)

target[i] = (byte)(target[i] + old[i]) — vectorised, wraps modulo 256.

### BinaryDelta.PatchReader (line 365)

Bounded read-ahead over the decompressed patch. Tolerates streams that return any number of
bytes per Read (fragmented input); a premature end is a damaged patch, never a short file.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Infrastructure/BrotliFile.cs

### BrotliFile (line 5)

DIST-SMART — Brotli with an explicit quality and a 16 MB window. `BrotliStream` only
exposes coarse levels; large binaries compress noticeably better with the big window.

### BrotliFile.DecompressFile — ASSETHASH_01 (line 35)

Decompresses `source` into `destination`.
ASSETHASH_01 — when `maxBytes` is given, decompression stops with
`InvalidDataException` the moment the output would exceed it, and the partial
output is deleted. A damaged or hostile asset can therefore never expand past the size the
signed update list promises (a "decompression bomb" cannot fill the disk).

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Infrastructure/ControlledFolderAccess.cs

### ControlledFolderAccess — CFASAFE_01 (line 8)

CFASAFE_01 — what the app may know about Windows Security's "Controlled folder access"
(ransomware protection) WITHOUT touching it.

When that protection is on, Windows refuses every write into Documents, Pictures, Videos, Music,
Desktop and Favorites from a program Microsoft does not already trust, and shows the user a
"blocked" notification for each attempt. A new, unknown program is exactly that. The app used to
write its starter memes and songs into Videos and Music at every start, so such a PC showed a
block notification on every launch and the app had no memes or music.

This type only READS: the protection's on/off value (one registry value every user may read) and
the well-known folder paths. It never changes a Windows Security setting and never adds the app to
the allowed list — only the user may do that, in Windows Security. Whether THIS program is on the
allowed list cannot be read without administrator rights, so the caller finds out by one real
write attempt (`ProtectedFolderGate` in the app).

Spec: docs/05_SYSTEM_LIFECYCLE_STORAGE.md #SYS-CFASAFE.

### ControlledFolderAccess.BlockMode (line 31)

Windows' value for "on, block" (2 = audit only, 0 = off; 3 and 4 concern disk sectors, not folders).

### ControlledFolderAccess.Mode (line 41)

The protection's mode, read once per process. 0 when it is off or cannot be read.

### ControlledFolderAccess.IsBlocking (line 44)

True when Windows is refusing writes into the protected folders from programs it does not trust.

### ControlledFolderAccess.ProtectedRoots (line 47)

The folders Windows protects by default for this user (existing ones only).

### ControlledFolderAccess.IsProtectedPath (line 50)

True when `path` is one of the default protected folders or inside one.

### ControlledFolderAccess.MayBlock (line 53)

True when a write to `path` may be refused: protection on AND a protected folder.

### ControlledFolderAccess.IsUnder (line 56)

Pure (unit-tested): is `path` equal to, or inside, one of `roots`?

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Infrastructure/CooperativeShutdownGate.cs

### CooperativeShutdownGate — PIPEDEDUP_01, FFMPEGSTOP_01, RESCUE_01 (line 7)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
PIPEDEDUP_01 — the single-flight gate around `GracefulProcessTerminator`, and the
one place the "stop an FFmpeg child safely" mechanics are written down.

WHAT WAS WRONG. `ProcessWorker` and `MergerWorker` run structurally identical FFmpeg
pipelines and each held its OWN copy of this logic: the same `_shutdownGate` /
`_shutdownTarget` / `_shutdownTask` triple, the same `BeginCooperativeShutdown`, the same
`AwaitActiveShutdownAsync`, the same `ReadExitCodeSafely`.

⚠️ THAT DUPLICATION IS NOT A STYLE COMPLAINT. IT HAS ALREADY SHIPPED TWO DEFECTS:
  1. FFMPEGSTOP_01 — `ReadExitCodeSafely` existed in both files with the same name, the
     same signature and the same doc comment. MergerWorker's was hardened to route through
     `GracefulProcessTerminator`; ProcessWorker's was left calling
     `Kill(entireProcessTree: true)` raw. The PRIMARY export path therefore shipped with no
     cooperative FFmpeg shutdown at all, producing unplayable MP4s (no moov atom) on cancel,
     while the secondary path had the fix.
  2. RESCUE_01 — `TryRescueFinishedRender` was a byte-identical copy in both files, and
     both carried a File.Exists-then-File.Move race plus an unbounded index loop that the
     neighbouring `ResolveOutputPath` already documented as a fixed defect.

Every future fix to either pipeline had a coin-flip chance of landing in only one copy. One
copy, here, is what stops that.

⚠️ WHAT IS DELIBERATELY *NOT* UNIFIED. The two RunAsync pipelines are different jobs — the
editor export applies trim/cuts/speed/memes/overlays/voice-over to ONE source, the merger
concatenates N sources with normalisation. Only the PROCESS MECHANICS live here. Log tags and
the quit-command policy stay caller-supplied, because they genuinely differ per pipeline and
crash digests are read by tag.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### CooperativeShutdownGate.Begin (line 44)

Starts the bounded cooperative shutdown ladder for `proc` exactly once,
off the calling thread.

Fire-and-forget on purpose: the ladder is strictly bounded
(`GracefulProcessTerminator.CooperativeGraceMs` +
`GracefulProcessTerminator.HardKillConfirmMs`) but it DOES wait, and callers
include `Cancel()` (UI thread) and cancellation-token registrations, which run
synchronously on whichever thread cancels. Neither may block.

Single-flight: Cancel(), the token registrations and Dispose() may all race each
other, but only ONE ladder ever runs per process. Faults are observed so a background stop
can never surface as an unobserved task exception.

### CooperativeShutdownGate.AwaitActiveAsync (line 77)

Awaits the in-flight shutdown ladder, if any. Bounded by the ladder itself; a no-op when no
ladder is running. Callers await this before reading an exit code, so the code comes from a
process that has actually finished finalizing its output rather than one still writing its
trailer, and before deleting a scratch directory, so no file still has a live handle.

### CooperativeShutdownGate.ReadExitCodeSafely — ISSUE_04 (line 92)

ISSUE_04 — reads a child process's exit code without ever throwing.

Callers reach here after a CANCELLABLE wait whose OperationCanceledException is
deliberately swallowed, so the process may still be dying (Kill is asynchronous) and
`ExitCode` would throw InvalidOperationException. Give it a bounded grace period, then
fall back to the -1 sentinel rather than letting that exception masquerade as a pipeline
crash.

⚠️ `attemptQuitCommand` IS CALLER POLICY AND THE TWO PIPELINES
DISAGREE ON PURPOSE. MergerWorker passes false — its caller has already attempted the
cooperative stop, so a second 'q' would only burn the grace budget. ProcessWorker passes
true for encoders, because its cancellable wait can return with the ladder unfinished, and
false for the analysis children (null-muxer probes, the single-frame thumbnail grab) that
have nothing to finalize. Do NOT collapse this to one default.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Infrastructure/CoreLogger.cs

### CoreLogger.WarnAction — LOGVIS_01 (line 12)

LOGVIS_01 — handled failures that must reach PRODUCTION logs (throttled by the host).
Debug is dev-only (FVS_DEV_LOG_DIR). It stays for traces and full stack dumps, never for
"X failed" lines.

### CoreLogger.Warn — LOGVIS_01 (line 31)

LOGVIS_01 — see `WarnAction`. Falls back to Debug when no host wired Warn (tests).

### CoreLogger.Swallowed — FAULTTIER_02, FAULTTIER_01 (line 48)

FAULTTIER_02 — A CAUGHT EXCEPTION THE CALLER BELIEVES CHANGED NOTHING.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
**THIS NOW GOES THROUGH THE FAULT SINK, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE POINT OF THE CHANGE.**
It used to write two log lines and stop. There are 337 call sites, which made it far and
away the most common way a failure was handled in this codebase — and every one of them was
invisible to `IFaultSink`, so the fault-tier system that FAULTTIER_01 introduced was
reporting on 3 failures out of roughly 900.

Routing it here converts all 337 in one edit instead of 337. Nothing about what the USER
sees changes — `FaultTier.Recoverable` is log-only by definition — but the
failures now exist as far as the system is concerned: they are classified, they are counted,
they reach the diagnostic bundle, and reclassifying any one of them to
`FaultTier.Degraded` is now a one-line edit at that call site rather than a
plumbing exercise.

⚠️ **"SWALLOWED" MEANS RECOVERABLE, AND THAT IS A CLAIM THE CALLER IS MAKING.** The bar
from FAULTTIER_01 is "the user's outcome is unchanged", NOT "we kept running". A site where
the feature the user asked for did not happen is mis-using this method and belongs at
Degraded. The 337 inherited call sites are grandfathered at Recoverable because that is what
they were already doing; they are not thereby blessed.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
