## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MemeLoudness.cs

### MemeLoudness — MEMELEVEL_02, MEMELEVEL_01, LOUDSTD_REMOVED_01 (line 10)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
MEMELEVEL_02 — A MEME IS AS LOUD AS THE GAMEPLAY IT INTERRUPTS. Shared by the Main App
(ProcessWorker) and the Merger (MergeClipGraph), so a meme lands at the same level in both.

MEMELEVEL_01 pinned every meme to a fixed -14 LUFS while the gameplay is deliberately left at its
recorded level (there is no loudness standard in this app — LOUDSTD_REMOVED_01). A typical
capture sits around -20 to -25 LUFS, so every meme jumped 6-11 dB above the video it cut into,
and the preview (which plays memes raw) never showed it.

Now: gain = measured gameplay loudness − measured meme loudness, clamped to
[`MinGainDb`, `MaxGainDb`]. Either side unmeasured → 0 dB (as recorded).
No per-meme limiter any more: the always-on safety limiter on the final mix
(`PeakSafety.SafetyLimiterFilter`) covers it.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### MemeLoudness.MaxGainDb (line 28)

Safety rails: a near-silent meme must not be boosted into a hiss bed.

### MemeLoudness.MeasureLufsAsync (line 32)

Measures a meme; null when it cannot be measured.

### MemeLoudness.GainFor (line 53)

Gain that brings a meme measured at `memeLufs` to `gameplayLufs`.

### MemeLoudness.Chain (line 61)

Filters appended to a meme's audio (leading comma): the matching gain, or nothing.

### MemeLoudness.SpliceFade — SPLICE_03, SPLICE_01 (line 69)

SPLICE_03 — the de-click fade pair for a piece of audio that is butt-joined to an unrelated
neighbour (a meme, another clip). Same rule as GranularSpeedBuilder's SPLICE_01/02: 8 ms,
capped at 2% of the piece, nothing on a piece too short to carry it. Leading comma.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MemePlacement.cs

### MemePlacement — MEME_03 (line 7)

MEME_03 — ONE MEME, PLACED SOMEWHERE IN THE VIDEO.

Before this type the app modelled a meme as a single `string? MemeFile` plus a
`bool MemeAtStart`, so exactly one meme could exist and it could only sit at the very
beginning or the very end. D1 and D2 replace that: several memes, each at a chosen moment,
each a CUTAWAY — the gameplay pauses, the meme plays in full, the gameplay resumes from the
exact frame it stopped on, and the finished video grows by the meme's length.

**`AtSourceSecRelative` is CLIP-RELATIVE SOURCE seconds** — measured from the
trim-in point, not from the start of the original file, and NOT a position in the finished
video. It is the moment of gameplay the meme interrupts. Converting to a position in the
finished video is `OutputTimeline`'s job and must not be done by hand.

⚠️ THE POINT MUST ALREADY BE SNAPPED. D8 forbids a meme interrupting a freeze or a speed
segment, so the UI is required to pass the click through
`OutputTimeline.SnapInsertionPoint` and to draw its marker at the snapped value.
Storing the raw click would drop the meme somewhere the user never saw.

⚠️ TWO MEMES MAY NOT SHARE A POINT (D7). Validate with
`OutputTimeline.HasInsertionAtSource` BEFORE accepting a placement and tell the
user a meme already sits there, rather than silently stacking them.

`Id` is a stable handle used to follow this placement through the timeline model
(`OutputTimeline.InsertionAt`) and into the FFmpeg graph's stream labels. It must be
unique within one export and safe to embed in a filter label, so it is generated, never typed.

`DurationSec` is resolved BEFORE the graph is built — probed for a video meme, or
the fixed still-image duration for a .jpg/.png. The timeline cannot be laid out without it,
because every downstream position depends on how much time this meme occupies.

MEMETRIM_01 — shortest and longest length a still picture may be given.

### MemePlacement.MinClipDurationSec — MEMETRIM_01 (line 69)

MEMETRIM_01 — the shortest part of a video meme that may be kept.

### MemePlacement.HasCustomPosition — MEMEPOS_01 (line 72)

MEMEPOS_01 — true when the user dragged the corner meme to its own spot.

### MemePlacement.StillImageDurationSec (line 75)

Still images have no intrinsic duration; this is what they are given instead.

MEMEMODE_01 — a CORNER OVERLAY plays OVER the running gameplay: it occupies ZERO output

`OutputTimeline.Insertion`, so nothing after it (music, voice-over, cuts, other
memes) moves.

The output seconds this meme adds to the finished video: its length inline, zero in a corner.

MEMEMODE_01 — only the corner overlays (zero output duration).

Generates a filter-graph-safe identifier. Never derive one from the file name — meme files

Projects placements into the timeline model's own insertion type. Kept here rather than on

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MemeTransitions.cs

### MemeTransitions — MEMETRANS_01, MEMEANIM_01 (line 6)

MEMETRANS_01 / MEMEANIM_01 — the FFmpeg text for how a meme enters and leaves, shared by the Main
App export (`ProcessWorker`), the Merger (`MergeClipGraph`) and the corner
overlay graph (`CornerMemeOverlayGraph`), so every path renders the same thing.

**Full screen** (applied to the meme's own clip, times from its first frame):

- `Cut` — nothing (the original behaviour).
- `Fade` — `FadeSec` dip from/to black on both edges, the sound faded with it.
- `Zoom` — a punch-in: the first `ZoomSec` starts `ZoomAmount`
larger and settles to 100 %. Built from `scale` (eval=frame) + a fixed `crop`, never
`zoompan` (banned suite-wide, North Star 4). The output size never changes.

**Corner**: `Fade` = alpha fade in/out over `CornerAnimSec`; `Slide` =
the box slides in from (and back out to) the nearest side edge over the same time.

### MemeTransitions.VideoFilters (line 31)

Video filters to APPEND (each starts with a comma) to a full-screen meme chain whose frames are already canvas-sized.

### MemeTransitions.AudioFilters (line 53)

Audio filters to APPEND to a full-screen meme's sound.

### MemeTransitions.CornerAlphaFilters (line 63)

Alpha filters for a corner meme's picture (frames timed from the meme's first frame).

### MemeTransitions.CornerOverlayX (line 72)

The overlay X expression for a corner meme. `restX` is the resting X expression;
for `Slide` the box travels from off-screen on the nearest side to it and back out at the end.

### MemeTransitions.CornerOpacityAt — MEMEANIM_01 (line 87)

MEMEANIM_01 — the preview's opacity for a corner meme `memeSec` into a visible span of `visibleSec`.

### MemeTransitions.CornerSlideOffsetAt — MEMEANIM_01 (line 96)

MEMEANIM_01 — how far (0 = resting, 1 = fully off-screen) a sliding corner meme is at `memeSec`.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MergeClipAnalyzer.cs

### MergeClipInfo (line 10)

What the merger needs to know about one queued file. `Ok` is false when the probe failed.

### MergeClipInfo.Timing — TIMINGTAG_02 (line 13)

TIMINGTAG_02 — frame-exact timing (intro + fades) when the file carries it; null otherwise.

### MergeClipAnalyzer — SCRAPER_03 (line 17)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
SCRAPER_03 — CLIPS ARE ANALYSED THE MOMENT THEY ARE QUEUED, IN THE BACKGROUND, ONCE.

A user adds clips long before pressing MERGE. That time is used: each file gets ONE ffprobe
(format + streams, which is where `IntroTag` lives) on the thread pool, a few at a
time, the instant it enters the queue. The result is cached per file identity
(path + size + last write), so reordering, removing and re-adding, reopening the merger, or
toggling the scraper never probes again. The UI thread only ever awaits finished tasks.

Concurrency is bounded (half the cores, 2..4) so a drop of forty clips does not start forty
ffprobe processes at once and stall the disk the preview is reading from.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### MergeClipAnalyzer.AnalyzeAsync (line 38)

Starts (or joins) the analysis of `path`. Never throws; never runs on the caller's thread.

### MergeClipAnalyzer.TryGetCompleted (line 68)

A finished, successful result for `path`, without starting anything.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MergeClipGraph.cs

### MergeMemeInput — MEMELEVEL_02, MEMEMODE_01 (line 9)

A meme input already opened by the worker, for one clip's graph.
**InputIndex:** FFmpeg input index of the meme file.
**AtRelSec:** Where it is inserted, seconds from the clip's kept-in point (see `CompositeTimeline.MemeAtRelSec`).
**GainDb:** MEMELEVEL_02 — gain that matches the meme to its host clip's gameplay loudness (`MemeLoudness.GainFor`); 0 = as recorded.
**Mode:** MEMEMODE_01 — full-screen cutaway (spliced, the clip grows) or corner overlay (over the body, zero added length).

### MergeClipGraphResult (line 27)

What one clip contributes to the merge graph.

### MergeClipGraph — MERGEGRAPH_01, FRAMESNAP_01 (line 30)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
MERGEGRAPH_01 — ONE CLIP WITH GRANULAR EFFECTS, AS FFMPEG FILTERS (Video-Merger-Migration.md P7.1).

The Main App's export engine for speed/freeze/zoom/cuts (`GranularSpeedBuilder`) is
reused as-is, per clip, so a ramp in the Merger renders exactly like the same ramp in the Main
App. Order matters and is fixed:
  1. trim the clip's kept window (FRAMESNAP_01 epsilon) — timestamps start at 0;
  2. granular chain in SOURCE pixels (zoom rectangles are source px, so no scaling before it);
  3. the clip's canvas chain (colour + scale/pad|crop + setsar) and fps=60 — same as plain clips;
  4. memes spliced into the clip's own output (D4: effects stay inside their clip).
`GranularSpeedBuilder.Build` names its pads with fixed labels, so every label of its
graph is prefixed per clip; many clips then share one filter graph safely.
Pure: no process, no disk. Clips without effects never come here (their chain is unchanged).
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### MergeEditorSource.PreviewUrl — MERGEPREVIEW_SILENT_01 (line 95)

MERGEPREVIEW_SILENT_01 — the preview URL when some clips have NO sound track. mpv's EDL assumes
every segment has the same tracks; a soundless clip leaves a hole in the audio clock (mpv logs
"Invalid audio PTS") and the picture races through that clip at ~1.5x, then the playhead jumps.
So the URL gets a SECOND, audio-only stream: each clip's own audio, or `silencePath`
(a `SilenceChunkSec` silent file, repeated) under a soundless clip. The caller then
selects that stream's audio track (`SilentStreamAudioTrack`). When every clip has
sound, or no silent file is available, this is exactly `MpvUrl`.

MERGEPREVIEW_SILENT_01 — writes (once) the small silent FLAC `PreviewUrl` repeats under
soundless clips into `directory`; returns its path, or null when it cannot be made.

MERGEPREVIEW_SILENT_01 — the audio track of the audio-only stream: the LAST audio track mpv lists
(the first stream may or may not expose one, depending on whether its first clip has sound).
`trackListJson` is mpv's `track-list` property. Null when none is listed.

(`ExtraFreezes`) are kept in their clips; everything else is replaced.

MERGEMEME_02 — what the merge will really do with a meme the editor holds at
`mergedSec`: a FULL SCREEN meme inside a clip's fade-in / fade-out is moved to
that clip's start / where its fade-out begins (D17), and a CORNER meme stops where its clip ends
(effects stay inside one clip, D4). The editor applies the first at once, so what it shows is
what is exported, and draws the second.

autosave stopped updating (the queue on disk froze), and a later IsEmpty crashed the app.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MergedTimeline.cs

### MergeClipSource (line 8)

One queued clip as the analysis saw it. Seconds are SOURCE seconds of that file.

### MergedClip — COMPOSITE_01 (line 11)

One clip's slice of the merged timeline. `MergedLengthSec` (COMPOSITE_01) is the
clip's length on the merged clock: its frame count at 60 fps, which can differ from the source
length (`LengthSec`) by up to half a frame. Negative = not given (source length).

### MergedTimeline — SCRAPER_02, COMPOSITE_01 (line 29)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
SCRAPER_02 — ONE MERGED TIMELINE FOR PREVIEW, MUSIC AND EXPORT.

The Video Merger's clock is "merged seconds": the clips' kept content laid end to end, at 1.0x,
BEFORE the merge speed is applied and WITHOUT the synthetic thumbnail intro that a custom
thumbnail prepends (that intro is added after the music mix, exactly as the Main App adds its
own, so it never moves the music).

Which thumbnail intros are removed (all of them are tagged by `IntroTag`):
  • Clips 2..N: removed while the Thumbnail Scraper is on.
  • Clip 1: kept, because it becomes the merged video's own share thumbnail, UNLESS a custom
    thumbnail is chosen. Then it is removed whether or not the scraper is on, and a new 0.1 s
    still built from the chosen frame takes its place.
  • Fades are never touched. Only the intro is removed.

The merger preview (playhead, dividers, thumbnail marker), the Music Wizard (clip lanes and the
music window), and MergerWorker (trim windows, music delays) all read THIS object. That is why a
song placed at 0:42 starts at 0:42 in the preview and in the file.

`Remap` moves a merged-time position (a music start, a thumbnail) from the timeline
it was chosen on to a new one by going through (clip, source second). A song the user started on
a particular moment of clip 3 therefore still starts on that moment after intros are removed or
restored.

COMPOSITE_01 (Video-Merger-Migration.md P2.4) — this class is now a SECONDS ADAPTER over
`CompositeTimeline`. The kept windows, intro removal and the integer merged-frame
boundaries all come from there; only the continuous in-clip playhead maths stays here. Callers
move to `Composite` as the merger migrates; delete this adapter when none are left.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### MergerPreviewPlan — MERGEPREVIEW_01 (line 44)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
MERGEPREVIEW_01 — THE MERGER PREVIEW PLAYS THE EXPORT'S SCHEDULE (Video-Merger-Migration.md P8.1).

The preview plays the merge as ONE mpv EDL whose clock is the MERGED clock (P4.2), so the effects
must be applied live, the way the Main App previews them: mpv `speed` per stretch, a timed
pause for a freeze, a jump over a cut, a meme cut-away for a meme. This plan is that schedule, read
straight from the chunk list of every clip's `OutputTimeline` — the SAME chunks the
export's graph and `CompositeTimeline` use — so the preview cannot drift from the file.

Clock mapping: chunk seconds are clip-relative (0 = the clip's kept-in point, length = the clip's
merged frames / 60), so merged seconds = clip start + chunk seconds. `OutputSecAt` is
the body output second (the music preview's clock), equal to
`CompositeTimeline.MergedSecToBodyOutputSec` (unit-tested).
Pure: no UI, no mpv.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

MEMEMODE_01 — every corner overlay, on the GAMEPLAY clock (`GameplaySecAt`: body

MEMEMODE_01 — the GAMEPLAY clock at `mergedSec`: `OutputSecAt` minus

only (the preview hands memes to `MemePreviewDirector`).

Builds the edit list for the window's current state.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MergerWorker.cs

### MergerWorker._lifetime — PIPELIFE_01, PIPELIFE_02, PROCGATE_01 (line 11)

PIPELIFE_01 / PIPELIFE_02 — process slot, cancel flag, single-flight finish and the
teardown ladder, shared with `ProcessWorker`.

⚠️ This replaces a PLAIN, UNSYNCHRONISED `Process? _currentProcess` field. PROCGATE_01
fixed a use-after-dispose race in the sibling pipeline — the field read twice, once to
null-test and once to act on, letting the worker thread run `_currentProcess = null;
proc.Dispose();` in between so teardown called Kill() on a disposed Process — and this
file never received that fix. Its `Cancel()` passed the raw field to the ladder and
its `Dispose()` did exactly the two-read pattern. Routing through the gate closes it.

### MergerWorker._finishEmitted — PIPELIFE_01 (line 25)

PIPELIFE_01 — alias over the shared lifetime's single-flight flag.

### MergerWorker.ProgressUpdate — PIPEDEDUP_01, FFMPEGSTOP_01, RESCUE_01 (line 30)

Single-flight gate for the cooperative shutdown ladder. Cancel(), the cancellation-token
registration inside `ExecuteFFmpegAsync`, and `Dispose` can all
race; the gate guarantees exactly ONE ladder ('q' quit command → grace period → hard
kill → exit confirmation) ever runs per FFmpeg process.

PIPEDEDUP_01 — the gate's three fields and their three methods used to be written out here
AND, separately, in `ProcessWorker`. Two copies of one mechanism is how
`ReadExitCodeSafely` silently diverged between these two files (FFMPEGSTOP_01) and how
`TryRescueFinishedRender` shipped the same race twice (RESCUE_01). One copy now lives
in `CooperativeShutdownGate`; the members below are thin delegations kept at
their original signatures so no call site in this file changes.

### MergerWorker.MusicTracksArePlanned — MUSICWIN_01 (line 51)

MUSICWIN_01 — true when `MusicTracks` is ALREADY the finished bed laid out by
`MusicBedPlan` (every song's chosen part, the crossfade leads and any looping
applied). The tracks are then used exactly as given: re-probing them and re-looping them here
would throw away the windows the user chose in the Add Music wizard and lay the songs out
differently from the preview the user just approved.

### MergerWorker.OutputBaseName — OUTNAME_01 (line 62)

OUTNAME_01 — the user's automatic file-name base for merged videos (Settings › Output
Files). Output is `<base>-<N>.mp4`; null or unusable input falls back to
`OutputFileNaming.MergerDefaultBaseName`.

### MergerWorker.CaptionTrack — AICAPTION_EXPORT_01 (line 71)

AICAPTION_EXPORT_01 — live commentary captions track burned into merge via ASS subtitles filter.

### MergerWorker.CaptionHudRects (line 76)

Optional portrait HUD exclusion zones avoided during ASS caption placement.

### MergerWorker.AutoSpikeFlattening — PEAKSAFE_01 (line 81)

PEAKSAFE_01 — the user's "soften sudden loud moments" switch: a peak tamer on EACH clip's
gameplay, thresholded against that clip's own measured loudness. The true-peak safety limiter
on the final mix is always on and is not controlled by this.

### MergerWorker.MatchClipLoudness — CLIPLEVEL_01, LOUDSTD_REMOVED_01 (line 88)

CLIPLEVEL_01 — Settings › Merger "Even out the volume between clips". When on, every clip with
sound is gained toward the MEDIAN measured loudness of the queue (clamped to
±`ClipMatchMaxGainDb`), so clips recorded at different levels do not jump. The
reference is the queue itself, never an external standard (LOUDSTD_REMOVED_01).

### MergerWorker.HardwareStrategy (line 98)

G03 / ISSUE 2 — which chip should encode. Mirrors `ProcessWorker.HardwareStrategy`.

⚠️ CALLERS MUST SET THIS FROM `ExportEncoderStrategy.Resolve`, exactly like the
Main App does. That is what makes the two applications reach the SAME answer on the same
machine: Settings override → the suite-wide boot-scan result the Main App published →
the "unknown, re-probe" sentinel. This worker deliberately runs NO scan of its own.

Accepted values: "NVIDIA" / "AMD" / "INTEL" / "CPU" / `HardwareScanner.ScanFailed`
/ "GPU" / "Auto". "GPU" is the legacy default kept only so an un-set caller still behaves
sanely (best available hardware encoder); it is NOT the path the UI takes any more.

### MergerWorker.LastReportedSpeed (line 112)

G09 — last `speed=` value FFmpeg reported for the current attempt (e.g. "3.4x").
"?" until the first progress line arrives.

### MergerWorker.ClipTrims (line 120)

IDEA_8 — optional per-clip in/out points, in SOURCE seconds, index-aligned with
`InputFiles`. A null list, a short list, or an entry that covers the whole file
all mean "use the clip untrimmed", so an older caller that never sets this behaves exactly
as before.

Trimming is done with the `trim`/`atrim` FILTERS rather than input-level `-ss`/`-t` on
purpose. The filters are frame-accurate, and — the part that matters — the identical
start/end numbers are applied to the video and the audio branch of the same clip in one
place, followed by `setpts=PTS-STARTPTS`/`asetpts=PTS-STARTPTS` to rebase both to zero.
That is what keeps picture and sound locked together through the concat. Input seeking
would have split that decision across two mechanisms and risked exactly the silent
audio-drift this feature must not introduce.

### MergerWorker.ClipTrim (line 136)

A clip's in/out point in SOURCE seconds. `EndSec` of 0 or less means "run to the
end of the file".

### MergerWorker.ClipIntroSkipSec — SCRAPER_02 (line 142)

SCRAPER_02 — per clip, the SOURCE second where the kept content must start because a tagged
thumbnail intro is removed there (0 = nothing removed). Index-aligned with
`InputFiles` and produced by `MergedTimeline.ContentStarts`, the same
object the preview and the Music Wizard use, so export and preview cut at the same frame.

### MergerWorker.ClipIntroTagSec — SCRAPER_01 (line 150)

SCRAPER_01 — per clip, the tagged intro length (0 = untagged). Decides whether the merged file itself starts with an intro.

### MergerWorker.ThumbnailClipIndex — SCRAPER_04 (line 153)

SCRAPER_04 — custom thumbnail. When `ThumbnailClipIndex` is a valid index, a
`IntroTag.StandardIntroSec` still of that clip's frame at
`ThumbnailSourceSec` (SOURCE seconds) is prepended to the finished picture, with
silence under it, AFTER the music mix. Same construction as the Main App's intro.

### MergerWorker._outputIntroSec — SCRAPER_01 (line 162)

SCRAPER_01 — intro length (output seconds) the finished file starts with; stamped as its tag.

### MergerWorker.Edl — MERGEGRAPH_01 (line 165)

MERGEGRAPH_01 — the Merger's edit list. When it describes `InputFiles` (same count,
same paths, same order), clips with granular effects are rendered through
`MergeClipGraph` and the output length comes from `CompositeTimeline`.
Null, or a mismatch, keeps the plain per-clip chain for every clip.

### MergerWorker.MemeInputFile (line 181)

A meme file opened as an extra FFmpeg input for one clip's graph.

### MergerWorker.MeasureClipLoudnessAsync — CLIPLEVEL_01, PEAKSAFE_01 (line 185)

CLIPLEVEL_01 / PEAKSAFE_01 — one measurement per clip with sound, over its kept window, at most
three at a time. Null entries = no audio or unmeasurable (that clip is then left alone).

### MergerWorker.ClipMatchGains — CLIPLEVEL_01 (line 217)

CLIPLEVEL_01 — the per-clip gain toward the queue's median loudness (0 when off/unmeasured).

### MergerWorker.MergedOutputTiming — TIMINGTAG_02 (line 233)

TIMINGTAG_02 — the merged file's timing tag. Merges are CFR 60. Fades are UNKNOWN (null) until
Video-Merger-Migration.md P7.3 computes them from clip 1 / the last clip.

### MergerWorker._outputFadeInFrames — OUTTAG_01 (line 239)

OUTTAG_01 (P7.3) — the merged file's own fades, in its 60 fps frames: clip 1's fade-in and the
last clip's fade-out, when they survive into the output unchanged (tag known, the kept window
still contains the fade, no granular effects on that clip, no meme at that edge). Otherwise
null = unknown, never a guess. The base speed shortens a fade like any other footage.

### MergerWorker.MinTrimmedClipSec (line 270)

Resolves the effective in/out window for one clip, clamped to the real file duration.
Returns the untrimmed window when no usable trim is configured. A window that would be
shorter than this is treated as a mistake and ignored — a zero-length clip in a concat
chain produces a corrupt output rather than an error.

### MergerWorker.TrimStartEpsilonSec — FRAMESNAP_01 (line 278)

FRAMESNAP_01 — trim starts are backed off by 0.5 ms. Cuts are real frame pts, so the frame AT
the cut must be kept; at any rate below 2000 fps this can never pull in the previous frame.

### MergerWorker.FailureDetail — ISSUE_06 (line 320)

ISSUE_06 — raw error text (FFmpeg stderr tail / exception) behind the last failure.
The UI hands this to ErrorReporter, which extracts the root-cause line for the dialog.

### MergerWorker.LastFailure (line 326)

Strongly typed failure model providing structured category, stage, attempt history,
exit/error codes, and supporting diagnostics for ErrorReporter.

### MergerWorker.CancelledMessage — ISSUE_04 (line 339)

ISSUE_04 — the single message used for a user-initiated stop, so the UI can tell
"you cancelled" apart from "something broke" without string-guessing.

### MergerWorker._isCanceled — PIPELIFE_01 (line 345)

PIPELIFE_01 — private alias over the shared lifetime's flag, so every existing read and
write in this file compiles unchanged. Writing `false` is a deliberate no-op.

### MergerWorker.WasCanceled (line 356)

True when this job ended because the user stopped it, not because it failed.

### MergerWorker.Cancel — PIPELIFE_02, PROCGATE_01 (line 359)

PIPELIFE_02 — this used to pass the raw `_currentProcess` field to the ladder, which
is the exact two-read race PROCGATE_01 documents. The shared lifetime takes one consistent
read and acts on that single reference.

### MergerWorker.BeginCooperativeShutdown (line 368)

Starts the bounded cooperative shutdown ladder for `proc` exactly once.
Single-flight: Cancel(), the token registration, and Dispose() may all race each other,
but only one ladder ever runs per process. Faults are observed so a background stop can
never surface as an unobserved task exception.

### MergerWorker.AwaitActiveShutdownAsync (line 377)

Awaits the in-flight shutdown ladder, if any. Bounded by the ladder itself.

### MergerWorker.ReadExitCodeSafely — ISSUE_04 (line 380)

ISSUE_04 — reads a child process's exit code without ever throwing.

Callers reach here after a CANCELLABLE wait whose OperationCanceledException is
deliberately swallowed, so the process may still be dying (Kill is asynchronous) and
`ExitCode` would throw InvalidOperationException. Give it a short grace period, then fall
back to a sentinel rather than letting that exception masquerade as a pipeline crash.

⚠️ attemptQuitCommand STAYS false here. The caller has already attempted the cooperative
stop, so a second 'q' would only burn the grace budget. See CooperativeShutdownGate.

### MergerWorker.RunTwoPassTailAsync (line 1377)

T01 — runs the analysis pass and the real pass against the pre-rendered scratch master.

Audio is NOT re-encoded: it was finalised in the master and is stream-copied through by
pass 2, so it is encoded exactly once across the whole merge and suffers no double loss.
Progress occupies the 60-100 slice of the bar (the master render owned 0-60).

### MergerWorker.ExecuteFFmpegAsync (line 1423)

**progressFloor:**
T01 — start of this invocation's slice of the 0-100 bar. Defaults keep every pre-existing
caller on the full 0-100 range, so single-pass merges behave exactly as before.

**progressCeiling:** T01 — end of this invocation's slice of the 0-100 bar.

### MergerWorker.CleanupCancelledJobAsync (line 1608)

Removes every artifact a cancelled FFmpeg job may have left behind: the per-job temp
directory plus any explicitly listed partial outputs. Runs BEFORE the cancelled status
is emitted, so by the time the UI reports the stop there are no 0-byte or truncated
video files left in the output location. Never throws — a cleanup failure must not
mask the cancellation itself.

### MergerWorker.TryDeleteFileWithRetryAsync (line 1628)

Deletes a file, retrying briefly: a just-stopped encoder's file handles can take a
moment to be released by the OS, and a single un-retried delete is exactly how
cancelled jobs leave partial outputs behind on disk. Never throws.

### MergerWorker.TryDeleteDirectoryWithRetryAsync (line 1667)

Directory counterpart of `TryDeleteFileWithRetryAsync`. Never throws.

### MergerWorker.TryRescueFinishedRender — RESCUE_01 (line 1704)

genuinely nothing left to save.

RESCUE_01 — delegates to `RescuedOutputPath.TryRescue`. This was a byte-for-byte
copy of `ProcessWorker.TryRescueFinishedRender` carrying the same
File.Exists-then-File.Move race and the same unbounded index loop; see
`RescuedOutputPath` for the full failure analysis. Keeping one copy is
deliberate: the twin pair `ReadExitCodeSafely` silently diverged between these two
files, and duplicated logic is how that happened.

⚠️ "Merged-Videos-RECOVERED-" and the "Merger" tag are preserved verbatim — they are how
the user and the crash digest know a rescued file came from the Merger and not the editor.

### MergerWorker.MapCaptionTrackForExport — ISSUE_11 (line 1725)

ISSUE_11 — disposing the worker now also STOPS the encoder.

WHAT WAS WRONG: this released the bookkeeping object and nothing else. Every caller is
expected to call `Cancel` first, but nothing enforced that, so any path that
disposed a worker without cancelling left FFmpeg grinding at full CPU on a merge whose
progress window had already gone — the machine stayed hot and loud for a file nobody would
ever receive. Killing the tree here makes the object's own teardown sufficient.

Maps `source` captions onto the merger export body-output clock.
When `frameAuthority` is present it already accounts for speed, cuts, freezes,
and inline meme splice offsets (so `splices` is null to avoid double-shifting),
while lines spanning across an inline meme insertion point are trimmed cleanly at the meme boundary.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MicLevelMonitor.cs

### MicLevelMonitor — VOMON_01 (line 8)

VOMON_01 — LISTENS WITHOUT RECORDING.

The EQ meter used to be fed only by `VoiceRecorder`, so it was frozen until a take
was already running. That made the single most common failure — the wrong input device selected,
or Windows microphone privacy blocking the app — invisible until AFTER a take had been lost.
This opens the same device with the same format, throws every buffer away, and reports only the
peak, so the meter proves the microphone works before the user commits to a take.

⚠️ It must be stopped before `VoiceRecorder` opens the same device: some drivers
(and every exclusive-mode endpoint) refuse a second capture handle. `Stop` is idempotent
and safe to call from the UI thread.

### MicLevelMonitor.LevelChanged (line 31)

Peak of the last buffer, 0..1. Raised on NAudio's capture thread.

### MicLevelMonitor.Stopped — MICHEALTH_01 (line 34)

MICHEALTH_01 — Raised when monitoring stops unexpectedly with a hardware or driver fault.

### MicLevelMonitor.PcmAvailable — SPECTRUM_01 (line 37)

SPECTRUM_01 — the raw PCM of every buffer, in the format the device was actually opened
with, for the frequency-spectrum meter. Raised on NAudio's capture thread. ⚠️ The buffer is
the driver's and is only valid during the event; handlers must consume it synchronously.

### MicLevelMonitor.IsRunning (line 44)

True while a capture handle is open and buffers are arriving.

### MicLevelMonitor.DeviceNumber (line 47)

Device this monitor is currently listening to, or -1 when stopped.

### MicLevelMonitor.BuffersSeen (line 50)

Number of DataAvailable callbacks received by this monitor.

### MicLevelMonitor.LastError (line 53)

Last fault seen on this monitor instance, if any.

### MicLevelMonitor.CaptureError (line 56)

Runtime capture fault during active monitoring (unexpected driver stop, buffer failure).

### MicLevelMonitor.ReleaseError (line 59)

Endpoint stop or disposal failure when closing waveIn.

### MicLevelMonitor.Start (line 62)

Opens (or re-opens) the given device. Restarting on the SAME device is a no-op, so this can
be called freely from selection-changed handlers without churning the driver.

### MicLevelMonitor.Stop (line 142)

Closes the capture handle. Safe to call when already stopped.

### MicLevelMonitor.ToCaptureFormat — SPECTRUM_01 (line 222)

SPECTRUM_01 — maps the device's NAudio format onto the analyzer's explicit contract.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.cs

### PcmSampleEncoding (line 11)

Sample encoding of a capture buffer.

### PcmSampleEncoding.Pcm (line 14)

Signed little-endian integer PCM.

32-bit IEEE float, little-endian.

The real format of a capture buffer. Never assumed — the analyzer decodes by it.

### PcmCaptureFormat.ProductionMicrophone (line 23)

The fixed production capture contract (MicLevelMonitor / VoiceRecorder): 44.1 kHz, mono, PCM16.

### PcmCaptureFormat.BlockAlign (line 26)

Bytes per interleaved frame.

A display band: a defined, ascending frequency range and the FFT bins it owns.

### SpectrumBand.CenterHz (line 45)

Geometric centre of the band.

### SpectrumBand.Contains (line 48)

True when `hz` falls inside [LowHz, HighHz).

SPECTRUM_01 — one immutable analysis result. Safe to hand across threads; never mutated.

### MicrophoneSpectrumSnapshot.Bands (line 77)

Ascending frequency bands, left to right.

### MicrophoneSpectrumSnapshot.BandDbfs (line 80)

Unclamped band level in dBFS (0 dBFS = full-scale sine). Finite; floors at -120.

### MicrophoneSpectrumSnapshot.Levels (line 83)

Display level per band, 0..1 over [`DisplayFloorDb`, `DisplayCeilingDb`].

### MicrophoneSpectrumSnapshot.Sequence (line 86)

Analyses performed by the producing analyzer since its last reset (1-based).

### MicrophoneSpectrumSnapshot.BinWidthHz (line 94)

Frequency resolution of the analysis: one FFT bin.

### MicrophoneSpectrumSnapshot.PeakBandIndex (line 97)

Index of the loudest band.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer — SPECTRUM_01 (line 109)

SPECTRUM_01 — windowed-FFT band analyzer over real capture PCM.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.SilenceDb (line 120)

Floor of the dBFS scale. Digital silence reads exactly this, never -∞.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.BinWidthHz (line 222)

Frequency resolution: one FFT bin, SampleRate / FftSize.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.Latest (line 225)

Most recent result, or null before the first complete window since the last reset.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.FramesConsumed (line 228)

Total complete frames decoded since the last reset (proves no sample vanishes across callbacks).

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.CarriedBytes (line 231)

Bytes of an incomplete frame carried to the next callback (0..BlockAlign-1).

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.StateElementCount (line 234)

Total managed state held, in elements. Constant for the instance's lifetime.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.Reconfigure (line 239)

Validates `format`, recomputes the band layout for its sample rate and
resets all state. Unsupported formats throw `NotSupportedException` — they are
never reinterpreted as mono PCM16.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.Reset (line 252)

Discards all buffered audio and the latest result (device / session change).

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.IsSupported (line 267)

True when the analyzer can decode `format`.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.Process (line 296)

Feeds raw interleaved bytes in `Format`. Any length is accepted: an incomplete
trailing frame is carried into the next call. Every `HopSize` new frames (once a
full window exists) one FFT runs. Returns the number of windows analysed by this call; when
it is > 0, `Latest` holds the newest result.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.ProcessSamples (line 338)

Convenience for tests and synthetic sources: mono float samples in [-1, 1].

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.PowerToDbfs (line 452)

Normalised band power to dBFS, floored at `SilenceDb`. Always finite.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.DbfsToLevel (line 460)

dBFS to the 0..1 display scale over [-60, 0] dBFS. Always finite and clamped.

### MicrophoneSpectrumAnalyzer.BuildBands (line 467)

Log-spaced ascending bands with every band at least one bin wide. A band that would be
narrower than one bin is widened to exactly one bin, and the REMAINING range is re-spaced
logarithmically so the top edge still lands on the effective maximum.

### SpectrumMeterBallistics — SPECTRUM_01 (line 508)

SPECTRUM_01 — meter ballistics on the display side: fast attack, slower release, and decay to
zero when no NEW snapshot has arrived for `StaleAfterSeconds` (callbacks stopped,
device failed, session reset). Time is supplied by the caller, so behaviour is deterministic
and testable. Not thread-safe; the UI thread owns it.

### SpectrumMeterBallistics.AttackSeconds (line 516)

Attack time constant (s). Rising bars reach 63% of a step in 20 ms.

### SpectrumMeterBallistics.ReleaseSeconds (line 519)

Release time constant (s). Falling bars lose 63% of their height in 220 ms.

### SpectrumMeterBallistics.StaleAfterSeconds (line 522)

No new snapshot for this long ⇒ the input is treated as absent and the bars fall.

### SpectrumMeterBallistics.ZeroThreshold (line 525)

Below this a bar is drawn as empty (and the meter is allowed to stop repainting).

### SpectrumMeterBallistics.Levels (line 533)

Current displayed level per band, 0..1.

### SpectrumMeterBallistics.Bands (line 536)

The band layout of the last snapshot seen (empty before the first).

### SpectrumMeterBallistics.IsSettled (line 539)

True when every bar is at zero and no live input is driving them.

### SpectrumMeterBallistics.Reset (line 542)

Drops all bars to zero immediately.

### SpectrumMeterBallistics.Update (line 552)

Advances the meter to `nowSeconds`. `snapshot` is the
newest available result (or null when there is no live input). Returns true when any bar
changed visibly, i.e. the caller should repaint.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MobileFilterBuilder.cs

### MobileFilterBuilder — NOMASK_01 (line 8)

Builds the FFmpeg filter_complex graph for portrait (9:16) mobile conversion.

Pipeline:
1. Source video → scale to 1280x1920 internal space (center crop)
2. Split into N+1 streams (1 base + N HUD layers)
3. For each HUD layer: crop from source, scale, overlay at computed position
4. Scale composed result from 1280x1920 → 1080x1620 (content area)
5. Pad onto 1080x1920 black canvas with 150px top offset
6. Overlay optional text PNG at y=0 (the 150px text strip)
7. Force yuv420p format

ZERO-LAYER CASE (NOMASK_01): when no crop rectangle is active — every rect is [0,0,0,0], which
is what the reserved "No Mask Profile" ships — steps 2 and 3 are skipped entirely, the HUD pad
is terminated with nullsink, and the result is the Portrait Canvas Trick plus the optional text
strip and nothing else. Steps 1, 4, 5, 6 and 7 are byte-for-byte identical to a masked profile,
so the framing of a no-mask export matches a HUD export exactly.

### MobileFilterBuilder.Build (line 28)

Builds the full mobile filter chain. Returns (filterString, outputPadLabel).
Exact port of build_mobile_filter_chain().

### MobileFilterBuilder.HudCanvasRects — AICAPTION_LAYOUT_02 (line 168)

AICAPTION_LAYOUT_02 — where each HUD piece of the user's Crop Tool layout lands on the FINISHED 1080x1920
portrait canvas, as (X, Y, W, H) pixels, for the caption layout to stay clear of. The layout's overlay
positions are already in that space (UI y includes the 150 px top strip and the 1280→1080 / 1920→1620
scale pair cancels out), and a piece's size is its 1080p crop × its scale. Same layer filter as
`Build`: retired roles, hidden teammates / spectating are skipped.

### MobileFilterBuilderExtensions.LookupHelper — KEYCASE_01 (line 267)

KEYCASE_01 — JsonObject indexes ordinally, but every element-key comparison in the Crop Tool
editor is OrdinalIgnoreCase. A config whose "scales" says "Loot" while "crops_1080p" says
"loot" therefore exported the layer at scale 1/1 with a default z, silently, with the user's
real values sitting untouched in the file. Look the key up exactly first, then ignoring case.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MpvCommandQueue.cs

### MpvCommandResult (line 10)

Command acknowledgement, not proof that a decoded seek has landed.

### MpvCommandQueue — MPVQUEUE_01 (line 16)

MPVQUEUE_01 — bounded FIFO with exactly one native acknowledgement outstanding.
Admission never calls native code. Only adjacent, unsubmitted replaceable requests coalesce;
relative seeks and command barriers retain their ordering. Completion never runs under the lock.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MpvIpcClient.cs

### MpvIpcClient (line 12)

Direct libmpv C API client. All method calls, property lookups, and event
notifications are routed through the native `libmpv-2.dll` via
`MpvWrapper` using the active `_mpvHandle` pointer.

**Event-Driven Architecture:** Instead of a 30fps polling timer,
this client spawns a dedicated background thread that blocks on
`mpv_wait_event`. Properties are observed via
`mpv_observe_property`, and `MPV_EVENT_PROPERTY_CHANGE` events
are intercepted in real-time. The `TimePosChanged` event fires
on the background thread — subscribers on the Avalonia UI thread must
marshal via `Dispatcher.UIThread.Post`.

### MpvIpcClient.GlobalMasterVolumeChanged (line 53)

Fired (with the level) on ANY change of level, mute or where the master is applied.

### MpvIpcClient.PerceptualGain — VOLCURVE_01 (line 76)

VOLCURVE_01 — linear gain of a slider position (0-100).

### MpvIpcClient.PositionForGain — VOLCURVE_01 (line 83)

VOLCURVE_01 — inverse of `PerceptualGain`: the slider position of a linear gain.

### MpvIpcClient.MasterLinearGain (line 87)

The linear gain a NON-mpv player (NAudio voice-over takes) must apply for the master: 0 when
muted, 1 when the Windows session already applies it, the perceptual gain otherwise.

### MpvIpcClient.PlayerMpvVolume (line 94)

The mpv `volume` property for a player whose own LINEAR balance is `balanceLinear`
(1.0 = the Music Wizard fader at 100%, or no fader at all).

### MpvIpcClient.IsSeeking (line 129)

True from seek submission until mpv has finished seeking, including paused seeks.

### MpvIpcClient.IsPaused (line 131)

Thread-safe pause state (updated by event loop, read by UI).

### MpvIpcClient.IsEof (line 133)

Thread-safe EOF state (updated by event loop, read by UI).

### MpvIpcClient.AnyPlaybackActive — THROTTLE_01 (line 147)

THROTTLE_01 — true while ANY live `MpvIpcClient` has a file loaded and is
actively playing it (unpaused, not at EOF). One volatile read, safe from any thread.
Background media work — the film-lane prewarm — polls this so background FFmpeg
extraction yields to whatever the user is watching.

⚠️ WHY NOT JUST IsPaused: mpv's default pause state is FALSE, so an idle client with no
file loaded would read as "playing" forever and would freeze every prewarm permanently.
Counting a client only while `Duration > 0 && !IsPaused && !IsEof`
says "video is being decoded right now", which is exactly the thing background work must
not fight for disk and CPU.

### MpvIpcClient.UpdatePlaybackContribution — THROTTLE_01 (line 161)

THROTTLE_01 — recomputes this client's contribution to `AnyPlaybackActive`.
Called on the event-loop thread whenever pause/duration/eof changes, and once from
`Dispose` so a dying player can never leave the count stuck above zero.

### MpvIpcClient.VideoFps — THUMB_01 (line 181)

THUMB_01 — the loaded file's frame rate, or 0 until mpv reports it.

Added so "move one frame" can mean ONE FRAME. The thumbnail marker's keyboard nudge used a
hard-coded 60, which is a frame and a half on 30 fps footage and two thirds of one at 120 —
so a step that claims to be frame-accurate silently was not on most real recordings.
Callers must still handle 0 (nothing loaded yet) and fall back themselves.

### MpvIpcClient.TimePosChanged (line 191)

Fires on the event-loop thread whenever `time-pos` changes.
Subscribers running on a UI thread must marshal via
`Dispatcher.UIThread.Post`.

### MpvIpcClient.SeekCompleted (line 198)

Fires after mpv's playback-restart confirms a seek has landed, or when a seek
is abandoned by an end-file/rejected command. May run on the mpv event thread.
The position cache is refreshed before a successful completion is published.

### MpvIpcClient.PauseChanged (line 205)

Fires when the `pause` property changes.

### MpvIpcClient.MpvIpcClient (line 211)

Wraps an existing mpv handle (created by MpvVideoView / OpenGL path).
The caller retains ownership of the handle lifecycle.

### MpvIpcClient.MpvIpcClient (line 222)

Parameterless constructor for audio-only mode (Music Wizard).
Call `StartAudioOnlyAsync` to create and initialize the
underlying mpv handle. Until then, all command/property calls are no-ops.

### MpvIpcClient.StartAudioOnlyAsync (line 235)

Creates a headless audio-only mpv instance using the native libmpv C API.
Sets `vid=no` and `vo=null` so no video decode or output occurs.
The client owns the handle and will terminate it on `Dispose`.

### MpvIpcClient.EventLoopWorker (line 301)

Background thread that blocks on mpv_wait_event and dispatches
property-change events to subscribers.

### MpvIpcClient.HandlePropertyChange (line 524)

Dispatches a property-change event to the appropriate state property
and fires the corresponding C# event.

### MpvIpcClient.PollDimensionsAsync (line 575)

Lazily reads width/height from the native handle (infrequent).

### MpvIpcClient.GetPropertyString (line 587)

Reads an mpv property as a UTF-8 string via
`mpv_get_property_string` + `mpv_free`.

### MpvIpcClient.SendCommandAsync — MPVQUEUE_01 (line 603)

MPVQUEUE_01 — bounded ordered admission. No native function runs on this caller.

### MpvIpcClient.SetPropertyAsync (line 673)

Sets a property on the same ordered queue as seeks and file loads.

### MpvIpcClient.ApplyPreviewGainAsync (line 684)

Applies the suite master (level, mute, curve) times this player's LINEAR balance
(`balanceLinear`, 1.0 = unity). See `PlayerMpvVolume`.

### MpvIpcClient.SetPropertyDoubleAsync (line 691)

Sets a raw mpv double property using invariant number formatting.

### MpvIpcClient.LoadFileAsync (line 695)

Loads a media file into the mpv player.
Sets the `start` property BEFORE issuing `loadfile`
(per project IPC rule: start must be set independently).

### MpvIpcClient.AddSubtitlesAsync — AICAPTION_EXPORT_01 (line 722)

AICAPTION_EXPORT_01 — ordered subtitle commands use argv, preserving paths verbatim.

### MpvIpcClient._eventLoopExited — ISSUE_13 (line 727)

ISSUE_13 — set by `EventLoopWorker`'s finally block. This is the ONLY reliable
signal that the background thread has genuinely stopped touching `_mpvHandle`.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MpvWrapper.cs

### MpvWrapper (line 9)

Native libmpv P/Invoke bindings using NativeAOT-friendly [LibraryImport]
source generation, plus safe synchronous wrappers for common operations.
All methods operate on the raw `mpv_handle*` (`nint`).

### MpvWrapper.MpvEventId (line 86)

mpv event IDs (partial — only the ones we handle).

### MpvWrapper.MpvFormat (line 103)

mpv property formats (partial).

### MpvWrapper.MpvEvent (line 112)

Layout: { int event_id; int error; uint64_t reply_userdata; void* data; }
Total on 64-bit: 4 + 4 + 8 + 8 = 24 bytes.

### MpvWrapper.MpvEventProperty (line 125)

Layout: { const char* name; mpv_format format; void* data; }
On 64-bit: 8 + 4 + 4(padding) + 8 = 24 bytes.

### MpvWrapper.SafeDestroy (line 138)

Safely terminates and destroys an mpv handle (null-safe).

### MpvWrapper.Stop (line 148)

Stops playback and clears the playlist.

### MpvWrapper.GetPropertyString (line 157)

Reads a property as a string. The returned nint is automatically freed.
Returns null if the handle is invalid or the property has no value.

### MpvWrapper.GetDuration (line 171)

Reads 'duration' as a double. Returns 0 on failure.

### MpvWrapper.ObserveProperty (line 179)

Registers interest in a property. When it changes, MPV_EVENT_PROPERTY_CHANGE
events will be delivered on the event queue.

### MpvWrapper.UnobserveProperty (line 190)

Removes a property observation by its ID.

### MpvWrapper.WaitEvent (line 198)

Waits for the next mpv event (up to `timeoutSeconds`).
Returns the marshalled `MpvEvent` struct.
The returned struct's `MpvEvent.Data` pointer is owned by
mpv and is only valid until the next call to this method.

### MpvWrapper.ReadEventProperty (line 216)

Reads a `MpvEventProperty` from an event's Data pointer
(only valid when `MpvEvent.EventId` == `MpvEventId.PropertyChange`).

### MpvWrapper.GetEventPropertyName (line 227)

Reads the property name from an `MpvEventProperty` (UTF-8 string).

### MpvWrapper.ReadEventPropertyDouble (line 235)

Reads the double value from an MPV_FORMAT_DOUBLE property change event.
The `MpvEventProperty.Data` pointer points directly to a double.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MusicBedPlan.cs

### MusicBedSegment — XFADEPLAN_01 (line 6)

One contiguous piece of the music bed: `DurationSec` seconds of `Path`,
starting `FileOffsetSec` into the file, laid at `OutputStartSec` in the
FINISHED video.

XFADEPLAN_01 — `LeadInSec` is how long this segment is ALREADY playing, under the end
of the previous one, when it reaches `OutputStartSec` (the export's song-to-song
crossfade). The file is read from `FileOffsetSec` for `LeadInSec + DurationSec`
seconds, so at `OutputStartSec` the song is `LeadInSec` into its window.
Zero for the first segment and for every plan built by the legacy overload.

### MusicBedTrack — MUSICWIN_01 (line 23)

MUSICWIN_01 — one song of a music bed and the part of it the user chose: play
`Path` from `InSec` to `OutSec` (seconds inside the file).
`KnownSec` is the file's full length, <= 0 when unknown; an
`OutSec` <= `InSec` means "to the end of the file".

### MusicBedTrack.WindowEndSec (line 31)

Where the chosen window ends inside the file, or +∞ when neither the end nor the length is known.

### MusicBedTrack.WindowSec (line 42)

Length of the chosen window, +∞ when unknown.

### MusicBedPlan — MUSICSYNC_01, CUTS_02, LOOP_01 (line 46)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
MUSICSYNC_01 — ONE DEFINITION OF "WHICH SECOND OF WHICH SONG PLAYS AT OUTPUT TIME T".

The export has always laid the music bed in OUTPUT time (CUTS_02). The start and end markers are
mapped through OutputTimeline, the first track starts at the wizard's offset, following tracks
start at 0, and LOOP_01 repeats the list until the video is covered. The live preview did none of
that. It computed the song position from RAW SOURCE seconds and played one file, so it
disagreed with the export by the base speed (1.1x by default), by every cut and freeze before the
playhead, and completely after the first track ended. A beat lined up by ear in the preview then
missed in the export.

Both now read this plan. `Build` is the export's former inline loop, moved verbatim
in behaviour. `Locate` answers the preview's question against the same segments.

⚠️ MUSIC SPEED IS ALWAYS 1.0x. The music bed is never time-stretched in the export and must never
be in the preview. Only the VIDEO changes speed. Preview sync is corrected by SEEKING the music,
never by nudging its playback rate.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### MusicBedPlan.Build — LOOP_01 (line 70)

**paths:** Music files, in play order.
**knownDurationsSec:** Full length of each file; <= 0 means unknown (the file then
covers whatever remains, which is what the export has always assumed).
**firstFileOffsetSec:** Where playback starts inside the FIRST file only.
**bedDurationSec:** How long the bed runs in the finished video.
**loop:** LOOP_01: after the list is exhausted, repeat every file from 0.
**startDelaySec:** Output time at which the bed begins.

### MusicBedPlan.Locate (line 132)

The file and the position inside it that the finished video plays at `outputSec`,
or `null` when the bed is silent there (before its start, after its end).

### MusicBedPlan.Build — MUSICWIN_01, XFADEPLAN_01 (line 148)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
MUSICWIN_01 + XFADEPLAN_01 — THE BED EXACTLY AS THE EXPORT WILL PLAY IT.

Every song plays its own chosen window (`MusicBedTrack.InSec` to
`MusicBedTrack.OutSec`), so silent intros and outros the user trimmed away are
never laid into the video. Consecutive songs overlap by the export's crossfade
(`MusicCrossfade.Overlap`): segment `i` starts `MusicBedSegment.LeadInSec`
early under the end of segment `i-1`, and its `MusicBedSegment.DurationSec` is
what remains of its window after that lead.

Why the lead must be in the PLAN, not only in the filter graph: `AudioFilterChain`
reads `Duration + overlap` seconds from each song. A plan that handed it a song's WHOLE
remaining length as `Duration` asked for 3 seconds more audio than the file has, so every
song ran dry 3 seconds before its slot ended and the next one rose out of silence — a hole
between every pair of songs, and 3 silent seconds at the very end. Here the lead comes out of
the window, so the export never reads past the end of a song.

The lead is solved so the export's own formula reproduces it from the durations it is given:
`lead = min(3, prevDuration / 2, window / 3)` satisfies
`lead = min(3, min(window - lead, prevDuration) / 2)`. When the bed runs out inside a song
the duration is cut short and the lead is recomputed from the shortened duration.

`crossfade` false lays the songs end to end with no lead (the legacy layout).
`loop` repeats the whole list, windows included, until the bed is full.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### MusicBedPlan.EndSec (line 228)

Output second at which the bed falls silent: the end of its last segment, or `startDelaySec` when empty.

### MusicBedPlan.OutgoingGainAt — XFADEPLAN_01 (line 232)

XFADEPLAN_01 — the outgoing-fade gain (0..1) the export applies at `outputSec`
to the segment playing there, when another segment follows it. A single preview player can
only play one song at a time, so it reproduces the outgoing fade with this gain and switches
to the next song at the boundary, where the export's incoming fade has just reached full level.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MusicCrossfade.cs

### MusicCrossfade — XFADEPLAN_01 (line 5)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
XFADEPLAN_01 — ONE DEFINITION OF THE SONG-TO-SONG CROSSFADE (02 AUD-MUSICFADE).

The export has always overlapped consecutive songs: the incoming song starts
`IncomingOverlapSec` before the outgoing one ends and fades in across that overlap,
while the outgoing song fades out over its last `OutgoingFadeSec`. Those two numbers
used to be private to `AudioFilterChain`, so nothing else could lay songs out the way
the export plays them. The music plan (`MusicBedPlan`), the Add Music wizard's song
lane and both live previews now read the SAME numbers and the SAME overlap formula from here.

⚠️ `Overlap` is the export's formula, verbatim: the overlap is the incoming window,
capped at half of the shorter of the two segments. Change it here or nowhere.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### MusicCrossfade.OutgoingFadeSec (line 22)

Song-to-song: the OUTGOING song starts fading out this long before its end.

### MusicCrossfade.IncomingOverlapSec (line 25)

Song-to-song: the INCOMING song starts this long before the outgoing song ends.

### MusicCrossfade.Overlap (line 28)

How long segment `currentDurationSec` is heard UNDER the end of the segment
before it (`previousDurationSec`). Zero for the first segment of a bed.

### MusicCrossfade.OutgoingFade (line 35)

The outgoing fade length the export applies to a segment that is followed by another one:
`OutgoingFadeSec`, capped at half of what the segment actually plays
(its own duration plus the lead it was given under the previous song).

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MusicEnergyAnalysis.cs

### MusicEnergyAnalysis — PCMSTREAM_03 (line 12)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
PCMSTREAM_03 — THE ADD MUSIC WIZARD'S ENERGY / BEAT ANALYSIS, STREAMED (AUD-DIALOGS).

Moved out of `MusicWizardWindow.AnalyzeAudioEnergyAsync`, which read the song's entire decoded
stdout into a `MemoryStream`, then `ToArray()`-copied it before looking at a single
sample — memory grew with the song's length, twice. The maths is unchanged, bit for bit:
1,000 Hz mono PCM16; 50 ms buckets of 50 samples (the last bucket averages only the samples it has);
bucket energy = mean of |s| / 32768; normalised by the max (when > 1e-6); beat threshold
`max(mean + 0.65·σ, 0.28)`; local maxima from index 2 to n-3; minimum spacing
`round(0.28 / 0.05)` buckets where the louder of two close peaks wins; peak time = index × 0.05 s.
Only the per-bucket energies (20 per second of audio) are retained.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### MusicEnergyAnalysis.EnergyAccumulator (line 35)

Incremental bucket averager — keeps one running sum, never the samples.

### MusicEnergyAnalysis.EnergyAccumulator.Finish (line 62)

The per-bucket mean energies, including a final partial bucket.

### MusicEnergyAnalysis.Compute (line 75)

Normalisation and beat picking over raw bucket energies. Null when there were no samples.

### MusicEnergyAnalysis.AnalyzeStreamAsync (line 119)

Streams a 1 kHz PCM16LE stream through the accumulator (bounded memory) and analyses it.

### MusicEnergyAnalysis.BuildStartInfo (line 127)

The ffmpeg invocation: whole file, 1 kHz mono s16le on stdout.

### MusicEnergyAnalysis.AnalyzeFileAsync (line 147)

Runs ffmpeg and analyses its stdout as it streams. Null when ffmpeg could not start, exited
non-zero, or produced no samples. Cancellation kills and reaps the child, then throws.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MusicPadAlignment.cs

### MusicPadAlignment — MUSICPAD_01 (line 6)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
MUSICPAD_01 — THE MUSIC LANDS WHERE THE PINK NOTES SAY, EVEN WITH FADES ON.

The Main App places the music in OUTPUT seconds measured from MARK START (OutputTimeline with the
trim start as origin — the same clock the preview plays). But the exported body does NOT start at
MARK START: with fades on, ProcessWorker extracts up to 1 s of footage BEFORE it (the fade-in pad,
`padStartHumanSec`) and the music is mixed into that padded body. Every track therefore
started — and ended — exactly one pad EARLY in the file compared to the preview (measured: onset
1.923 s instead of 2.918 s on a 3–9 s trim at 1.1x). Voice-over takes were already measured from
the padded start; music was not.

`Align` moves every track by the lead pad, so the song meets the video on exactly the
frames the preview showed. Two edge refinements keep "music from the beginning / to the end" full:
  • LEAD — the first track starts on MARK START and the lead fade is on: it pre-rolls into the
    fade-in pad from earlier in the song (as far as the song has room before its start point),
    so the song is still in sync with the video AND fills the fade-in.
  • TAIL — the bed reaches MARK END and the tail fade is on: the last track runs on through the
    fade-out pad (its own fade-out then ends with the picture).
Pure; unit-tested.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### MusicPadAlignment.Align (line 32)

**tracks:** Tracks as the UI built them: delays in output seconds from MARK START.
**padStartSec:** Output seconds of the fade-in pad in front of MARK START (0 = none).
**padEndSec:** Output seconds of the fade-out pad after MARK END (0 = none).
**bodySec:** Output seconds of the padded body (pads included).

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/MusicPlaylist.cs

### MusicPlaylistEntry — PLAYLIST_01 (line 6)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
PLAYLIST_01 — THE ADD MUSIC WIZARD'S SONG LIST, AS PLAIN NUMBERS.

The wizard used to hold ONE song plus an "auto-fill queue" of whole files that always played
from 0:00 to their very last sample. Users could not say where the second song should start or
stop, so silent intros and outros became dead air in the middle of the video, and they could not
see which song played over which clip. Every song is now an entry with its own window
(`InSec` → `OutSec`) and the whole list is laid out by the same plan the
export uses (`MusicBedPlan.Build(IReadOnlyList{MusicBedTrack}, double, bool, double, bool)`).

`UserTrimmed` records that the user moved an edge by hand, so a late-arriving
silence analysis never overwrites a deliberate choice.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/Native/LibAvFrameDecoder.cs

### LibAvFrameDecoder — LIBAVFRAME_01, LIBAVPROBE_01 (line 7)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
LIBAVFRAME_01 — ffmpeg's `-ss T -i FILE -frames:v 1 [-vf scale=W:-2]`, without the process.

Every step mirrors fftools at 1e5c65f539 (the bundled N-119166 build), so the SAME frame comes out:
  1. OPEN — the shared LibAvMediaBackend.OpenInputBlocking (scan_all_pmts=1, interrupt callback,
     find_stream_info). Layout guards as LIBAVPROBE_01, plus AVFrame/AVPacket default guards.
  2. SEEK (ffmpeg_demux.c ifile_open) — timestamp = T + ic->start_time; when the demuxer lacks
     AVFMT_SEEK_TO_PTS and any stream has video_delay, seek 3/23 s earlier (the dts heuristic);
     avformat_seek_file(ic, -1, INT64_MIN, ts, ts, 0). A failed seek is ignored, as ffmpeg does.
  3. STREAM (ffmpeg_mux_init.c map_auto_video) — score = w·h + 1e8·NEW_PACKETS + 5e6·DEFAULT,
     attached pictures score 1; highest wins, first on ties. All other streams → AVDISCARD_ALL
     (ffmpeg discards unused inputs the same way, after the seek).
  4. DECODE (ffmpeg_dec.c) — avcodec_find_decoder(codec_id), threads=auto, pkt_timebase =
     stream time_base; flushed after the seek; send/receive until a frame is SELECTED.
  5. SELECT (ffmpeg_demux ts_offset + ffmpeg_filter insert_trim + trim.c) — frame pts is
     best_effort_timestamp; ffmpeg shifts every packet by av_rescale_q(-timestamp, 1/1e6, tb)
     and its trim filter (start 0) keeps the FIRST frame with shifted pts ≥ 0. So: the first
     decoded frame with best_effort + offset ≥ 0. Real timestamps and time_base only — never a
     nominal frame rate. End of stream before such a frame → NoFrame (ffmpeg writes nothing).
  6. ORIENT (ffmpeg_filter.c autorotate) — the frame's DISPLAYMATRIX side data gives transpose
     clock / cclock / *_flip, hflip+vflip, or a lone vflip, exactly as ffmpeg chooses them; any
     other angle needs the rotate filter → MediaError (subprocess).
  7. SCALE (vf_scale.c + scale_eval.c) — out size from the ORIENTED size: W wide, height
     av_rescale(W, h, w·2)·2 for -2. ONE sws_scale_frame from the decoded format straight to rgb24 (ffmpeg's png format; widened to BGRA)
     at that size (swscale defaults = bicubic; interlaced flag cleared and chroma location
     reset exactly as vf_scale's defaults do), so an 8K source never becomes an 8K managed buffer.
     The 90° transposes are applied to the small BGRA result in managed code.

Deliberately NOT reproduced (→ MediaError → ffmpeg subprocess, so the outcome is unchanged):
attached-picture streams, container-level cropping (AV_PKT_DATA_FRAME_CROPPING), non-90°
rotation, frames without any timestamp. Not handled at all (same as most players): MPEG-TS
timestamp wrap/discontinuity correction.

⚠️ OWNERSHIP — ALL ON ONE POOL THREAD, ALL DETERMINISTIC, REVERSE ORDER OF CREATION:
SwsContextHandle → dst FrameHandle → decode FrameHandle → PacketHandle → CodecContextHandle →
FormatContextHandle → CancellationTokenRegistration → InterruptStateHandle (LAST, after the
context that points at it is closed). Each SafeHandle is created only from a non-NULL pointer
and frees with the matching libav *_free(&p). The pixels are COPIED into a managed array before
any handle is released; nothing native escapes this method.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### LibAv — LIBAVPROBE_02 (line 186)

LIBAVPROBE_02 — libav entry points as UNMANAGED FUNCTION POINTERS bound with

loaded by absolute path.

⚠️ WHY NOT [LibraryImport]. The first cut used source-generated [LibraryImport] plus
`NativeLibrary.SetDllImportResolver`. .NET allows ONE resolver per assembly, and
FreeVideoStudio.Core already has another legitimate owner of that slot: the libmpv P/Invokes
(MediaPipelineChecks registers a resolver for `MpvWrapper`'s assembly to point libmpv-2.dll at
binaries\). Whoever registers second throws, so the two features could not coexist — the
harness proved it ("A resolver is already set for the assembly"). Function pointers need no
resolver at all, cannot bind a DLL found through PATH or the app directory (an unbound pointer is
null, never "searched for"), and are fully NativeAOT-safe: no marshalling stubs, no
Marshal.GetDelegateForFunctionPointer, no reflection, no runtime code generation.

Strings in: converted to NUL-terminated UTF-8 here (libav's file protocol converts UTF-8 to
UTF-16 on Windows). Strings out (`const char*` owned by libav) are returned as
`byte*` and copied with `Utf8`; they are never freed.

The 21 imported symbols (all verified present in the bundled DLL export tables).

Binds every entry point from the given module handles. Throws

(all-or-nothing), so a partially bound table can never be called.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/Native/LibAvLibrary.cs

### LibAvLibrary — LIBAVPROBE_02 (line 6)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
LIBAVPROBE_02 — DETERMINISTIC LIBAV LOADING. NEVER THE USER'S PATH.

The libraries are loaded ONCE per process, by ABSOLUTE PATH, from the first directory that
holds all four of avutil-60, swresample-6, avcodec-62 and avformat-62:
  1. the directory of the ffprobe.exe the caller resolved (FFM-BINPATH already chose it, so the
     native probe reads media with exactly the libraries ffprobe would have used);
  2. `backend\`, `..\..\..\..\..\backend\`, `binaries\`, `..\..\..\..\..\binaries\`
     relative to the exe (the FFM-BINPATH order: installed layout first, dev tree second).

⚠️ DEPENDENTS. Windows resolves avformat's imports (avcodec-62.dll, avutil-60.dll) by MODULE NAME,
and a name that is already loaded in the process wins over any search path. Loading in strict
dependency order — avutil → swresample → avcodec → avformat — therefore makes every dependent
bind to the copy we chose, regardless of PATH, the current directory or SetDllDirectory.

The entry points in `LibAv` are bound with NativeLibrary.GetExport against THOSE
handles (no DllImportResolver — see LibAv for why). No libav entry point is called before
`TryEnsureLoaded` succeeded. A failure is LATCHED: the process does
not retry a broken install on every probe; MediaMetadataProbe uses ffprobe instead.
The libraries are never unloaded (process lifetime), which also means a running app holds
backend\*.dll open — the installer/upgrader already requires the app to be closed.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### LibAvLibrary.SwScale — LIBAVFRAME_01 (line 42)

LIBAVFRAME_01 — frame tier only. Imports avutil-60 alone (PE import table), so it binds to the copy already loaded.

### LibAvLibrary.LoadOrder (line 45)

Strict dependency order (verified from each DLL's PE import table).

### LibAvLibrary.TryEnsureLoaded (line 53)

Loads (once) and verifies the libraries. Never throws.

### LibAvLibrary.TryEnsureFrameTierLoaded (line 146)

Loads (once) the metadata tier and then the frame tier. Never throws.

### INativeMediaBackend — LIBAVPROBE_01 (line 22)

LIBAVPROBE_01 — narrow in-process metadata seam. Callers get an ffprobe-shaped
`JsonObject` (the subset of `-show_format -show_streams` that the product
actually reads) and never see a pointer.

### INativeMediaBackend.ProbeAsync (line 29)

Probes `mediaPath`. Throws `OperationCanceledException` when
`cancellationToken` fires or `timeout` elapses (the latter
with no token, mirroring AsyncProcessRunner's timeout). Never throws for media errors.

### LibAvMediaBackend — LIBAVPROBE_01 (line 37)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
LIBAVPROBE_01 — ffprobe's METADATA, without the process.

Mirrors `fftools/ffprobe.c` at 1e5c65f539 for the fields MediaProber / OutputSizeEstimator
consume: avformat_alloc_context → avformat_open_input (format_opts scan_all_pmts=1, exactly as
ffprobe sets it) → avformat_find_stream_info(NULL) → read AVFormatContext / AVStream /
AVCodecParameters. Output keys and formatting follow ffprobe's JSON writer:
`format.duration` "%f" seconds (omitted when AV_NOPTS_VALUE), `format.bit_rate` (omitted
when ≤ 0), `format.tags`; per stream, in container order (NO selection here — every
consumer keeps its own existing "first video / first audio" rule): `index`,
`codec_type`, for video `width`/`height`/`pix_fmt`/`color_range`/
`color_space`/`color_transfer`/`color_primaries` (colour keys omitted when
UNSPECIFIED, as ffprobe's optional-field rule does), `avg_frame_rate` "num/den",
`duration`, `bit_rate`. Attached pictures (MP3 cover art) remain video streams,
because that is what ffprobe reports and what every existing consumer already sees.

⚠️ OWNERSHIP, ALL ON ONE THREAD, ALL DETERMINISTIC:
  • InterruptStateHandle (native block read by the [UnmanagedCallersOnly] callback) — disposed LAST.
  • CancellationTokenRegistration — disposed before the state it writes to (Dispose waits for a
    running callback).
  • AVDictionary options — freed with av_dict_free in a finally, success or failure.
  • AVFormatContext — before open: freed with avformat_free_context if open is never reached;
    open FAILED: libav already freed it and nulled the pointer, nothing to do; open SUCCEEDED:
    owned by FormatContextHandle → avformat_close_input(&p).
No pointer is stored in a field, returned, or captured by a lambda that outlives the call.

⚠️ INTERRUPTION. AVIOInterruptCB is installed BEFORE avformat_open_input, so every blocking read
inside open and find_stream_info polls it. It aborts on the caller's token or on the
deadline; libav then returns AVERROR_EXIT and the probe throws OperationCanceledException. A probe
cannot outlive its timeout by more than one libav I/O step.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### LibAvMediaBackend.TryInitialize (line 82)

True when the libraries loaded and passed the version guard (loads them on first call).

### LibAvMediaBackend.TryInitializeFrameTier — LIBAVFRAME_01 (line 93)

LIBAVFRAME_01 — true when the metadata tier AND the frame tier (swscale-9) are usable.

### LibAvMediaBackend.OpenInputBlocking — LIBAVPROBE_01, LIBAVFRAME_01 (line 137)

LIBAVPROBE_01 / LIBAVFRAME_01 — the ONE open sequence, shared by the metadata probe and the
single-frame decoder: avformat_alloc_context → interrupt callback installed → open_input
(scan_all_pmts=1, as both ffprobe and ffmpeg set it) → find_stream_info(NULL).
Returns the opened context (caller disposes), or null with `error` set when
libav rejected the input. Throws OperationCanceledException when the interrupt fired.
Ownership is exactly the pre-existing rule: alloc'd-but-never-opened → avformat_free_context;
open failed → libav already freed it; opened → FormatContextHandle.
