## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/OutputFileNaming.cs

### OutputFileNaming — OUTNAME_01, SETNAMEONE_01, RESCUE_01 (line 8)

OUTNAME_01 — the user-editable automatic file name of every finished export.

Each sub-application owns ONE base name, edited in Settings › Output Files and stored in
`AppSettings.MainOutputBaseName` (the Video Merger adds "-merged", SETNAMEONE_01). A finished
render is saved as `<base>-<N>.mp4` where N is the first free index in the
destination folder (1, 2, 3 …). Defaults: `MainDefaultBaseName` →
`FreeVideoStudio-1.mp4`, `MergerDefaultBaseName` → `FreeVideoStudio-merged-1.mp4`.
SETNAMEONE_01: there is ONE name; the Video Merger adds `MergerSuffix` to it
(`MergerBaseFor`), so the two tools' files are told apart without a second setting.

⚠️ The base name is USER INPUT that ends up in a file path. `Sanitize` is the only
gate: it strips path separators and every character Windows forbids in a file name, removes
trailing dots/spaces/dashes (Windows silently drops trailing dots and spaces, which would make
two different settings collide on disk), rejects reserved device names (CON, NUL, COM1 …) and
clamps the length. Anything that sanitizes to nothing falls back to the tool's default, so an
export can never fail — or escape its folder — because of this setting.

Rescued renders (RESCUE_01) deliberately do NOT follow the custom name: their fixed
`MainRecoveredPrefix` / `MergerRecoveredPrefix` are how the user and the
crash digest tell the two tools' preserved files apart in the shared temp root.

### OutputFileNaming.MainDefaultBaseName — OUTNAME_01 (line 38)

OUTNAME_01 — Main App default: FreeVideoStudio-1.mp4, FreeVideoStudio-2.mp4 …

### OutputFileNaming.MergerSuffix — SETNAMEONE_01 (line 41)

SETNAMEONE_01 — what the Video Merger adds to the one file name.

### OutputFileNaming.MergerDefaultBaseName — OUTNAME_01, SETNAMEONE_01 (line 44)

OUTNAME_01 / SETNAMEONE_01 — Video Merger default: FreeVideoStudio-merged-1.mp4 …

### OutputFileNaming.MergerBaseFor — SETNAMEONE_01 (line 47)

SETNAMEONE_01 — the Video Merger's file-name base for the user's one name: the cleaned name plus
`MergerSuffix` (kept within `MaxBaseNameLength`). "Clips" → "Clips-merged".

### OutputFileNaming.MainRecoveredPrefix — RESCUE_01 (line 59)

RESCUE_01 — fixed prefix of a rescued Main App render in the temp root.

### OutputFileNaming.MergerRecoveredPrefix — RESCUE_01 (line 62)

RESCUE_01 — fixed prefix of a rescued Video Merger render in the temp root.

### OutputFileNaming.MaxBaseNameLength (line 65)

Upper bound for the base name, leaving room for "-10000.mp4" inside MAX_PATH budgets.

### OutputFileNaming.Sanitize — OUTNAME_01 (line 81)

OUTNAME_01 — returns a base name that is always safe to use as a Windows file-name prefix.
Never throws; returns `fallback` when nothing usable is left.

### OutputFileNaming.NumberedFileName — OUTNAME_01 (line 108)

OUTNAME_01 — "<base>-<index>.mp4".

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/OutputFileSize.cs

### OutputFileSize.MergerQualityRatio — MERGEQUALITY_01 (line 16)

MERGEQUALITY_01 (Video-Merger-Migration.md P11) — share of the 100% bitrate a quality below 100%
targets: 2^((15 − CQ)/6), the constant-quality curve (≈0.79 at 95%, ≈0.28 at 50%, ≈0.10 at 5%).
Below 100% the Merger now ENCODES to this bitrate (VBR) instead of an uncapped CQ, and the size
estimate uses the same number, so a lower setting can never produce a bigger file than 100%.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/OutputTimeline.cs

### OutputTimeline — TIME_01 (line 8)

TIME_01 — THE SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH FOR "HOW LONG IS THE FINISHED VIDEO, AND WHICH SOURCE
FRAME IS PLAYING AT A GIVEN MOMENT OF IT".

Before this type existed the same mathematics was written by hand in THREE places:

  - `GranularSpeedBuilder.CreateTimeMapper` — source to output.
  - `GranularSpeedBuilder.Build`'s local `TimeMapper` — source to output, again.
  - `MusicWizardWindow.MapPhase3OutputToSourceRelativeSeconds` — output to source.

They drifted, exactly as duplicated maths always does. The Music Wizard's copy treated a freeze
as REPLACING the footage underneath it (`cursor = Math.Max(cursor, segEnd)`), while the
export treats a freeze as an INSERTION that holds a frame and then carries on playing from the
same spot (`sourceCursor = fStart`). Measured consequence, with the export as ground truth:

```csharp

scenario                    EXPORT truth   Wizard said       ERROR
no segments                     60.000s      60.000s     +0.000s
slow-mo 10-20s @0.5x            70.000s      70.000s     +0.000s
FREEZE 5-8s (3s)                63.000s      60.000s     -3.000s
FREEZE 1.5s @20s                61.500s      60.000s     -1.500s
two freezes                     63.000s      60.000s     -3.000s
freeze + slow-mo                73.000s      70.000s     -3.000s
base 2x + freeze                33.000s      31.500s     -1.500s

```

The export behaviour is the correct one and is what this type implements: a 1.5 second freeze
makes the finished video 1.5 seconds longer, and nothing is skipped.

TIME UNITS AND FRAMES OF REFERENCE — read before touching anything here:

  - **Absolute source seconds** — a position in the ORIGINAL file, including
  any trim offset. `SourceToOutput` takes these.
  - **Clip-relative source seconds** — measured from the trim-in point, so
  the clip always starts at 0. `OutputToSourceRelative` returns these.
  - **Output seconds** — a position in the FINISHED video, after speed
  changes and freezes have stretched it.

This asymmetry is not a design choice, it is the contract the six existing callers already rely
on, preserved deliberately so this refactor changes no behaviour outside the Music Wizard.

Phase 3 of MEME_TIMELINE_PLAN.txt will add meme insertions to this same chunk list. That is the
entire reason the model is shaped around a chunk list rather than a closed-form formula.

### OutputTimeline.Chunk (line 60)

One continuous stretch of the finished video.

A normal chunk plays source footage from `SourceStartSec` to
`SourceEndSec` at `Speed`. A freeze chunk holds the single frame at
`SourceStartSec` for `FreezeHoldSec` and consumes NO source time —
which is why its `SourceEndSec` is only a hair past its start.

### OutputTimeline.Chunk.IsFreeze (line 78)

A held frame from the source. Occupies output time, consumes no source time.

### OutputTimeline.Chunk.IsInsertion — MEME_04 (line 81)

MEME_04 — FOREIGN content spliced in (a meme). Like a freeze it occupies output time and
consumes no source time, but unlike a freeze the picture is NOT from the source file, so
the preview cannot show it by seeking mpv.

### OutputTimeline.Chunk.HoldsSource (line 88)

Either kind of block that adds output time without advancing the source.

### OutputTimeline.Chunk.OutputLengthSec (line 91)

How many seconds of FINISHED video this chunk occupies.

### OutputTimeline.Cut (line 96)

CUT_01 — a stretch of footage the user deleted from the middle of the clip.
CLIP-RELATIVE seconds, like `Insertion.AtSourceSec`.

### OutputTimeline.CutMergeGapSec (line 105)

CUT_01 — two cuts closer than this are merged into one. Below roughly a third of a second
the gap between them is not a piece of video anyone meant to keep, and each surviving
sliver would cost a whole parallel branch in the export graph
(see GranularSpeedBuilder.HighChunkCountWarnThreshold).

### OutputTimeline.MinCutLengthSec (line 113)

CUT_01 — a cut shorter than this is discarded as a mis-click / sub-frame drag.

### OutputTimeline.Insertion — MEME_04 (line 116)

MEME_04 — a meme spliced into the middle of the video. `AtSourceSec` is
CLIP-RELATIVE and must already be snapped (see `SnapInsertionPoint`).

### OutputTimeline.TotalSourceSeconds (line 127)

Length of the trimmed source clip, in seconds. Speed and freezes do not affect it.

### OutputTimeline.OriginSec (line 130)

ABSOLUTE source second that clip-relative 0 corresponds to (the trim start).

### OutputTimeline.DescribeSourceRange — AISPEEDMAP_01 (line 133)

AISPEEDMAP_01 — how the ABSOLUTE source range [`absStartSec`, `absEndSec`]
plays in the finished video: every overlapping normal chunk (with its speed), freeze and cut, in
ABSOLUTE source seconds. AI features read this so their timing is judged as the viewer sees it.

### OutputTimeline.PlaybackSpan — AISPEEDMAP_01 (line 159)

AISPEEDMAP_01 — one stretch of source and how it plays. `Speed` 0 with
`FreezeHoldSec` > 0 is a held frame; `IsCut` means it is not in the video.

### OutputTimeline.TotalOutputSeconds (line 163)

Length of the FINISHED video in seconds, with every speed change and freeze applied.
This is the number every ruler in the application should be drawn against.

### OutputTimeline.Create (line 183)

Builds the timeline. The chunk construction below is a faithful move of the logic that was
in `GranularSpeedBuilder.CreateTimeMapper`; it is deliberately NOT "tidied up", because
its exact ordering and its 0.001 epsilons are what make it agree with the exported graph.

### OutputTimeline.NormalizeCuts (line 344)

CUT_01 — clamp, order, merge and de-sliver a raw cut list.

Merging is not cosmetic. Two cuts separated by a 0.1s sliver would leave that sliver as its
own chunk, which costs a whole parallel branch in the export graph and shows up as a
one-frame flash nobody wanted. Overlapping cuts must merge for a harder reason: the chunk
walk inside `Create` assumes holes never overlap, and two overlapping
holes would emit chunks out of source order and corrupt every mapping built on them.

Public and static so the UI can run the SAME normalisation while the user is still dragging,
and show them exactly the cuts the export will make.

### OutputTimeline.ToClipRelative (line 393)

Clamps an ABSOLUTE source position into clip-relative seconds.

### OutputTimeline.SourceToOutput (line 400)

ABSOLUTE source seconds -> FINISHED-VIDEO seconds.
Byte-for-byte the behaviour of the old `CreateTimeMapper` lambda.

### OutputTimeline.OutputToSourceRelative (line 435)

FINISHED-VIDEO seconds -> CLIP-RELATIVE source seconds. The exact inverse of
`SourceToOutput` outside freezes.

DELIBERATELY LOSSY INSIDE A FREEZE, AND THAT IS CORRECT. While a frame is held, every moment
of finished video maps to the SAME source instant, so this returns that instant. Callers that
need to know whether the playhead is sitting inside a held frame must ask
`IsHoldingFrameAt` rather than trying to detect it from the returned value.

CUT_01 — AMBIGUOUS AT A JOIN, BY THE SAME LOGIC. One instant of finished video sits at both
the last frame before a cut and the first frame after it; two source positions map to it and
the inverse has to pick one. It picks the EARLIER side, matching the freeze convention above.
That is wrong for a player, which wants to seek and keep rolling forward, so the preview must
compose the two calls:

```csharp
NextSurvivingSource(OutputToSourceRelative(t))
```

which resolves a join to the resuming side and can never return deleted footage. Do not
"fix" the boundary here instead — the freeze and normal-chunk cases share this loop.

### OutputTimeline.IsHoldingFrameAt (line 478)

True when the finished video is holding a still frame at this moment. The preview uses this
to keep its own clock running while mpv sits on one frame, instead of assuming playback
stalled.

### OutputTimeline.InsertionAt — MEME_04 (line 500)

MEME_04 — the Id of the meme playing at this moment of the finished video, or null if the
gameplay itself is on screen. The preview needs this because a meme is NOT in the source
file: seeking mpv cannot show it, so the UI must draw a marker instead.

### OutputTimeline.SnapInsertionPoint — MEME_04 (line 522)

MEME_04 / D8 — moves a requested insertion point FORWARD to the end of any freeze or speed
segment it lands inside, so a meme never interrupts one. The UI must draw its marker at the
returned value, not at the raw click, or the meme lands somewhere the user never saw.

### OutputTimeline.HasInsertionAtSource — MEME_04 (line 543)

MEME_04 / D7 — true when a meme already occupies this snapped point. Two memes may not share
one insertion point; the caller blocks the placement and tells the user.

### OutputTimeline.Cuts (line 555)

The normalised cuts this timeline was built with, clip-relative and non-overlapping.

### OutputTimeline.HasCuts (line 558)

True when this clip has any deleted footage at all. The cheap early-out.

### OutputTimeline.SurvivingSourceSeconds (line 561)

CUT_01 — seconds of source footage that actually survive to the finished video.
`TotalSourceSeconds` deliberately still reports the FULL trimmed clip, because
every existing caller uses it as the domain of source time; this is the separate question
"how much of it is left", which is what a duration readout wants.

### OutputTimeline.RemovedSourceSeconds (line 577)

Total seconds removed by cuts.

### OutputTimeline.IsCutAtSource (line 580)

True when this CLIP-RELATIVE source moment was deleted. The boundaries are deliberately
asymmetric — the start belongs to the cut, the end belongs to the surviving footage — so
that `NextSurvivingSource` is idempotent and cannot loop.

### OutputTimeline.NextSurvivingSource (line 592)

CUT_01 — the first surviving source moment at or after `sourceRelSec`.

This is what the preview seeks to when playback reaches deleted footage: the player never
decodes a removed frame, it jumps the hole in one seek. Cuts are non-overlapping and ordered
after normalisation, so one forward pass is enough and the result can never fall inside
another cut.

### OutputTimeline.CutMarkerOutputPositions (line 612)

CUT_01 — where each cut sits in FINISHED-VIDEO seconds, for drawing the scissors markers.
Every cut collapses to a single instant on the output ruler, which is precisely why a cut
must be drawn as a fixed-width marker and can never be a drag-resizable block.

### OutputTimeline.InsertionOutputRanges — MEME_05 (line 624)

MEME_05 — where each meme sits on the FINISHED-VIDEO ruler: its id, and the output second it
starts and ends on.

THE EXACT MIRROR OF `CutMarkerOutputPositions`, and worth stating why. A cut is
zero output seconds wide, so it can only ever be a fixed-width glyph. An insertion is the
opposite: it occupies zero SOURCE seconds but real OUTPUT seconds, so on this ruler — and
only on this ruler — it is a genuine block with a left edge, a right edge and a middle you
can grab. That asymmetry is the whole reason memes are placed in the Speed Editor (output
time) while cuts are marked on the Main App (source time): each feature is drawn where it
actually has a shape.

⚠️ Do NOT compute these edges as "anchor, anchor + DurationSec" in output time by hand.
The accumulation below walks every preceding chunk at its own speed, which is the only
thing that stays correct when a 2x segment sits between the clip start and the meme.

### OutputTimeline.FreezeOutputRanges — FREEZESFX_01 (line 642)

FREEZESFX_01 — where each freeze (held frame) plays in the finished body, in output seconds.
The export dips the music across these windows under the freeze's slow-motion sound.

### OutputTimeline.ExtraChunkCost (line 669)

CUT_01 — how many extra export chunks these cuts cost, for the pre-export RAM warning.
A cut lands inside a stretch of footage and splits it in two, so it adds ONE branch — half
what a speed segment costs (which adds itself plus the gap after it). A cut touching the
very start or end of the clip splits nothing and is free.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/PcmStream.cs

### IPcmSampleSink (line 17)

Receives decoded signed 16-bit mono samples, one bounded chunk at a time.

### PcmStream — PCMSTREAM_01 (line 23)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
PCMSTREAM_01 — DECODED AUDIO IS STREAMED THROUGH ONE FIXED BUFFER, NEVER ACCUMULATED.

The waveform lane and the Music Wizard used to read ffmpeg's whole s16le stdout into an unbounded
`MemoryStream` and then copy it AGAIN into a `short[]` / `byte[]`, so memory grew
with the decoded duration (two or three copies alive at once). Here stdout is read in
`BufferBytes` chunks from `ArrayPool{T}`, a split sample (odd byte at the end of
one read) is carried into the next read, and each chunk is handed to an incremental
`IPcmSampleSink`. A trailing odd byte at EOF is dropped — exactly what the old
`length / 2` did. Memory = the buffer + whatever compact result the sink keeps.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### PcmStream.BufferBytes — PCMSTREAM_01 (line 38)

PCMSTREAM_01 — the one read buffer (bytes).

### PcmStream.PumpAsync (line 41)

Reads Int16LE samples from `source` until EOF and feeds `sink`.
Returns the number of whole samples delivered.

### PcmScratch — PCMSTREAM_01 (line 96)

PCMSTREAM_01 — bounded scratch for the waveform's SECOND pass. The exact bucket mapping of
`WaveformPeaks.Reduce` (`bucket = i * count / N`) needs the FINAL sample count N,
which is only known at EOF (a short source ends early). Samples are kept in memory up to
`MemoryCapBytes` and spill to a delete-on-close temp file beyond that, so memory stays
constant however long the decoded audio is, and the result is bit-identical to the one-shot reduce.

### PcmScratch.ReplayAsync (line 137)

Second pass: replays every stored sample, in order, through `sink`.

### PeakBucketReducer — PCMSTREAM_01 (line 179)

PCMSTREAM_01 — the incremental twin of `WaveformPeaks.Reduce` (N known up front).

### FfmpegPcmPipe — PCMSTREAM_02 (line 207)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
PCMSTREAM_02 — ONE FFMPEG-TO-PCM CHILD, ALWAYS REAPED.

stdout is handed to a streaming consumer; stderr is drained CONCURRENTLY (a child that fills its
stderr pipe blocks, and then stdout never reaches EOF) into a bounded tail kept for diagnostics.
Cancellation, a consumer failure or a malformed stream kill the whole process tree, and the child is
waited for (bounded) before the method returns, so no ffmpeg outlives the request that started it.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### FfmpegPcmPipe.StderrTailBytes (line 219)

How much of stderr is kept (the tail) for logs.

### FfmpegPcmPipe.ReapTimeout (line 222)

How long the reap may wait for the killed child to exit.

### FfmpegPcmPipe.RunAsync (line 227)

Starts `psi`, runs `consumeStdout` on its stdout and waits for exit.
Throws `OperationCanceledException` on cancellation and rethrows a consumer failure —
in every case only AFTER the child was killed and reaped.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/PeakSafety.cs

### PeakSafety — PEAKSAFE_01 (line 7)

PEAKSAFE_01 — the two stages that protect a viewer's ears, shared by the Main App export, the
Video Merger export and the live preview so all three apply the SAME filters.

**1. THE PEAK TAMER (user switch: Settings › "Sudden loud moments").** A fast peak
compressor on the GAMEPLAY bus whose threshold sits `TamerHeadroomLu` above the
clip's own measured integrated loudness. The body of the gameplay never reaches it; only a
sudden burst far above the clip's average (an explosion, a scream) is pulled down. It runs on
the gameplay alone — never the summed mix — because a threshold derived from the GAMEPLAY's
loudness would otherwise squash a music bed that is simply mixed louder than the game.

**2. THE SAFETY LIMITER (always on, no switch).** A true-peak brick wall on the final
mix at `SafetyCeilingDbtp`. It is 4x oversampled so it catches inter-sample peaks
(plain `alimiter` only sees sample peaks), and it carries `level=disabled`.

⚠️ `level=disabled` IS LOAD-BEARING. `alimiter`'s `level` (auto-level)
defaults to ON and rescales the output by 1/limit after limiting: the old
`alimiter=limit=-1.5dB` chain measured +1.5 dB LOUDER on everything with peaks back at
0.0 dBFS (+1.0 dBTP). A limiter must never raise the level.

### PeakSafety.TamerHeadroomLu (line 29)

How far above the measured integrated loudness the tamer starts to act, in LU.

### PeakSafety.TamerMinThresholdDb (line 32)

The tamer threshold is clamped into this window (dBFS).

### PeakSafety.SafetyCeilingDbtp (line 40)

The always-on true-peak ceiling of every export, in dBTP.

### PeakSafety.SafetyLimiterLimitDb (line 43)

The sample-peak limit handed to the oversampled `alimiter`. Measured: -2.3 dB at 4x
oversampling lands true peaks at -2.1 dBTP on hot pink noise + an 8 kHz tone, i.e. at or
under `SafetyCeilingDbtp`; -2.0 dB landed at -1.8 dBTP.

### PeakSafety.TamerThresholdDb (line 52)

The tamer's threshold for a clip measured at `integratedLufs`.

### PeakSafety.TamerFilter (line 56)

The tamer filter (no leading comma, no labels), or null when the loudness is unknown —
a threshold cannot be placed relative to a level nobody measured.

### PeakSafety.SafetyLimiterFilter — AVSYNC_03 (line 71)

The always-on true-peak safety limiter (no leading comma, no labels).
AVSYNC_03 — `latency=1` IS LOAD-BEARING. `alimiter` looks ahead by its attack time
and, without delay compensation, emits every sample that much late: the whole mix trailed the
video by exactly 1.0 ms (measured with the bundled FFmpeg: 1 kHz bursts at n s decoded at
n + 0.0010 s through this chain, n + 0.0000 s with `latency=1`; identical sample count).

PREVIEWFIDELITY_01 — THE PREVIEW SAYS WHEN IT IS NOT THE EXPORT.

The export is authoritative. Where the active preview backend cannot reproduce a MATERIAL export
effect, the preview must not pretend: this evaluator lists each such difference and the window shows
them (a small "PREVIEW ≈ EXPORT" marker with the reasons). Only material differences are listed —
a black band, missing zoom/framing, a different tone map, missing sound. Sub-pixel rounding, the
CAS sharpening of zoomed frames and the live fallback's few seconds before a rendered mix is ready
are not, and are deliberately never shown.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/ProcessWorker.cs

### ProcessWorker._lifetime — PROCGATE_01, PIPELIFE_01 (line 12)

PROCGATE_01 — the currently running child process, and the ONLY field in this class that is
handed between the export thread and the cancelling thread while holding an OS resource.

It used to be a bare, NON-volatile field — note that its two neighbours below were already
marked volatile, and this one, the only one that matters for correctness, was not. Cancel()
tested it for null and then called Kill() on it as two separate reads, so the export thread
could run `_currentProcess = null; proc.Dispose();` in between. Kill() on a disposed Process
throws ObjectDisposedException straight into CoreLogger.Swallowed, and the user's cancel was
silently lost — AFTER the log had already announced "Terminating FFmpeg process tree."

Access is now exclusively through `SetCurrentProcess`, `TakeCurrentProcess`
and `PeekCurrentProcess`, all of which hold the shared FfmpegJobLifetime gate (PIPELIFE_01). The gate is
held for a reference copy only — never across a Kill, a Dispose or any I/O — so it cannot
deadlock against the export thread.

PIPELIFE_01 — process slot, cancel flag, single-flight finish and teardown ladder, shared
with `MergerWorker`. The members below keep their original names and delegate,
so none of this file's ~30 call sites change.

### ProcessWorker._isCanceled — FFMPEGSTOP_01, PIPEDEDUP_01, RESCUE_01, PIPELIFE_01 (line 35)

FFMPEGSTOP_01 — single-flight gate for the cooperative shutdown ladder.

WHAT WAS WRONG. Every termination site in this file called
`Kill(entireProcessTree: true)` as the FIRST and ONLY action, and no
`ProcessStartInfo` here redirected stdin — so FFmpeg's interactive quit
command ('q') had no channel and this pipeline was STRUCTURALLY INCAPABLE of asking the
encoder to stop cleanly. Killing an MP4 muxer mid-write means the `moov` atom is never
emitted: the output is `mdat` payload with no index — right size, right name, and
unplayable in every player. On the failure paths that reach
`TryRescueFinishedRender`, that corrupt file was then moved to
`FreeVideoStudio-RECOVERED-*.mp4` and presented to the user as preserved work.

⚠️ THE PROJECT ALREADY BUILT THE FIX AND THIS FILE NEVER RECEIVED IT.
`GracefulProcessTerminator` states the failure verbatim and is used by
`CrashLogDigest`, `DeploymentLifecycle`, `AsyncProcessRunner`,
`HudAutoDetector` and `MergerWorker`. ProcessWorker — the PRIMARY user-facing
export path — was the only media worker that never referenced it. The two copies of
`ReadExitCodeSafely` are the proof: MergerWorker's routes through the ladder,
this one called Kill raw, from an identical signature. Duplicated logic is how that
divergence happened and went unnoticed.

The gate guarantees exactly ONE ladder ('q' → grace → Kill(tree) → exit confirmation)
ever runs per FFmpeg process, because `Cancel`, the cancellation-token
registrations and `Dispose` can all race each other.

PIPEDEDUP_01 — these three fields and their three methods were written out here AND,
separately, in `MergerWorker`. Two copies of one mechanism is exactly how
`ReadExitCodeSafely` silently diverged between these two files (FFMPEGSTOP_01) and how
`TryRescueFinishedRender` shipped the same race twice (RESCUE_01). One copy now lives
in `CooperativeShutdownGate`; the members below are thin delegations kept at
their original signatures so no call site in this file changes.

PIPELIFE_01 — kept as a private alias over the shared lifetime's flag so every existing
read and write in this file compiles unchanged. Writing `false` is deliberately a no-op:
a cancelled job is never un-cancelled, and nothing in either pipeline ever tried to.

### ProcessWorker._finishEmitted — PIPELIFE_01 (line 78)

PIPELIFE_01 — alias over the shared lifetime's single-flight flag.

### ProcessWorker.TwoPassSlowPass1Fraction (line 93)

T01 — SLOW-route split. Used only when the temp drive cannot hold the scratch master, in
which case the filter graph genuinely runs twice and the two halves cost about the same.

### ProcessWorker.LastReportedSpeed (line 99)

G09 — last `speed=` value FFmpeg reported (e.g. "3.4x"). Instance-scoped rather than a
local so the two-pass stages can report through the same field. "?" until the first
progress line arrives.

### ProcessWorker.EmitProgress (line 110)

Single monotonic progress emitter. The bar can NEVER move backwards (kills the
phase-seam reset-to-zero and the size-retry snap-back), so every consumer sees a
steady forward sweep. Reset per job by construction (a fresh worker per export).

written before multi-meme support still export identically. When it is non-empty it is
authoritative and the legacy fields are ignored.

Strongly typed failure model providing structured category, stage, attempt history,

### ProcessWorker.ResolvedMeme — MEME_05 (line 259)

MEME_05 — ONE MEME, FULLY RESOLVED: probed, levelled, given an FFmpeg input slot, a pair of
filter-graph labels and the exact moment of the rendered stream it cuts into.

⚠️ `CutOutputSec` IS NOT `AtSourceSecRelative` CONVERTED NAIVELY. It
is a position in `[v_render_out]` — the stream that has ALREADY had speed changes,
freezes and the thumbnail intro applied but NOT the memes. Computed in any other clock, in
particular one that already counts earlier memes, every meme after the first drifts later by
the sum of the ones before it. See the block that fills this in.

`SlotIndex` is the position in the final concat: 0 means "ahead of the first
piece of rendered video", `n` means "between piece n-1 and piece n", and the last slot
means "after everything".

MEMEPOS_01 / MEMEANIM_01 / MEMETRANS_01 — presentation extras.

MEME_05 — an FFmpeg filter label may only contain letters, digits and underscores. Meme
placements carry generated ids, but a payload written by hand — or recovered from an older
crash file — could carry anything, and one stray bracket or comma silently corrupts the whole

### ProcessWorker.SetCurrentProcess — PROCGATE_01 (line 352)

PROCGATE_01 — publish the live child process. Export thread only.

### ProcessWorker.TakeCurrentProcess — PROCGATE_01 (line 355)

PROCGATE_01 — claim the live child process AND clear the slot in one atomic step, so exactly
one caller can ever be responsible for disposing it. Every teardown site uses this instead of
the old `_currentProcess = null; proc.Dispose();` pair.

### ProcessWorker.PeekCurrentProcess — PROCGATE_01 (line 362)

PROCGATE_01 — one consistent read for callers that only observe.

### ProcessWorker.BeginCooperativeShutdown — FFMPEGSTOP_01 (line 365)

FFMPEGSTOP_01 — starts the bounded cooperative shutdown ladder for `proc`
exactly once, off the calling thread.

Ported verbatim from `MergerWorker.BeginCooperativeShutdown`, which is the
already-proven shape. Fire-and-forget on purpose: the ladder is strictly bounded
(`CooperativeGraceMs` + `HardKillConfirmMs`) but it DOES wait, and this is called
from `Cancel` (UI thread) and from cancellation-token registrations (which run
synchronously on whichever thread cancels). Neither may block. Faults are observed so a
background stop can never surface as an unobserved task exception.

Single-flight: `Cancel`, the token registrations and `Dispose`
may all race, but only one ladder ever runs per process.

### ProcessWorker.AwaitActiveShutdownAsync — FFMPEGSTOP_01 (line 382)

FFMPEGSTOP_01 — awaits the in-flight shutdown ladder, if any. Bounded by the ladder itself.
Called before `ReadExitCodeSafely` so the exit code is read from a process that
has actually finished finalizing its output, not one still writing its moov atom.

### ProcessWorker.Cancel — PROCGATE_01, FFMPEGSTOP_01 (line 389)

PROCGATE_01 — best-effort cancellation. The authoritative mechanism is the CancellationToken
registrations taken around each child process; this remains the belt-and-braces path, but it
now acts on a SINGLE consistent read of the process reference instead of re-reading the field
between the null test and the Kill.

FFMPEGSTOP_01 — the Kill is now the LAST rung of a ladder rather than the first action. The
call still returns immediately (the ladder runs off-thread), so a cancel can never hang the
UI, but FFmpeg now gets the chance to finalize its container instead of being shot mid-write.

### ProcessWorker.ReadExitCodeSafely — ISSUE_04, FFMPEGSTOP_01, PIPEDEDUP_01 (line 403)

ISSUE_04 — reads a child process's exit code without ever throwing.

Callers reach here after a CANCELLABLE wait, so the process may not have finished dying
yet (Kill is asynchronous). Give it a short grace period, then fall back to a sentinel
rather than letting InvalidOperationException masquerade as a pipeline crash.

FFMPEGSTOP_01 — was a bare WaitForExit -> Kill(tree) -> WaitForExit while the sibling copy
in MergerWorker had already been hardened to the bounded ladder. PIPEDEDUP_01 removed the
second copy so the two can never disagree again; the default stays true here because this
pipeline's cancellable wait can return with the ladder unfinished.

### ProcessWorker.RunAsync (line 417)

Runs the complete rendering pipeline. Returns true on success.
Exact port of ProcessThread.run() logic flow.

### ProcessWorker.ResolveOutputDirectory — ISSUE_04 (line 2389)

ISSUE_04 — where the finished file goes.
`OutputDirectory` is set by the UI layer, which has already validated it and
(if needed) asked the user to pick one. The shell-resolved Downloads folder and the
%USERPROFILE% guess below exist only so a headless/gated code path still produces a file
rather than throwing.

### ProcessWorker.TryRescueFinishedRender — ISSUE_03, RESCUE_01, OUTPATH_01 (line 2414)

ISSUE_03 — moves a COMPLETED render out of the per-job temp folder, which the pipeline's
`finally` block deletes wholesale, into the temp ROOT where it will survive.

This is the safety net for the one moment where the expensive work is already done but the
file is not yet at its destination. Returns the preserved path, or null if even that could
not be managed (in which case there is genuinely nothing left to save).

RESCUE_01 — delegates to `RescuedOutputPath.TryRescue`.

WHAT WAS WRONG: this method picked its destination with a `while (File.Exists(...))`
scan and then called the TWO-argument `File.Move`, which throws when the destination
exists. Two rescues inside the same one-second stamp — cancel-then-restart, or Main App and
Merger together — meant the second one threw, was swallowed, returned null, and the
finished render (the ONLY copy, which is why it is being rescued at all) was abandoned. The
index loop also had no ceiling, so an unwritable temp root spun forever inside a failure
handler. `RescuedOutputPath` carries the OUTPATH_01 reservation primitive this
path always should have used, in ONE copy shared with `MergerWorker`.

⚠️ The prefix and the log tag stay distinct from the Merger's — they are how the user and
the crash digest tell the two tools' rescued files apart.

### ProcessWorker.ResolveOutputPath — OUTPATH_01 (line 2445)

OUTPATH_01 — RESERVES the output name instead of merely testing it.

This was a File.Exists scan: check, then return the name, then write to it much later. Two
pipelines running at once — which is exactly what a cancel-then-restart used to produce —
both saw the same index free and both returned it, so the second File.Move(..., overwrite:
true) silently destroyed the first render. The window is small but the loss is total and
silent, which is the worst combination.

FileMode.CreateNew with FileShare.None is an ATOMIC create-or-fail at the filesystem level:
exactly one caller can win a given name, in this process or any other. The zero-byte
placeholder it leaves is overwritten by the pipeline's own File.Move/File.Copy, which
already pass overwrite: true.

The iteration ceiling exists so a directory that cannot be written to (permissions, a full
disk, an offline network share) fails loudly after a bounded number of attempts instead of
spinning forever inside the export.

### ProcessWorker.FormatForLog — ISSUE_15, PIPEDEDUP_01, ISSUE_07 (line 2488)

ISSUE_15 — renders an argument list into a human-readable, copy-pasteable command for
the DEBUG log only. Never used to launch a process.

PIPEDEDUP_01 — this was a PRIVATE COPY of `ProcessArgs.FormatForLog`, byte for
byte. Every other consumer in this assembly — AudioLoudnessProbe, MediaProber,
WaveformGenerator, HudAutoDetector — already called the shared one; this file alone kept
its own. The escaping rules it implements are a correctness contract (ISSUE_07: a path
containing a quote, or ending in a backslash, breaks a hand-assembled command line), and a
second copy is a second place for that contract to drift. Delegating keeps the private
name so none of the six call sites in this file change.

### ProcessWorker.RunTwoPassTailAsync (line 2506)

T01 — runs the analysis pass and the real pass against the pre-rendered master.

Audio is NOT re-encoded: it was finalised in the master and is stream-copied through, so it
is encoded exactly once across the whole export and suffers no double loss.

**Returns:** True when `finalPath` was produced successfully.

### ProcessWorker.RunPassAsync (line 2547)

T01 — runs one of the two passes, mapping its `-progress` output onto a progress sub-band.
Deliberately small and self-contained: these invocations have no filter graph, no encoder
fallback chain and no size-retry, so none of RunFfmpegOnce's machinery applies.

### ProcessWorker.CleanupTwoPassArtifacts (line 2668)

T01 — see `TwoPassEncoding.Cleanup`.

### ProcessWorker.RunNvencComplexityProbeAsync — PROBE_01 (line 2672)

PROBE_01 — measures the clip's NVENC encoding complexity with a 5-second
reference-quality sample, replacing blind bitrate guessing for size-locked
exports. See the PROBE_01 block in RunAsync for the calibration data and
the margin model that consumes the returned bits-per-second figure.

The slice is taken from the MIDDLE of the extract range (`-ss mid -t 5`),
decoded in SOFTWARE (no `-hwaccel*` flags — the zero-copy guardrail) and
encoded through h264_nvenc at reference CQ 20 with no rate cap, writing to
the null muxer so nothing ever touches the disk. Speed segments collapse
naturally: the `fps` filter duplicates/drops frames exactly like the export
graph does, so duplicated frames cost ~nothing and the measurement already
reflects the rendered timeline's complexity.

Returns null (non-fatally) when NVENC is unavailable, the output cannot be
parsed, or the export is cancelled — callers keep the budget-derived rate.

### ProcessWorker.Dispose — ISSUE_11 (line 2845)

ISSUE_11 — disposing the worker now also STOPS the encoder.

WHAT WAS WRONG: this released the bookkeeping object and never told FFmpeg anything. Every
caller is *expected* to call `Cancel` first, but nothing enforced it — so any
path that disposed a worker without cancelling (an early return, an exception, a UI teardown
that skipped a step) left a full-speed encode running on a file that would never be
delivered, with the progress overlay already gone. The user saw fans at full tilt and a
pegged CPU with nothing on screen to explain it.

Killing the tree here makes teardown self-sufficient. Calling Cancel() first remains the
correct, orderly path — this is the backstop, not a replacement for it.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/ProgressiveLanes.cs

### LaneClip (line 11)

One clip as a lane sees it.

### LaneTile — LANEINSTANCE_01 (line 16)

One unit of lane work: a clip's slice of the lane.
LANEINSTANCE_01 — two identities, never confused:
`CacheKey` is the SOURCE MEDIA (file identity + kept window): it keys the decoded strip /
peaks, the disk cache and the shared ffmpeg job, so identical occurrences share ONE decode.
`InstanceKey` is the TIMELINE OCCURRENCE: it keys the presenters' tiles, layout and leases,
so the same clip queued twice is drawn twice.

### LaneTile.Occurrence — LANEINSTANCE_01 (line 30)

LANEINSTANCE_01 — the occurrence identity (falls back to the source key for hand-built tiles).

### LanePlanner.MinTilePx (line 36)

Clips narrower than this on the lane get no tile (nothing readable fits).

### LanePlanner.Plan — LANECACHE_02, LANEINSTANCE_01 (line 39)

Tiles for one lane, LEFT→RIGHT. `tileWidthPx` is one filmstrip frame's width on
screen (0 for a waveform lane). `kind` separates cache namespaces.
LANECACHE_02 (P11) — the cache key is the CLIP (file identity + kept window), never the on-screen
width: a filmstrip clip always has the same `ThumbGrid` frames and a waveform the same
peaks, so a resize or a reorder is a cache hit and only re-places what is already there.

LANEINSTANCE_01 — every tile also gets a unique `LaneTile.InstanceKey`: the clip's queue
index (validated unique within this plan — a repeated index is disambiguated by its ordinal, never
merged) joined with its `LaneTile.CacheKey`. Because the source key is part of the
occurrence key, an index that is reused by a DIFFERENT clip after a reorder/insert/remove can never
keep the previous clip's tile.

### ThumbGrid — LANECACHE_02 (line 81)

LANECACHE_02 (P11) — every clip's filmstrip is ONE fixed grid of frames in SOURCE time, generated once
(a frame every `MinStepSec` s, at most `MaxFrames`), whatever the window width.
Drawing picks, for every on-screen slot, the grid frame nearest to that slot's moment.

### ThumbGrid.Slots (line 99)

How many frame slots fit a tile `tilePx` wide at `slotPx` per frame.

### ThumbGrid.Pick (line 103)

The grid frame shown in each slot (slot centres mapped onto the clip, nearest frame).

### ProgressiveLaneRunner (line 115)

Runs lane work in order with bounded parallelism. Only the LATEST run is alive: starting a run,
or `Cancel`, cancels the previous one. Thread-safe to call from the UI thread.

### ProgressiveLaneRunner.Generation (line 125)

Generation of the newest run (a finished item from an older generation must not paint).

### ProgressiveLaneRunner.RunAsync (line 128)

Starts `items` in order, at most `maxParallel` at once. Completes when all finished or the run was superseded. Never throws.

### ProgressiveLaneRunner.RunCoreAsync (line 157)

superseded before every item started

### ProgressiveLaneRunner.RunOneAsync (line 165)

superseded run

### LaneCache (line 190)

Small LRU cache for lane images (not thread-safe: UI thread only).

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/RawVideoFrameReader.cs

### RawVideoFrameReader — LOCALTRACK_01 (line 12)

LOCALTRACK_01 — streams a stretch of a video as fixed-size RGB24 frames at a fixed rate through FFmpeg's stdout.
Frame `i` sits at `i / fps` seconds after `startSec`. Only one frame is held at a time; the FFmpeg
process is killed when the caller stops early or cancels.

### RawVideoFrameReader.ReadAsync (line 36)

Calls `onFrame` for every frame (index, bytes); it returns false to stop early.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/RenderedMixGate.cs

### RenderedMixGate — PREVIEWMIX_02 (line 7)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
PREVIEWMIX_02 — WHICH RENDERED MIX MAY BE HEARD. One owner for the rendered preview mix's state,
so "an older render can never replace newer settings" is a property of one small, tested type
instead of five fields scattered across a window.

The rules (each one closed a real hole in the first version):

- **Invalidate on observation, not on a timer.** `Observe` is called with the
CURRENT signature before any mix audio is played; a changed signature makes `IsActive`
false at once, so the live players take over before a single stale sample is heard.
- **Every render is a numbered ticket.** Only the NEWEST ticket may complete, and only if the
signature it rendered is STILL the current one, re-computed at completion
(`TryComplete`). A render that finishes after an edit, or after a newer render
started, is discarded.
- **A WAV that is being overwritten is no longer a valid mix.** The renders alternate between
two files. Starting a render into the file the current mix lives in invalidates that mix first;
before, undoing back to its settings re-activated a file that held a half-written newer render.
- **A failed render is not retried in a loop.** The failed signature is latched
(`FailedSignature`) and only a NEW edit renders again — the first version retried every
250 ms for as long as the failure lasted.

Not thread-safe by design: every call is made on the UI thread.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### RenderedMixGate.Ticket (line 37)

A render in flight: its number, the signature it renders and the file it writes.

### RenderedMixGate.CurrentSignature (line 40)

The signature of the edit as it is NOW (null = the edit needs no rendered mix).

### RenderedMixGate.RenderedSignature (line 43)

The signature the valid rendered mix was made from, or null.

### RenderedMixGate.RenderedPath (line 46)

The WAV of the valid rendered mix, or null.

### RenderedMixGate.Map (line 49)

How preview time maps into `RenderedPath`.

### RenderedMixGate.FailedSignature (line 52)

The signature whose render last FAILED (latched until the edit changes).

### RenderedMixGate.InFlight (line 55)

The ticket currently allowed to complete, if any.

### RenderedMixGate.IsActive (line 58)

True while the rendered mix matches the current edit and replaces the live players.

### RenderedMixGate.IsPending (line 62)

True when the edit needs a mix that is neither ready, nor rendering, nor known to fail.

### RenderedMixGate.Observe (line 66)

Records the current signature. Returns true when it changed (the mix, if any, is no longer active
from this call on). `nowMs` starts the debounce for `ShouldStart`.

### RenderedMixGate.ShouldStart (line 78)

True when a render of the current signature should start now (debounced).

### RenderedMixGate.Begin (line 87)

Starts a render of the current signature into `targetPath`. Any older ticket is
superseded. If the target is the file the valid mix lives in, that mix is invalidated first.

### RenderedMixGate.TryComplete (line 104)

Accepts a finished render only if it is the newest ticket and its signature still equals
`signatureNow` (re-computed by the caller at completion). Returns true when the
mix became the valid one.

### RenderedMixGate.Fail (line 123)

Records a render that failed (not cancelled): its signature is not retried until the edit changes.

### RenderedMixGate.Reset (line 131)

Drops everything (the clip was unloaded or the window is closing).

### PreviewMixPolicy — PREVIEWMIX_02, TEMPO_01 (line 143)

PREVIEWMIX_02 — when the edit needs the export's rendered audio instead of the live players. The live
players cannot reproduce: the music's sidechain ducking/carving and edge fades, the voice protection's
carve, the safety limiter, the meme levels, the peak tamer measured over the exported range, and the
export's tempo engine (TEMPO_01: atempo / Rubber Band — the live gameplay player time-stretches with
mpv's own scaletempo). Any of them present means a rendered mix.

### PreviewMixPolicy.IsFreeze — FREEZESFX_01 (line 168)

FREEZESFX_01 — a freeze (0x) carries the synthesised slow-motion sound (and dips the music), which
the live player cannot produce, so the preview plays the rendered mix like the export.

### PreviewMixPolicy.ChangesTempo — TEMPO_01, FREEZESFX_01 (line 174)

True when the export runs a tempo filter for this rate (TEMPO_01). A freeze (0x) has no tempo: its
audio is synthesised (FREEZESFX_01).

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/RescuedOutputPath.cs

### RescuedOutputPath — RESCUE_01, OUTPATH_01 (line 8)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
RESCUE_01 — moves a finished render out of a failing pipeline and into the temp root under a
name that is RESERVED, not merely observed to be free.

WHAT WAS WRONG. `ProcessWorker.TryRescueFinishedRender` and
`MergerWorker.TryRescueFinishedRender` were two byte-identical copies of this shape:

```csharp

    while (File.Exists(rescued)) { rescued = $"...-{n}.mp4"; n++; }
    File.Move(corePath, rescued);

```

That is precisely the defect `ProcessWorker.ResolveOutputPath` documents under OUTPATH_01
and was rewritten to remove — "a File.Exists scan: check, then return the name, then write to
it much later. Two pipelines running at once ... both saw the same index free". The rescue
path never received that fix, and the consequence here is STRICTLY WORSE than it was for
ResolveOutputPath:

  1. `ResolveOutputPath` feeds a `File.Move(..., overwrite: true)`. The rescue called
     the TWO-argument `File.Move(string, string)`, which THROWS `IOException`
     when the destination exists. The throw was caught, null was returned, and the finished
     render — the ONLY copy, which is the entire reason it is being rescued — was abandoned on
     a path the failing pipeline was about to clean up. A completed export, lost silently, with
     one log line as the only trace.
  2. The name is stamped to ONE SECOND (`yyyyMMdd-HHmmss`) and the temp root is shared by
     every process. Cancel-then-restart — the scenario OUTPATH_01 was written for — and Main
     App + Merger running together both put two rescues inside the same second.
  3. The `n` loop had NO CEILING, unlike ResolveOutputPath's. On a directory where
     File.Exists keeps returning true (permissions, a full disk, an offline share) it spun
     forever INSIDE A FAILURE HANDLER.

⚠️ ONE COPY, ON PURPOSE. The two workers previously held two copies of this method and two
copies of `ReadExitCodeSafely`; the latter pair silently DIVERGED (one was hardened to
use GracefulProcessTerminator, the other was left calling Kill raw). Anything that both
pipelines do identically belongs here, once, so a fix cannot miss a caller.

⚠️ THE PREFIX AND LOG TAG ARE PARAMETERS, NOT CONSTANTS. "FreeVideoStudio-RECOVERED-" and
"Merged-Videos-RECOVERED-" are user-visible and tell the user which tool produced the file;
"Merged-Videos-RECOVERED-" (`OutputFileNaming`) are user-visible and tell the user which tool produced the file;
"Output" and "Merger" are how crash digests attribute the failure. Both must stay distinct.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### RescuedOutputPath.MaxIndex — RESCUE_01 (line 51)

RESCUE_01 — the same ceiling `ResolveOutputPath` uses, for the same reason: a
directory that cannot be written to must fail loudly after a bounded number of attempts
instead of spinning forever inside the export's failure handling.

### RescuedOutputPath.TryRescue (line 73)

Moves `sourcePath` into `tempDirectory` under a reserved
name and returns that name, or null when there was nothing to rescue or the rescue failed.

TOTAL BY CONTRACT: this is invoked from inside failure handling, so it never throws. An
exception escaping here would turn a partial failure into a total one.

**sourcePath:** The finished render to preserve. Missing file => null.
**tempDirectory:** Destination root. Created if absent — never presumed to exist.
**filenamePrefix:** User-visible prefix, e.g. "FreeVideoStudio-RECOVERED-".
**logTag:** CoreLogger tag, e.g. "Output" or "Merger".
**failureMessage:**
The caller's own wording for the failure line ("Could not preserve the finished render" /
"... the finished merge"). Kept as a parameter so both messages survive verbatim.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/SceneCutDetector.cs

### SceneCutDetector — SCENECUT_01 (line 6)

SCENECUT_01 (docs/01 TL-LOCALTRACK) — finds hard cuts between consecutive frames so a tracker STOPS at a cut instead
of latching onto whatever sits where its target was. Each frame becomes a 64-bin luma histogram (every 4th pixel);
the distance between two frames is half the L1 difference of their normalised histograms (0 = same picture,
1 = nothing in common). A cut is a distance of at least `CutThreshold` that is also
`SpikeFactor`× the median of the recent distances, so fast but continuous camera motion is not a cut,
AND whose brightness distribution moved by at least `MinShift` (mean CDF difference), so a fade or a
flash that only slides the histogram a few levels is not a cut either.

### SceneCutDetector.Push (line 26)

Feeds one RGB24 frame; returns true when it starts a new shot.

### SceneCutDetector.Shift (line 74)

Mean absolute difference of the two cumulative histograms (0 = same brightness spread, 1 = black vs white).

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/TextOverlayGenerator.cs

### TextOverlayGenerator.GeneratePng — TEXTGEN_01 (line 13)

Renders the portrait caption to a transparent PNG.

TEXTGEN_01: the defaults are BOUND to the portrait canvas, not typed as literals. This
PNG is composited by `MobileFilterBuilder` with `overlay=0:0` onto the final
portrait frame, so `width` must equal the canvas width and
`height` must equal the top padding band the caption lives in. Writing
1080 and 150 here duplicated `CoordinateConstants.PortraitW` and
`CoordinateConstants.UIPaddingTop`; changing either constant would then have
silently misaligned the caption instead of failing.

A `fontSize` parameter used to sit in this signature and was never read — the size
is chosen by the auto-fit loop below, which starts at 110 and descends until the text
fits BOTH the given width and height. It has been removed rather than left implying a
control the caller never had.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/TwoPassEncoding.cs

### TwoPassEncoding (line 6)

T01 — shared pieces of the two-pass size-targeting route, used by BOTH `ProcessWorker`
(Main App) and `MergerWorker` (Video Merger). Crop Tools never encodes video and has
no use for any of this.

── WHAT TWO-PASS ACTUALLY DOES ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Pass 1 encodes the whole timeline and writes a per-frame COMPLEXITY MAP. Pass 2 reads that map
and spends the SAME total bit budget where it is actually needed — taking bits from a static
lobby and giving them to a firefight. This is fundamentally different from `-rc-lookahead`,
which only sees ~32 frames (half a second at 60fps) and therefore can never make a trade
across the length of a clip.

Nothing in here touches pixels. No blur, no smoothing, no filter changes — only bit allocation.

── WHERE IT APPLIES, AND WHERE IT MUST NOT ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
(1) A BITRATE BUDGET MUST EXIST. Two-pass redistributes a fixed budget; CRF/CQ exports have no
    budget, so a second pass costs time and changes nothing. Main App: the MB quality slider,
    never ORIGINAL QUALITY. Merger: 100% "Lossless" only, never the CQ range below it.
(2) libx264 ONLY. `h264_nvenc` does not implement libavcodec's stats-file two-pass; its
    equivalent is `-multipass` + `-rc-lookahead`, both already configured in
    `EncoderManager` and explicitly not to be altered. Do not try to wire
    `-pass 1`/`-pass 2` into the NVENC/AMF/QSV paths — FFmpeg will accept the flags and
    silently ignore them, which is worse than not offering the feature.

── THE SCRATCH MASTER AND MANDATE #1 ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
A naive two-pass re-runs the ENTIRE filter graph for both passes, and in this codebase the
graph (14-18 way split, lanczos portrait scale, zoom pads, HUD overlays) dominates wall clock —
that costs ~2x. The FAST route renders the graph ONCE into a near-lossless master and points
both passes at that file: ~1.3-1.4x instead of ~2x.

⚠️ `docs/README.md` (formerly `project_structure.txt`) mandate #1 forbids caches for video pipelines. The master is NOT a
cache. It is created inside the job's own temp directory, consumed by the two passes, and
deleted in the same job — it is never reused across exports and never outlives the job folder.
Do NOT evolve it into anything that survives an export, and do not delete this feature on the
assumption that it violates the mandate.

When the staging drive cannot hold the master (see `DiskSpaceGuard.HasRoomFor`) callers
fall back to the SLOW route — the graph genuinely runs twice — rather than abandoning the
accurate size targeting the user asked for.

### TwoPassEncoding.MasterCrf (line 48)

CRF of the scratch master. Visually lossless and far above anything the quality slider can
request (a 5-100 MB target over a typical clip is ~1-24 Mbit/s; the master is ~30 Mbit/s),
so generation loss across the two encodes is not measurable. Do NOT raise this to "save
space" — the file is deleted at the end of the job and its whole purpose is transparency.

### TwoPassEncoding.MasterCodecArgs (line 56)

Codec flags for the scratch master.

`veryfast` is deliberate: this stage is bounded by the filter graph, not by the encoder, so
spending encoder effort here buys nothing and directly lengthens the export. A short GOP
with `keyint_min=1` keeps the master cheap to seek for the two passes that read it.

### TwoPassEncoding.PassArgs (line 74)

Codec flags for pass 1 (analysis) or pass 2 (real encode).

Both passes MUST use identical encoder settings apart from the pass number — x264 validates
the stats file against them, and a mismatch either aborts pass 2 or silently degrades it.
If you change `-preset`, `-bf`, `-profile:v` or `-level:v` here, they change for both by
construction. Keep it that way.

### TwoPassEncoding.Cleanup (line 99)

Removes the scratch master and x264's stats files.

x264 writes BOTH `<prefix>-0.log` and `<prefix>-0.log.mbtree`. A stale
stats file left behind is the dangerous case: pass 2 of a LATER attempt would happily read a
complexity map built for a different bitrate or a different video and produce a quietly
worse file with no error anywhere. Callers therefore also run this BEFORE each attempt, not
only after.

Everything lives in the job's temp directory and dies with it, so this is belt-and-braces —
but the belt matters.

Byte order of `DecodedVideoFrame.Pixels`.

4 bytes per pixel in memory order B, G, R, A. Alpha is always 255 (video is opaque).

### DecodedVideoFrame — LIBAVFRAME_01 (line 17)

LIBAVFRAME_01 — one decoded video frame as MANAGED data. No libav pointer, no UI type, no

### DecodedVideoFrame.Pixels (line 35)

Top-down rows, `Stride` bytes apart, `PixelFormat` order.

### DecodedVideoFrame.TimestampSeconds (line 42)

The chosen frame's decoded timestamp in source seconds (best_effort_timestamp × time_base), when known.

### VideoFrameRequest — LIBAVFRAME_01 (line 46)

LIBAVFRAME_01 — what to decode. `SeekMicroseconds` is the value ffmpeg's
`-ss` would parse (use `AtFfmpegSeek` with the SAME string the subprocess path
passes, so both backends quantize the timestamp identically). `TargetWidth` = 0 keeps
the display size; otherwise the output is that wide and the height follows the aspect ratio
exactly like ffmpeg's `scale=W:-1` (or `scale=W:-2` when `EvenHeight`).

### VideoFrameRequest.AtFfmpegSeek (line 55)

Builds a request from an ffmpeg `-ss` argument such as "0.5" or "12.345".

ffmpeg's duration syntax, the subset the product passes to `-ss`.

### FfmpegTime.ParseSecondsToMicroseconds (line 63)

libavutil/parseutils.c av_parse_time(duration=1) for "[-]S+[.m*]": integer seconds plus at
most 6 fractional digits, further digits TRUNCATED (not rounded), exactly as libav does.

### VideoFrameBackend (line 102)

Which single-frame backend `VideoFrameGrabber` may use.

### VideoFrameBackend.Auto (line 105)

Native libav first; the caller's ffmpeg subprocess ONCE if native cannot answer. The default.

### VideoFrameBackend.Native (line 107)

Native libav only (tests, benchmarks). No subprocess.

### VideoFrameBackend.Ffmpeg (line 109)

ffmpeg subprocess only — the pre-LIBAVFRAME behaviour, kept as the explicit fallback.

### NativeFrameStatus (line 113)

Why a native single-frame decode produced no frame.

### NativeFrameStatus.Ok (line 116)

A frame was decoded; `NativeFrameResult.Frame` is set.

### NativeFrameStatus.Unavailable (line 118)

The frame tier cannot run in this process (DLLs, version or layout guard). Latched.

### NativeFrameStatus.MediaError (line 120)

libav rejected this input, or it needs something the native path deliberately does not reproduce.

### NativeFrameStatus.NoFrame (line 122)

The input opened and decoded fine but holds no frame at/after the timestamp (or no video
stream) — the SAME answer ffmpeg gives (no output picture), so it is terminal: no fallback.

### NativeFrameResult (line 129)

Outcome of one native decode. Managed data only.

### INativeFrameDecoder — LIBAVFRAME_01 (line 132)

LIBAVFRAME_01 — narrow in-process single-frame seam.

### INativeFrameDecoder.DecodeAsync (line 135)

Decodes the frame ffmpeg `-ss T -i file -frames:v 1` would output. Throws
`OperationCanceledException` on cancellation or when `timeout`
elapses. Never throws for media errors.

### VideoFrameGrabber — LIBAVFRAME_03, LIBAVPROBE_03 (line 143)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
LIBAVFRAME_03 — THE ONE ROUTER FOR "DECODE ONE VIDEO FRAME AT T", AND ITS FALLBACK RULE.

Callers (MemeThumbnailCache, GeminiTrackingService) pass BOTH paths: a converter for the
native `DecodedVideoFrame` and their pre-existing ffmpeg subprocess code, unchanged.
The router decides which one answers; the caller's observable result type never changes.

FALLBACK — explicit, bounded, logged, testable, one direction only (the LIBAVPROBE_03 policy):
  • native Unavailable (DLLs missing, version/layout guard) → subprocess. Latched by the loader,
    logged once by LibAvLibrary. Not a fault: the outcome is the pre-native outcome.
  • native MediaError, or an unexpected managed exception in the native path or in the caller's
    converter → subprocess ONCE with the remaining time budget, reported Faults.Recoverable
    ("LIBAV-FRAME-FALLBACK", log-only).
  • native NoFrame → terminal (null). ffmpeg would also produce no picture; asking it again only
    doubles the cost (MemeThumbnailCache's 0.5 s → 0 retry stays the caller's own rule).
  • cancellation → re-thrown, never a fault, never a fallback.
  • native timeout → OperationCanceledException (as AsyncProcessRunner's timeout always threw);
    no fallback, the budget is spent.
  • the subprocess never falls back to native. There is no loop.
`VideoFrameBackend.Ffmpeg` (or env `FVS_FRAME_DECODE=ffmpeg`) forces the old path;
`VideoFrameBackend.Native` (or `FVS_FRAME_DECODE=native`) forbids it.

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### VideoFrameGrabber.BackendEnvironmentVariable (line 173)

Environment override: `ffmpeg` or `native`; anything else = Auto.

### VideoFrameGrabber.NativeOverride (line 189)

Test seam for the CURRENT async flow only. Null = `LibAvFrameDecoder.Instance`.

### VideoFrameGrabber.FallbackCount (line 197)

Frames answered by the subprocess after native could not (process lifetime). Diagnostics/tests.

### VideoFrameGrabber.NativeCount (line 199)

Frames answered natively (process lifetime). Diagnostics/tests.

### VideoFrameGrabber.DecodeNativeAsync (line 202)

The native decoder alone (parity tests, benchmarks). No fallback, no fault.

### VideoFrameGrabber.GrabAsync (line 206)

One frame at `request`.SeekMicroseconds. `fromNative` converts
a native frame to the caller's result; `subprocess` is the caller's
pre-existing ffmpeg path, invoked with the time budget it may use. Returns null when
neither produced a picture (the callers' existing "no picture" outcome).

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/VideoRenderMode.cs

### VideoRenderMode (line 6)

Singleton holding the result of the GPU capability probe.
Set once at startup by AvaloniaApp; read by the view layer and IPC client
to determine which rendering path to use (GPU texture sharing vs. software mmap).

### VideoRenderMode.Current (line 15)

Gets the global instance. Must be initialized via `Initialize`
before first use.

### VideoRenderMode.UseHardwareAcceleration (line 22)

True if hardware-accelerated video rendering should be used.

### VideoRenderMode.RendererName (line 27)

Human-readable renderer name (for status display).

### VideoRenderMode.FailureReason (line 32)

If hardware acceleration is disabled, the reason why (for diagnostics).

### VideoRenderMode.Initialize (line 44)

Resolves the preview render path and stores the result. Call exactly once at application
startup — `AvaloniaApp.OnFrameworkInitializationCompleted` does this for EVERY process in
the suite, so the Main App, the Video Merger and Crop Tools all select their preview path
through this one method. There is no per-app variant and there must never be one.

ISSUE 2: this used to run the D3D11/DXGI probe fresh in every process. It now reads the
suite-wide answer published by whichever app started first
(`HardwareCapability`) and only probes when there is nothing valid to reuse.

⚠️ THE CACHE IS SESSION-KEYED, AND THAT IS LOAD-BEARING. Windows blocks GPU access inside
an RDP session, so a result recorded in a local session is actively WRONG inside a remote
one. `HardwareCapability` includes the Windows session id and the remote flag
in its key, so crossing that boundary always re-probes. Do not "simplify" that away.

Fail-safe: any cache problem falls through to a real probe, i.e. the original behaviour.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/WavAudioReader.cs

### WavAudioReader — AOTCLEAN_02 (line 7)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
AOTCLEAN_02 — A WAV-ONLY REPLACEMENT FOR NAudio's `AudioFileReader`.

`AudioFileReader` lives in the NAudio umbrella assembly and falls back to
`MediaFoundationReader` for anything that is not WAV/MP3/AIFF. MediaFoundationReader is
built on classic COM interop (`[ComImport]` interfaces marshalled through P/Invoke), which
NativeAOT does not support at all: the ILC reported it as IL2050, and at runtime that path would
throw. Referencing the umbrella package also dragged NAudio.Wasapi, NAudio.Asio, NAudio.Midi and
NAudio.WinForms into a NativeAOT binary that uses none of them.

Every file this suite reads through NAudio is a WAV it wrote itself (voice-over takes from
`VoiceRecorder`, the bundled UI cue sounds). So this reader is exactly what
AudioFileReader does for a WAV (WaveFileReader → SampleChannel → IEEE float, with the same
byte-position conversion), and the solution now references only NAudio.Core and NAudio.WinMM.
A non-WAV path fails loudly here instead of silently taking an interop route AOT cannot run.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### WavAudioReader.Volume (line 63)

Linear gain applied to the samples (1.0 = unchanged).

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/WaveformEnvelope.cs

### WaveformEnvelope — WAVEPIXEL_01, LANECACHE_02 (line 5)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
WAVEPIXEL_01 (04 UI-MERGERQUEUE, 2026-10-11) — A WAVEFORM TILE IS DRAWN AT PIXEL RESOLUTION.

A clip's cached `WaveformPeaks` (40/s, up to `WaveformPeaks.MaxPeaks`) are
width-independent and stay that way (LANECACHE_02 keys never see the width). The lane used to turn
EVERY peak into two geometry points on the UI thread, so a clip 2 px wide with 6000 peaks built
~12 000 segments, and a merge of thousands of clips stalled the dispatcher on every rebuild.

Here the peaks are reduced, OFF the dispatcher, to one level per device pixel column of the tile
(`Columns`): column j holds the MAX of its peak bucket (the upper edge of the min/max
envelope; peaks are absolute, so the lower edge is its mirror −max). Levels are normalized exactly
like the old shape — divided by the clip's loudest peak, floored at `MinTop` — and since
max-of-bucket-max is the global max, the reduced shape keeps the old envelope's extent bit-for-bit.
Geometry is therefore O(visible pixels), never O(peaks).
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### WaveformEnvelope.MinTop (line 24)

The normalization floor: a near-silent clip is not blown up to full height.

### WaveformEnvelope.MaxColumns (line 27)

Hard ceiling on columns per tile, whatever the monitor (a 4K lane is 3840 device px).

### WaveformEnvelope.RebuildRatio (line 30)

Hysteresis: a placed tile is re-reduced only when its columns are off by more than this factor.

### WaveformEnvelope.Top (line 33)

The value every level is divided by: the clip's loudest peak, at least `MinTop`.

### WaveformEnvelope.Columns (line 41)

Columns for a tile `tileWidthDip` wide at `renderScaling`:
one per device pixel, never more than the peaks themselves nor `MaxColumns`; 0 = nothing to draw.

### WaveformEnvelope.Reduce (line 54)

The pixel envelope: `columns` normalized levels (0..1). Column j covers peaks
[j·n/c, (j+1)·n/c) — every peak in exactly one column, none skipped. Pure; safe on any thread.

### WaveformEnvelope.PointCount (line 76)

Geometry points one tile of `columns` costs: begin + upper edge + corner + lower edge.

### WaveformEnvelope.NeedsRebuild (line 79)

Is a tile drawn with `have` columns too coarse or too dense for `want`?

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/WaveformPeaks.cs

### WaveformPeaks — LANECACHE_02 (line 12)

══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
LANECACHE_02 (Video-Merger-Migration.md P11) — A CLIP'S WAVEFORM AS NUMBERS, NOT AS A PICTURE.

The Merger's waveform lane used to be a PNG rendered by ffmpeg at the lane's pixel width, so every
resize produced a new ffmpeg run per clip. Here a clip's sound is read ONCE as tiny mono PCM
(`SampleRate` Hz — plenty for an envelope) and reduced to peaks at a FIXED rate
(`PeaksPerSecond`, at most `MaxPeaks`). The window draws those peaks as a
vector shape stretched to whatever width the clip has, so resizing and reordering cost nothing.
Peaks are 0..1 (absolute sample peak per bucket). Serializable for the on-disk cache.
══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

### WaveformPeaks.PeakCount (line 30)

Number of peaks for a window of `durationSec`.

### WaveformPeaks.Reduce (line 34)

Reduces signed 16-bit mono samples to `count` peaks (0..1).

### WaveformPeaks.ReduceStreamAsync — PCMSTREAM_01 (line 50)

PCMSTREAM_01 — `Reduce` over a PCM16LE stream in bounded memory. Pass 1 streams the
samples through a 64 KiB buffer into `PcmScratch` (memory-capped, spills to a temp
file) while counting them; pass 2 replays them with the FINAL count, so the bucket mapping —
including a stream shorter than requested — is identical to the one-shot reduce.
Returns null when the stream holds no whole sample.

### WaveformPeaks.BuildStartInfo (line 68)

Builds the ffmpeg invocation that decodes the window to `SampleRate` Hz mono s16le.

### WaveformPeaks.ExtractAsync — PCMSTREAM_01 (line 92)

Reads the window's audio once and returns its peaks; null when there is no audio or ffmpeg fails.
PCMSTREAM_01/02 — stdout is reduced while it streams (bounded memory), stderr is drained
concurrently, and cancellation kills and reaps the child before the exception surfaces.

### WaveformPeaks.ToBytes (line 119)

Little-endian float32 array, for the on-disk cache.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Media/ZoomPreviewSimulator.cs

### ZoomPreviewSimulator — PREVIEWFIDELITY_01 (line 5)

LIVE ZOOM PREVIEW — ONE IMPLEMENTATION, SHARED BY EVERY PREVIEW SURFACE IN THE SUITE.

Turns "where is the playhead" into the mpv `video-crop` string that makes the on-screen
preview show exactly what the exported file will show, including the SLOW glide.

── WHY THIS CLASS EXISTS ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
The Granular editor grew its own copy of the export's ramp maths. Adding the same preview to
the Main App and to Music Wizard phase 3 would have meant a THIRD and FOURTH copy of:
  the steal-window rules · the two-Slow-zooms gap rule · the progress ramp · the centre lerp ·
  the even-pixel rounding · the edge clamp
Four copies of one algorithm is four chances for the preview to stop matching the export, and
a preview that lies is worse than no preview. There is now exactly ONE copy, here, and it
reads its timing constants straight from `GranularSpeedBuilder` — the export engine
itself. Preview and export cannot drift apart without a compile error.

⚠️ IF YOU CHANGE THE RAMP RULES, CHANGE THEM IN `GranularSpeedBuilder` AND THIS
FILE FOLLOWS AUTOMATICALLY. Never hard-code 0.5 / 1.0 in a window.

── GPU ONLY. THIS IS DELIBERATE. ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Callers must gate on `VideoRenderMode.Current.UseHardwareAcceleration`. On the GPU path
mpv applies the crop inside its own renderer — a texture-coordinate change, effectively free.
On the CPU fallback path (hwdec=no, software scale into a WriteableBitmap) every crop CHANGE
forces mpv to rebuild its software scaler, and a 0.5s glide produces ~10 of them; on machines
that by definition have no GPU that is a visible hitch. CPU-only machines therefore keep the
static guide rectangle and nothing else — an owner decision, not an oversight.

── KNOWN, ACCEPTED DIFFERENCE FROM THE EXPORT ───────────────────────────────────────────────
Near the frame edges the export PADS with black where this preview CLAMPS the crop inside the
picture. So a zoom box pushed hard against an edge previews very slightly differently from the
rendered file. Documented in docs/README.md (formerly project_structure.txt); do not "fix" it by letting the crop leave
the frame — mpv rejects an out-of-bounds crop and the preview would simply stop updating.
PREVIEWFIDELITY_01 — the difference is no longer silent: `Result.EdgeClamped` and
`AnyEdgePadding` report a material clamp so the window can say the preview is not the export.

### ZoomPreviewSimulator.Result — PREVIEWFIDELITY_01 (line 42)

Result of a simulation tick. `Crop` is empty when no crop applies.
**EdgeClamped:**
PREVIEWFIDELITY_01 — true when the export's window runs past the frame edge here (the export
PADS with black, this preview had to CLAMP), by more than `MaterialEdgeFraction` of the
window. The preview is then not what the file will show, and the caller says so.

### ZoomPreviewSimulator.MaterialEdgeFraction — PREVIEWFIDELITY_01 (line 54)

PREVIEWFIDELITY_01 — a clamp that moves the window by more than this fraction of its own size is
a visible difference (a black band in the export); anything smaller is sub-pixel noise.

### ZoomPreviewSimulator.AnyEdgePadding — PREVIEWFIDELITY_01 (line 60)

PREVIEWFIDELITY_01 — true when ANY box zoom in `segments` would, at some point of
its glide or hold, show black padding in the export that this clamping preview cannot show.
Checked at the held zoom and at quarter points of a slow glide. AI-tracked zooms clamp in the
export too (their crop expression is `min/max`-bounded), so they never pad.

### ZoomPreviewSimulator.Compute — PORTRAIT_01 (line 81)

Computes the mpv `video-crop` value for a moment in the clip.

**segments:**
The speed segments carrying the zoom boxes. Segments without a complete zoom are ignored.

**tSec:**
Playhead position in seconds, RELATIVE TO THE TRIM START — the same origin the export uses.
Callers holding an absolute player time must subtract their trim start first.

**durSec:** Trimmed clip length in seconds. Used as the "clip end" boundary.
**Returns:** `Result.None` when the picture should be uncropped.
**portraitMode:**
PORTRAIT_01 — true when the export will run the Portrait Canvas Trick.

⚠️ THIS IS NOT OPTIONAL POLISH; WITHOUT IT THE PREVIEW LIES. In portrait the export does
THREE things: (1) crop a window around the zoom box, WIDENED to the source aspect ratio,
(2) scale that back to full frame, (3) centre-crop to the 2:3 slice, keeping only the
middle 1280 of the 3414-wide internal space (= 720 of 1920 source px). Steps 1 and 3
cancel, so the net result is exactly the box the user drew.

The preview used to perform step 1 ONLY. Measured on a 1920x1080 source with a 480x720 box:
the export showed a 480px-wide region, the preview showed 1280px — 2.67x too wide. The
centres matched at every position, so nothing drifted sideways; the FRAMING was simply
wrong, which reads to the eye as "not the same".

It also means a portrait preview must be cropped to the 2:3 slice even when NO zoom is
active, because that is what the export always produces.

**srcW:** Source width. Required for the no-zoom portrait slice, where there is no zoom box to read it from.
**srcH:** Source height.

### ZoomPreviewSimulator.BoxWindow (line 229)

The box zoom's visible window at progress `p`: the export's centre lerp and zoom
ramp (`GranularSpeedBuilder.BuildConstantZoomFilter`), then — because mpv rejects an
out-of-bounds crop — clamped into the picture. `EdgeClamped` reports a material clamp.

### ZoomPreviewSimulator.PortraitSurvivingWidth — PORTRAIT_01 (line 268)

PORTRAIT_01 — how much of the SOURCE width survives the portrait centre-crop.

Derived from `CoordinateConstants` rather than hard-coded, so it tracks the Portrait Canvas
Trick automatically: the frame is scaled up until it covers the 1280x1920 internal space,
then 1280px is cropped from the middle. Undoing that scale gives the surviving source width
— 720px on a 1920x1080 source, which is the number mandate #3 quotes.

### ZoomPreviewSimulator.PortraitSliceOnly — PORTRAIT_01 (line 282)

PORTRAIT_01 — the crop for "portrait, but no zoom happening right now".
Exactly the 2:3 slice the export always delivers, full height.

### ZoomPreviewSimulator.GetCachedTrajectory (line 303)

Parses a trajectory JSON string at most once per unique payload across scrubbing ticks.

## src/FreeVideoStudio.Core/Project/MemePresentationJson.cs

MEMEMODE_01 — the ONE JSON mapping of a meme's presentation (mode, corner, size, sound), used by
the project file, the crash-recovery document (both through `ProjectSerializer`) and
the editors' own session JSON. Hand-written, no reflection (PROJ_02).

**Backward compatibility.** Every key is optional. A meme written before MEMEMODE_01 has
none of them and reads back as a full-screen cutaway with sound — exactly what it always was. An
unknown or malformed value falls back to the same defaults rather than failing the load
(06 PROJ-SCHEMA: the reader is forgiving).
